## 21. THE JUNCTION THEOREM — the exact spoke calculus of a decorated sheet, the spoke rule, the vertex-degree Cauchy–Schwarz, the graded rates, and the number they give for the singly-attached sub-gas (18:17 EDT, read from `date`; written after the reboot of 16:46)

**Setting.** S a closed branch-free orientable surface at spin ½ (the rest); Y a piece — a set of plaquettes off S, link-connected through its off-S links, every off-S link of multiplicity ≥ 2 in Y — at spin 1 on every plaquette. T := L(Y) ∩ L(S) the rest links, m_T(ℓ) ≥ 1 the number of Y-plaquettes on ℓ ∈ T. N(Y) := W(S ∪ Y)/((3a₁)^{|Y|}W(S)) — the plaquette weight is d₁a₁ = 3a₁ with χ₁ the unnormalised character (§2, line "f = Σ d_j a_j χ_j"; §11 "with the weight 3a₁χ₁ on w"), so N(Y) is the ratio of the two Haar integrals, §24 (ii); stated here 2026-10-31 23:3x after iii-ac's referee read took the weight as a₁ and read J2 a factor 3^{−|Y|} off.

**J1 (the σ-link identity).** For U ∈ SU(2), with D¹(U)_{AB} := ½ tr(σ^A U σ^B U⁻¹) the real adjoint matrix,

  U_{ab}(U⁻¹)_{cd} = ½ δ_{ad}δ_{bc} + ½ Σ_{A,B} D¹(U)_{AB} σ^A_{ad} σ^B_{cb}.

(Contract with X_{bc}: the left is (UXU⁻¹)_{ad}; the right is (tr X/2)δ_{ad} + Σ_A (Σ_B D¹_{AB} tr(σ^BX)/2) σ^A_{ad}, which is the decomposition of X into its trace and Pauli parts carried by the adjoint action.) S orientable ⇒ its plaquettes can be oriented so that every link of S is traversed once forward and once backward by its two S-plaquettes, so every S-link carries exactly the pair U ⊗ U⁻¹ of J1. Integrating a link with no Y-plaquette gives the singlet ½δ_{ad}δ_{bc}; the index contractions of S's plaquette traces then close into one index loop per VERTEX of S (the loop runs around the vertex cycle), so W(S) = (2u)^{|S|}·2^{−E_S}·2^{V_S} = u^{|S|}2^{χ(S)} — S2 re-derived. Integrating a link carrying one Y-plaquette with D¹(U)_{CD} (its first index at the link's tail, its second at the head, whichever way the plaquette traverses it) gives (1/6)σ^C_{ad}σ^D_{cb}: relative to the bare ½δδ, a factor 1/3, with σ^C inserted on the vertex cycle at the tail and σ^D on the vertex cycle at the head.

**J2 (the exact calculus; m_T ≡ 1).** With τ_v := tr(Π σ^{A})/2 the trace of the Pauli matrices inserted on v's vertex cycle in the cycle's order (τ_v = 1 at a vertex no rest link of Y touches), and H_Y(C⃗, D⃗) := ∫_{off-S links} Π_{q∈Y} tr(Π_{ℓ∈q} M_ℓ) with M_ℓ = the 3×3 matrix unit E_{C_ℓD_ℓ} (or its transpose, by the traversal) at ℓ ∈ T and M_ℓ = D¹(U_ℓ)^{±1} off S,

  N(Y) = 3^{−|T|} Σ_{(C_ℓ, D_ℓ)_{ℓ∈T}} Π_{v∈V(S)} τ_v · H_Y(C⃗, D⃗).

Known answers, all exact: a lone wall (four rest links, H = Π δ around the square, every vertex a corner σ^Xσ^X = 1) gives 3^{−4}·3^4 = 1 — S7; a disc of n plaquettes attached along its boundary gives 3^{1−n} — S9; a single spine of m walls gives 3^{−m}⟨T_u, T_v⟩ = J_m with T_u(A⃗) = tr(Π σ^{A_i})/2 in u's spoke order — S13 (the two far vertices of each wall are corners, the m spoke indices meet P^{(m)} at the spine, the identity between invariant tensors). The three laws of §§13, 17 are three special cases of one formula.

**J3 (the spoke rule).** τ_v ≠ 0 iff the Pauli word at v is ∝ 1 iff the F₂²-sum of the indices at v vanishes (X, Y, Z ↦ the nonzero vectors of F₂²); |τ_v| ∈ {0, 1}. A corner — two consecutive rest links of one plaquette at v — carries one index twice and contributes 0 to the sum. Hence: **a vertex of S touched by exactly one spoke of Y kills the piece**, N(Y) = 0 (Gauss's law of the sheet at a vertex); and the number of index words at a vertex with c_v corners and r_v free spokes is 3^{c_v}·E(r_v), E(r) := (3^r + 3(−1)^r)/4 = 1, 0, 3, 6, 21, 60, 183, 546, 1641 at r = 0..8.

**J4 (the vertex-degree Cauchy–Schwarz).** With r_v the number of free spokes at v (rest links of Y at v not paired by a corner of their own plaquette), e(r) := E(r)/3^{r/2} (e = 1, 0, 1, 1.1547, 2.3333, 3.8490, 6.7778, 11.675, 20.259 at r = 0..8), and D_Y the double of Y glued along T:

  |N(Y)| ≤ Π_{v} √e(r_v) · √W(D_Y) / (3a₁)^{|Y|}.

*Proof.* Cauchy–Schwarz on the index sum of J2 with 3^{−|T|} = 3^{−|T|/2}·3^{−|T|/2}: ‖3^{−|T|/2}Π_vτ_v‖₂² = 3^{−|T|}Π_v 3^{c_v}E(r_v) = Π_v E(r_v)3^{−r_v/2} because the index slots at different vertices are disjoint variables and Σ_v(2c_v + r_v) = 2|T|; ‖3^{−|T|/2}H_Y‖₂² = W(D_Y)/(3a₁)^{2|Y|} by Peter–Weyl (h_Y(U_T) = (3a₁)^{|Y|}Σ H_Y Π_ℓ D¹(U_ℓ)_{C_ℓD_ℓ}, the matrix elements orthogonal with norm² 1/3). ∎ It is EXACT on lone walls (1), on discs (3^{1−n}: the boundary vertices have r = 2 or a corner, e = 1; D is a sphere of 2n plaquettes, 3^{2−2n}), and on the tripod (e(3) at u and w, √(3^{−3}R₃): 2/9); on the four-spine it reads e(4)·3^{−2}√3 = 0.449 against the exact 7/27. It carries NO C(T): only vertices of S with three or more free spokes cost anything, and S10′'s cut constant is gone. Non-orientable S: J1 holds with ε-conjugated Paulis at the orientation-reversing links (A1), the traces change by signs only, J3 and J4 are unchanged.

**J5 (the double).** Cutting D_Y along its links of multiplicity ≥ 3 and capping, W(D_Y)/(3a₁)^{2|Y|} ≤ 3^{Σ_P(χ(P)+n_b(P)−n(P))} ≤ 3^{2#pieces − 2|Y|} ≤ 3^{2k}, k := the number of plaquettes of Y with no rest link and every off-S link of multiplicity ≥ 3 (each is isolated in both copies; every other piece has ≥ 2 plaquettes), so √W(D_Y)/(3a₁)^{|Y|} ≤ 3^{k}. The rank form — the interiors 3^{χ(P)−n(P)} and the boundary characters contracted through the branch links' projectors, |tr(⊗P_ℓ·Π)| ≤ Π rank — gives √(Π_P 3^{χ(P)−n(P)}·Π_{ℓ} R_{m_ℓ}) instead, R_m = dim Inv(1^{⊗m}) = 1, 1, 3, 6, 15 at m = 2..6; on a spine 3^{−m/2}√R_m, on a foam of multiplicity-4 links 3^{#links/2} ≈ 3^{|Y|/2}·… — both are the sup bound's 3 per plaquette on the three-dimensional foam, where nothing better is proved.

**J6 (the universal rates, m_T ≡ 1).** e(r)^{1/(2r)} increases in r (1, 1.0243, 1.1117, 1.1443, 1.1729, 1.1918, 1.2068 at r = 2..8) and r_v ≤ 8 on ℤ⁴, so Π_v√e(r_v) ≤ e(8)^{|T|/8}; k ≤ |Y| − |T|/3 (a plaquette of a piece has at most three rest links); the product is at most max_{t∈[0,3]}(e(8)^{t/8}·3^{1−t/3})^{|Y|} = 3.0903^{|Y|} at t = 3. **For every piece with singly-used rest links, |N(Y)| ≤ 3.0903^{|Y|}, i.e. γ_sup = 3·e(8)^{3/8} = 9.271** — the foam's 9 plus the degree factor, for every size. Graded by size: r_v ≤ min(8, |Y|) and k = 0 below nine plaquettes (a fully branched plaquette needs eight witnesses, two per link, no two sharing more than one link), so with D = min(8, s)

  γ(s) ≤ 3·e(D)^{3/D}: γ(1) = 3 (S7), γ(2) = √3 (every 2-piece is a disc, S9), γ(3) = 3.4641, γ(4) = 5.6577, γ(5) = 6.7349, γ(6) = 7.8103, γ(7) = 8.6101, γ(s ≥ 8) = 9.2709,

each an algebraic number (3·(E(D)/3^{D/2})^{3/D}); the census reads 1.82 at s = 3 and 2.14 at s = 4 — the degree accounting (every vertex at the maximal spoke count) is what is loose.

**[2026-10-31 23:3x EDT — J6 WITHDRAWN AS A PROVED RATE, on iii-ac's referee read.]** The intermediate inequality k ≤ |Y| − |T|/3 ("a plaquette of a piece has at most three rest links") is FALSE on J2's own lone wall: a piece under the setting (no off-S link, the multiplicity condition vacuous) with four rest links, k = 0, |Y| = 1, |T| = 4 gives 0 ≤ −1/3. The conclusion 3.0903^{|Y|} survives on that piece only because all its link ends are corners (r_v = 0). As displayed, J6's chain does not prove its conclusion, and the graded rates γ(s) = 3·e(D)^{3/D} above rest on the same step: both stand WITHDRAWN as proved rates — the repair iii-ac names is to count plaquettes by their rest-link number j = 0..4 (the free-spoke bound Σ_v r_v ≤ 6|Y| still holds, a four-rest-link plaquette contributing no free end) with the j = 4 plaquettes set apart in the k-bound, or to restrict a piece's plaquettes to j ≤ 3 by definition, in which case the lone wall leaves the piece class and S7 leaves J2's known-answer list; the repair by rest-link number and corners is J6′ below (23:5x), second reader owed. J8's "every input proved" and this section's Status line inherit the withdrawal: the sub-gas number 0.4709 is a reading of the displayed formula until J6 is repaired. The certificate's GRADED mode (graded=S21) remains a computation of the displayed formula, never a theorem; the run of record for the number is the TABLE mode on §24's Theorem L (|N(Y)| ≤ Π_v r_v, γ(s) = 3θ(s)⁴ from the exact star table), which does not use J6 and which iii-73 signed the same night (§25). Two queries of the same read, open: (q1) J7's √(1 + 0.75) per doubly-used link assumes r ≥ 3 at both ends (at r = 2, e(1)/e(2) = 0; at r = 1 the spoke channel dies), so "typically 1.155" needs the vertex degrees stated; (q2) §22's "if ρ(D) ≤ 1 for every closed D" has no reading above one (the 4-cube 0.30, the box's double 0.52, the two-block foam 3^{−10/36}) and stays a lemma.

**J6′ (THE REPAIR, 2026-10-31 23:5x EDT — the universal and graded rates for singly-used rest links, by rest-link number and corners; second reader owed on J5 and on this).** For a plaquette q of Y let j_q ∈ {0, 1, 2, 3, 4} be its number of rest links and c_q its number of corners (vertices of q where both of q's links are rest links; c_q = 0, 0, 0 or 1, 2, 4 at j_q = 0, 1, 2 (opposite or adjacent), 3, 4). Since every rest link is singly used, each free spoke at a vertex is one end of one rest link of one plaquette that is not paired by that plaquette's corner there, so

  Σ_v r_v = Σ_{q∈Y} (2j_q − 2c_q),  with  j_q − c_q ≤ 2 for every q (the maximum at two opposite rest links; the lone wall has j = 4, c = 4 and contributes nothing).

With D := min(8, |Y|) (r_v ≤ min(8, |Y|): a vertex has eight links and each plaquette of Y contributes at most one free spoke at a vertex) and e(r)^{1/(2r)} increasing in r (J6's list), √e(r_v) ≤ e(D)^{r_v/(2D)}, so Π_v √e(r_v) ≤ e(D)^{Σ_q (j_q − c_q)/D}. J4 and J5 then give

  |N(Y)| ≤ 3^{k} · Π_{q∈Y} e(D)^{(j_q − c_q)/D} ≤ Π_{q ∈ K} 3 · Π_{q ∉ K} e(D)^{2/D} ≤ max(3, e(D)^{2/D})^{|Y|},

K the k-set of J5 (plaquettes with no rest link and every off-S link of multiplicity ≥ 3, each with j_q − c_q = 0; k = 0 below nine plaquettes, since such a plaquette's four links each carry two more plaquettes of Y). Hence, with 3·e(D)^{2/D} = E(D)^{2/D}:

  γ(1) = 3 (S7), γ(2) = √3 (S9, exact), γ(s) ≤ E(min(8, s))^{2/min(8,s)} for 3 ≤ s ≤ 8: 6^{2/3} = 3.3019, 21^{1/2} = 4.5826, 60^{2/5} = 5.1435, 183^{1/3} = 5.6764, 546^{2/7} = 6.0541, 1641^{1/4} = 6.3647; and γ(s ≥ 9) ≤ 3·max(3, e(8)^{1/4}) = 9, i.e. |N(Y)| ≤ 3^{|Y|} for every piece with singly-used rest links.

Each value is an algebraic number; the lone wall (1), the discs (3^{1−n}) and the m-spines (E(m)/3^m ≤ E(D)^{2m/D}/3^m) sit under it; the census's 1.82 at s = 3 and 2.14 at s = 4 sit under 3.30 and 4.58. J6's displayed 3.0903 and 9.2709 came from trading |T| against k through the false step; the direct count is tighter at every s ≥ 3 because a plaquette cannot both carry three rest links and belong to K.

**J6′ READ (iii-ac, 23:5x EDT, by message, nothing on disk): SIGNED step by step** — (1) Σ_v r_v = Σ_q(2j_q − 2c_q) and the table of j − c (0, 1, 1, 2, 1, 0); (2) r_v ≤ min(8, |Y|); (3) √e(r) ≤ e(D)^{r/(2D)}; (4) the product with J4; (5) k = 0 below nine plaquettes; (6) the six values recomputed and met — conditional on J5's 3^k for the s ≥ 9 tail. **J5 READ by the same hand: its second and third inequalities SIGNED** (χ + n_b ≤ 2 for a connected surface with n_b boundary components, the pieces partitioning the 2|Y| plaquettes; 2·#pieces − 2|Y| ≤ 2k because every plaquette with a rest link is glued to its copy, so the one-plaquette pieces are the 2k isolated ones), **its first inequality — the bound of each cut piece by its capped closed surface's 3^{χ−n} — REFUSED AS DISPLAYED**: the line "cutting D_Y along its links of multiplicity ≥ 3 and capping" asserts it without a derivation, a projector of operator norm one bounding a pairing across one cut (J10's form) and not a product over pieces each closed by caps. **THE FIX (00:0x): J5 IS NO LONGER IN THE CHAIN.** The sub-gas rate's s ≥ 9 tail is Theorem L's through the table — γ(s) = 3θ(s)⁴ = 3·max_{k≤s}(r2max(k)²)^{1/k} with the exact table through k = 8 and the certified bounds beyond (§24, §25), a bound on every piece and so on the sub-gas — and for s ≤ 8, where k = 0 and J5 was never used, γ(s) = min(E(D)^{2/D}, 3θ(s)⁴). The certificate's GRADED mode is rewritten to exactly this (kp_cert_table_wip.awk at 00:0x, the tree's `aperanton_kp_cert.awk` at run an's END; it refuses to run without the table), and it passes wherever the table mode passes, so the sub-gas number is at least the full gas's and rests on J1, J3, J4, J6′ (iii-ac) and Theorem L (iii-73). If anyone wants J5's cap bound back, iii-ac named the test: the box's double, W(D_box)/(3a₁)²⁴ = 1.578·10⁻⁷ (§22), cut along its twelve spokes, 3^{Σ(χ+n_b−n)} against it. Run an's arms still pin J6's withdrawn formula; they are re-pinned from the rewritten mode's run of record after its END.

**J7 (multiply-used rest links — the two channels).** At a rest link carrying m Y-plaquettes, Inv(½⊗½⊗1^{⊗m}) = Inv(1^{⊗m}) ⊕ Hom(1, 1^{⊗m}), and the link integral is the sum of a LIFT channel — the singlet of the sheet's pair, weight 1 relative to the bare link, the m plaquettes merged by P^{(m)}, no insertion on S: the link lifted off the sheet — and R_{m+1} SPOKE channels of weight 1/3 each, σ^A at the tail and σ^B at the head, the m plaquettes' indices fused into (A, B) through an equivariant co-isometry F^{(i)}: 1^{⊗m} → V₁ (for m = 2 the one channel F = ε/√2: ∫D¹_{AB}D¹_{CD}D¹_{C′D′} = ε_{ACC′}ε_{BDD′}/6). So a multiply-used rest link is EITHER absent from the sheet's vertex cycles OR one composite spoke. The Y-functions of the channels are the isotypic components of h_Y in U_ℓ (spins 0 and 1; the spin-2 part of 1⊗1 never couples to the sheet), orthogonal, so Σ_c‖h_c‖² ≤ W(D_Y)/(3a₁)^{2|Y|}, and the Cauchy–Schwarz over the channel sum gives

  |N(Y)| ≤ √( Σ_{c ∈ {lift, spoke}^{T₂}} Π_v e(r_v^{(c)}) ) · √W(D_Y) / (3a₁)^{|Y|},

r_v^{(c)} counting a composite spoke once at each end in its spoke channel and not at all in its lift channel. Since e(r−1)/e(r) ≤ 0.866 (r = 3) and ≈ 1/√3 beyond, a doubly-used link costs at most √(1 + 0.75) = 1.32 against the singly-used bound, typically 1.155. The cube on one link (six faces of a unit cube sharing one link with S): the lift channel is the cube's own 3^{2−6}, the spoke channel dies by J3 (one spoke at each end) — N = 1/81 exactly, as the composite rule says. The two-lone-walls-on-one-link reading (1/3 from the lift, ∓2/9 from the spoke: 5/9 or 1/9) is iii-ac's known-answer test of this section.

**J8 (what the rates give, and what the number needs).** The size-graded fold-in (`records/surface-gas/kp_reach6.py`, z_br(s) = (γ(s)a₁e^{2α})^s with no prefactor, the page dressing, μ = 5): **the sub-gas of pieces with singly-used rest links reads β* = 0.4709 at F₀ = 20 and 0.4759 at F₀ = 22**, every input proved (S2–S4′, S6–S9, S11, S12 on the census, S13, J1–J6, Lemma P and the counts), against 0.3589 at the uniform γ = 9.271 — the graded table is worth 0.11 of β. The number is governed by the rate on pieces of five or more plaquettes: γ(s ≥ 5) = 15 reads 0.3747, 25 reads 0.3247, 35 reads 0.2490 (below Theorem 8.7's 0.2452). The full gas needs the multiply-used pieces: J7's per-link 1.32 is affordable, the double's cut at the doubly-used links (3 per plaquette isolated in both copies, J5) and the degree factor at vertices of eight spokes are not — the crude universal rate for a plaquette with three doubly-used rest links at eight-spoke vertices and one off-S link of multiplicity six is 3·13.7 ≈ 41, and at 41 the KP sum diverges at β = 0.45 (the tail needs 5·γ·a₁e^{κ} < 1, γ < 21 there). **So the proved number for the FULL gas is not yet a number**: the junction theorem's constants for dense pieces are the object, in this order — (i) iii-ac's exact box reading and the two-walls-on-one-link reading as the two known answers of J2/J7; (ii) the v2 bound computed exhaustively over every census piece to F = 20 (a mechanical read of r_v, m_T, the ranks), which turns γ(s ≤ 20) into exact maxima; (iii) a tail bound below 21 for s > 20 — the degree factor is the loose term (the four-spine's 0.449 against 7/27, the box's 12.7 against a value that will be far below one) and the vertex tensors' signs are where the missing cancellation lives: iii-bd's inner-product form across a spine link is the model of the sharper cut. The conjecture the readings suggest, |N(Y)| ≤ 1 for every piece (γ = 3, β* ≈ 0.49), is stated as a conjecture and nothing more.

**Status.** Nothing above β = 0.2452 is claimed. The Part F criterion is not met: the sub-gas number 0.4709 is proved for the sub-gas; the full gas waits on J8's three items.

**§21, added 18:5x EDT (law 18 on J7's worked example; two known answers read).** (a) TWO LONE WALLS ON ONE LINK OF S read 1 in the census (iii-ac: profile (21/6/1/0) at F = 16, 896 polymers, r = 1; the L-tromino's two internal walls; the hand road through χ₁ = χ_½² − 1 gives T₁ = 2^{4−|S|}, T₂ = T₃ = 2^{3−|S|}, ρ = 4 on every sphere — the adjoint exchange between the two walls is exactly zero after the sheet's own integration). J7's bookkeeping was RIGHT and my arithmetic was wrong: redoing the spoke channel with the walls' indices placed correctly — w₁'s index at ℓ's v-end is its link-2 index C₂, at the w-end its link-1 index C₁, the corner δ's — the channel sums Σ_{A,C₂,C₂′} ε_{AC₂C₂′}·iε_{AC₂′C₂} = −6i at v and ±6i at w, and the spoke term is (1/6)·3^{−6}·81·36 = +2/3, not ∓2/9: N = 1/3 (lift) + 2/3 (spoke) = 1, the product of the two lone values, as the census and the hand road say. The known-answer test of the two-channel calculus PASSES after the correction of its author.

(b) J9 (THE PAIRING EXPANSION — a third road to J₄). Since tr(σ^{a₁}⋯σ^{a_{2k}})/2 = Σ_{pairings} (−1)^{crossings} Π δ (the sl₂ weight system, T-774), every vertex tensor is a signed sum of pairings of its free spokes, and with the corners pulled out

  N(Y) = 3^{−|T|}·3^{#corners}·Σ_{π = (π_v)_v} (−1)^{cr(π)} I(π),  I(π) = ∫_{off-S} Σ_{pair indices} Π_{q∈Y} (M_q)_{·,·},

M_q the product of q's off-S matrices between its two rest-link slots, each pair of slots identified by its δ. On the four-spine: nine pairings at (u, w); the three with the same pairing at both ends give ∫(tr DᵀD)² = 9 each, the six with different pairings give Σ_{AA′}(DᵀD)²_{AA′} = 3 each with signs (+,+,+ ; −,−,−,−,+,+), so Σ = 27 − 6 = 21 and N = 3^{−12}·3^{8}·21 = 21/81 = 7/27 — iii-bd's reading, by hand, sharing no line with the engine or with S13's inner product. The pairing form is the exact evaluator's form; as a bound it gives Π_v (r_v − 1)!!·3^{|Y_∘|}, weaker than J4 wherever r_v ≥ 4.

(c) THE BOX. iii-ac's engine reads the 36-plaquette set (the 2×2×2 surface and its twelve interior plaquettes) at ρ/4 = 10657165/3^{19} = 0.0092 summed over its thirty-five spin assignments with twelve plaquettes at spin 1; the per-assignment column (the surface at ½, the interior at 1) is the number J2 computes, and it is at most that. J4's bound for it is 12.7 (six face centres at r = 4, twelve corners at the edge midpoints, the double's rank bound 1); the loose term is the DOUBLE: gauge-fixing the copy's spokes, W(D_box)/(3a₁)^{24} = 3^{−12}·∫_{SU(2)^6} Π_{(i,j) ∈ edges of the octahedron} χ₁(U_iU_j) — the spin-1 octahedron integral the Yang–Mills seat is computing (F₁(1)) — whose sup bound 3^{12} is what the rank form used; at F₁(1) = 3^{4} the bound would read 0.157. So the double's exact value, not the degree factor alone, is where J4 loses on dense pieces, and the double is COMPUTABLE: the junction bound with the exact double is the sharper instrument for every census piece.

(d) THE FLOATS CORRECTED (the Yang–Mills seat's brackets from the closed forms on the 2^{−128} grid, 18:5x). Three of J6's printed floats were typing slips against their own closed forms: γ(4) = 3·(7/3)^{3/4} = 5.663746 (printed 5.6577), γ(7) = 3·(546/3^{7/2})^{3/7} = 8.600465 (printed 8.6101), γ(s ≥ 8) = 3·(1641/81)^{3/8} = 9.270541 (printed 9.2709; the script's default carried 9.2673). γ(2) = 1.732051, γ(3) = 3.464102, γ(5) = 6.734884, γ(6) = 7.810250 stand. The closed forms are the statement; the certificate carries them as roots of exact rationals and never types a float. Record b of `kp_reach6.py` re-reads the sub-gas at the corrected table: see the record beside this page (the number moves in the fourth place, if at all).

## 22. The double is where the smallness lives: the box calibrated, the operator form, and the one universal lemma the full gas needs (18:50 EDT, read from `date`)

**J4 with the exact double on the 2×2×2 box interior.** Gauge-fixing the copy's spokes, W(D_box)/(3a₁)^{24} = 3^{−12}·F with F := ∫_{SU(2)^6} Π_{(i,j) ∈ E(octahedron)} χ₁(U_iU_j) dU — the spin-1 octahedron integral. F = 16981/202500 = 0.083857 is the Yang–Mills seat.s identity-hold reading of the spin-1 walls on the octahedron (F₁(1), the 120-point 11-design 2I, 18:4x), taken here as that integral pending its own confirmation of the identification; `records/surface-gas/octa_f1.py` computes the same integral exactly by a road sharing no line — each U_i integrated by the projector onto Inv(1^{⊗4}) in the pairing basis, Gram 6I + 3J, inverse (5I − J)/30, the twelve edge pairs contracted as one integer tensor sum — and its value is appended when it ends. So W(D_box)/(3a₁)^{24} = 1.578·10^{−7}, and J4 reads

  |N(box)| ≤ (7/3)^{3} · √(1.578·10^{−7}) = 12.70 · 3.97·10^{−4} = 0.0050,

where the rank form had given 12.7: the degree factor of the six face centres (12.7) is more than paid by the double, and the bound sits below iii-ac's thirty-five-assignment sum ρ/4 = 0.0092 (the per-assignment column, the exact N(box), is awaited from two engines). **The lesson is general:** on a dense piece the junction bound is sharp enough once the double is exact; what is loose in J5 is the cap and the rank form, not J4.

**J10 (the operator form).** Cut the Y-network along any set K of off-S links into two halves, each half carrying the vertex tensors of the sheet's vertices it touches. The cut links' projectors have operator norm one, so

  |N(Y)| ≤ ‖Ω₁‖·‖Ω₂‖,  ‖Ω_i‖² = N(Y_i ∪_K Y_i*) ,

the Frobenius norm over the cut legs of a half being the value of the DOUBLED HALF — the half's plaquettes with their copy glued along K — which is itself a decoration of S. The spine cut at its link gives iii-bd's |J_m| ≤ ‖T_u‖‖T_w‖/3^m = E(m)/3^m (S13); a disc cut across gives 3^{1−n}. J4 is the case K = ∅ with one half the sheet; J10 moves the cut into the piece, where the halves' doubles are smaller objects.

**The one universal lemma the full gas needs.** Every term of §21's accounting is now exact or sharp except one: a universal bound on the weight of a closed spin-1 complex,

  ρ(D) := ( ∫ Π_{q∈D} χ₁(U_q) dU )^{1/|D|},

since W(D_Y)/(3a₁)^{2|Y|} = ρ(D_Y)^{2|Y|} enters J4 and J7 as √. What is known: a closed surface has ρ = 3^{(χ−n)/n} → 1/3; the spine's double 3^{−m}R_m, ρ → 1 from below; the box.s double ρ = (1.578·10^{−7})^{1/24} = 0.521; the two-block foam's rank form 3^{−10}; the sup bound ρ ≤ 3 everywhere; and the rank form Π_P 3^{χ(P)−n(P)}·Π_ℓ R_{m_ℓ} exceeds one only where high-multiplicity links outnumber the pieces' Euler deficits — the full plaquette set of a region of ℤ⁴ (every link in six plaquettes) reads 15^{2/3} = 6.1 per plaquette on the rank form and 3 on the cap. **If ρ(D) ≤ 1 for every closed D, the junction bound's rate is the degree factor alone, 3·e(8)^{3/8} = 9.27 for every piece at every multiplicity (the channel factor 1.32 per doubly-used link included: ≤ 3·1.457³·1.32³ = 21 at eight-spoke vertices, 15 at three) and the full gas has a proved number ≈ 0.36 at the uniform rate and more when graded; if ρ can exceed one on dense complexes, the dense tail is where the lattice gap's expansion needs a different organisation.** The lemma is stated as the object; the experiments that bear on it: the full plaquette set of the 2⁴ and 3⁴ blocks (24 and 216 plaquettes) integrated exactly by the same projector method; the doubles of every census piece (iii-ac's exact-double column); the 3D foams. Nothing above β = 0.2452 is claimed.

**§22, added 18:57 EDT — two roads on the box's double, the convention derived, and the 4-cube's full plaquette set weighed exactly.** (a) THE CONVENTION. In the double of the box interior each interior plaquette q (vertices f₁, m, f₂, c; spokes U_a from the centre c to the face centre a) and its copy q* share the two rest links; merging them gives (1/3)·χ₁(U_{f₂}^{−1}U_{f₁}·(U*_{f₂}^{−1}U*_{f₁})^{−1}) = (1/3)·χ₁(V_{f₂}^{−1}V_{f₁}) with V_a := U_aU*_a^{−1}, Haar-distributed; so W(D_box)/(3a₁)^{24} = 3^{−12}·∫Π_{(a,b)∈E(octahedron)} χ₁(V_aV_b^{−1}) — the INVERSE on the second spoke, which the Yang–Mills seat's F₁(1) carries (its walls traversed as closed squares). On the octahedron, which has triangles, this is not the integral without the inverse. (b) THE SECOND ROAD AGREES TO THE RATIONAL: records/surface-gas/octa_f1b.py (the projector onto Inv(1^{⊗4}) in the pairing basis, Gram 6I + 3J, inverse (5I − J)/30, the twelve edges contracted vertex by vertex as one integer sum, ORIENT=inv) reads F = 61131600/30^6 = 16981/202500 = 0.0838568 — the Yang–Mills seat's value under the 120-point design, two engines sharing no line; and without the inverse (ORIENT=same) 20891760/30^6 = 87049/3037500 = 0.0286581, a different number, as the seat said. So W(D_box)/(3a₁)^{24} = 1.5779·10^{−7}, ρ(D_box) = 0.5207, and J4 with the exact double reads 0.00505 for the box. (c) THE 4-CUBE'S FULL PLAQUETTE SET, EXACTLY (records/surface-gas/tesseract_rho.py): 24 plaquettes, 32 links, every link in exactly three plaquettes, so every link integral is the rank-one projector ε_{X₁X₂X₃}ε_{Y₁Y₂Y₃}/6 (the three plaquettes in one order on both), and THE INTEGRAL FACTORISES OVER THE SIXTEEN VERTICES: ∫Π_{24}χ₁ = 6^{−32}·Π_v t_v, t_v the tetrahedral ε-network of the four links at v contracted along the six plaquette corners there — t_v = +6 at all sixteen vertices (every pair of links at a vertex of the 4-cube spans one plaquette: the net is K₄, Σε_{abc}ε_{ade}ε_{bdf}ε_{cef} = 6), so ∫Π_{24}χ₁(U_q) = 6^{−16} = 3.54·10^{−13} and ρ = 6^{−2/3} = 0.3029 — the densest 4D complex the rank form priced at 6.1 per plaquette (15^{2/3}) and the cap at 3 weighs LESS per plaquette than a closed surface's 1/3 at this size. The same theorem holds for every closed complex of multiplicity three throughout: the weight is 6^{−L}·Π_v t_v with t_v a closed ε-network at v. (d) THE VERTEX-AMPLITUDE FORM, in general: with an orthonormal basis of intertwiners u_ℓ^{(i)} at each link, ∫Π_qχ₁(U_q) = Σ_{labelings} Π_v A_v, A_v the contraction at v of the chosen intertwiners' legs along the plaquette corners at v, and |A_v| ≤ 1 by the Frobenius inequality (every corner joins two different links) — which is the rank bound; the smallness is in the amplitudes: the tetrahedral A_v = 1/6, a surface vertex of d plaquettes A_v = 3^{1−d/2}, and the box's double sums 3^{12} labelings to 1.6·10^{−7}. THE UNIVERSAL LEMMA is therefore a bound on vertex amplitudes summed over labelings — ρ(D) ≤ 1 is now evidenced on every dense complex read (the box's double 0.52, the 4-cube 0.30, the 3D two-block 3^{−10/36}) and refuted on none. Nothing above β = 0.2452 is claimed.

## 23. THE VERTEX-NORM THEOREM — the weight of a closed spin-1 complex is at most the product over its vertices of the square roots of their star integrals; the universal double bound becomes a finite computation (19:05 EDT, read from `date`)

**Theorem V.** Let D be a finite closed set of plaquettes of ℤ⁴ (every link in at least two), all at spin 1, and I(D) := ∫Π_{q∈D}χ₁(U_q) dU. For a vertex v of D let G_v be the graph whose vertices are the links of D at v and whose edges are the plaquettes of D at v (each plaquette at v joins its two links at v; two links span at most one plaquette, so G_v is simple, a subgraph of the cocktail-party graph K_{2,2,2,2} on the eight directions, with every vertex of degree ≥ 2), and let

  S(G_v) := ∫_{SU(2)^{V(G_v)}} Π_{(i,j) ∈ E(G_v)} χ₁(U_i^{−1}U_j) dU

be its STAR INTEGRAL. Then |I(D)| ≤ Π_{v} √S(G_v).

*Proof.* Expand each link integral in an orthonormal basis of its invariants: ∫D¹(U_ℓ)^{⊗m_ℓ} = Σ_λ |u_ℓ^λ⟩⟨u_ℓ^λ|, the ket's m_ℓ legs the plaquettes' row indices at ℓ's tail, the bra's their column indices at its head. The plaquette traces contract indices only at vertices (a corner joins two links' legs for one plaquette), so I(D) = Σ_{labelings λ} Π_v A_v(λ), A_v(λ) := ⟨W_v | ⊗_{ℓ∋v} u_ℓ^{λ_ℓ}⟩, W_v the product of the corner δ's at v. This is a closed tensor network whose tensors are the A_v and whose indices (the labels) are each shared by exactly two tensors (a link's two ends), with no self-loop; contracting it node by node, each merge contracting at once every index the two tensors share — a matrix product exactly because no tensor shares an index with itself (a self-loop would be a trace and would cost √dim) — is a sequence of matrix products, each bounded in Frobenius norm by the product of the factors' Frobenius norms, the last a 1×1, so |I(D)| ≤ Π_v ‖A_v‖_F (wording corrected 2026-10-31 23:3x on iii-73's referee read; the step is where the Frobenius inequality lives). And ‖A_v‖_F² = Σ_λ |A_v(λ)|² = ⟨W_v| ⊗_ℓ (Σ_λ|u_ℓ^λ⟩⟨u_ℓ^λ|) |W_v⟩ = ∫Π_{ℓ∋v}dU_ℓ ⟨W_v| ⊗_ℓ D¹(U_ℓ)^{⊗m_ℓ} |W_v⟩ = ∫Π_{q∋v} Σ_{ab} D¹(U_ℓ_q)_{ab} D¹(U_{ℓ′_q})_{ab} = ∫Π_{q∋v} χ₁(U_{ℓ_q}^{−1}U_{ℓ′_q}) = S(G_v), each corner δ turning into one spin-1 character of the two links' relative rotation. (The sign of I(D) is not claimed in general; every value read so far is positive.) ∎

**Known answers.** A closed surface: every vertex of d plaquettes has G_v = C_d and S(C_d) = 3^{2−d} (integrate round the cycle, 1/3 per link, χ₁(1) = 3 at the close), so Π_v√S = 3^{Σ(1−d_v/2)} = 3^{V−E} = 3^{χ−F}: THE BOUND IS EXACT on every closed surface. The 4-cube's full plaquette set: G_v = K₄ at all sixteen vertices, S(K₄) = 1/36, Π√S = 6^{−16}: EXACT again (§22 (c)). The box's double: at its two centres G = K_{2,2,2} with S = 16981/202500 (§22), at the twelve edge midpoints and the six face centres the local graphs are small cycles and their unions — the product over all twenty vertices is an upper bound on the exact 1.578·10^{−7} and is read beside it when the local graphs are tabulated. The vertex graphs are finite in number: a subgraph of K_{2,2,2,2} with minimum degree two — so **the universal double constant is a FINITE COMPUTATION**: with s(G) := S(G)^{1/|E(G)|} the per-plaquette-corner rate and s_max its maximum over the admissible vertex graphs, Σ_v|E(G_v)| = 4|D| gives

  I(D) ≤ s_max^{2|D|},  i.e. ρ(D) ≤ s_max².

**The values in hand.** s(C₃) = 3^{−1/3} = 0.693, s(C₄) = 3^{−1/2} = 0.577, s(C_d) ↓ 1/3; s(K₄) = 36^{−1/6} = 0.550; s(K_{2,2,2}) = (16981/202500)^{1/12} = 0.813. The dense graphs are the large ones; **S(K_{2,2,2,2}) (eight directions, twenty-four plaquettes at one vertex — the vertex star of the full plaquette set of ℤ⁴) is the value that decides s_max**, with K_{2,2,2,1} and the near-complete subgraphs beside it; the projector contraction that read the octahedron in four seconds needs 9^{12} intermediates on K_{2,2,2,2} and is asked of the engines in the label basis (S(G) = Σ_{λ}C(λ)², C the spin-network evaluation of G with the intertwiners λ at its vertices).

**What it closes if s_max ≤ 1.** The double in J4 and J7 obeys √W(D_Y)/(3a₁)^{|Y|} = √I(D_Y) ≤ s_max^{|Y|}, so the junction bound's rate for EVERY piece, singly or multiply attached, is 3·(degree factor)·(channel factor)·s_max with the three factors bounded in J6 and J7: at most 3·e(8)^{3/8}·1.155³·s_max = 14.3·s_max at eight-spoke vertices and 3·e(3)·1.32³·s_max = 8.0·s_max at three-spoke ones — below the KP tail's divergence line (21 at β = 0.45) for any s_max ≤ 1, and the full gas then has a proved number by the graded fold-in. If s(K_{2,2,2,2}) is near 0.81 the universal rate is ≈ 9.4 on the densest attachment and the graded number stays near the sub-gas's. Nothing above β = 0.2452 is claimed; s_max is the one number owed.

## 24. THE LOCAL THEOREM — the junction bound without the double: every piece is bounded by a product of local star ratios at the vertices it touches, exact on every reference object, and the full gas's rate is a finite local maximum (2026-10-29 20:32 EDT, read from `date`)

**Corrections first (law 18).** §23's closing paragraph is withdrawn in two places. (i) Its vertex graphs were written as simple; in the double D_Y a plaquette and its copy share the same two rest links at a sheet vertex, so the double's vertex graphs carry doubled edges, and the Yang–Mills seat's exact readings of the doubled stars (the doubled triangle 23/15, s = 1.0738; the doubled four-cycle 259/225, s = 1.0177; doubled trees exactly 1) put s_max above 1 — "if s_max ≤ 1" was not available as written. (ii) Σ_v|E(G_v)| = 4|D_Y| = 8|Y|, so √I(D_Y) ≤ s_max^{2|Y|}: the per-plaquette factor of the double is s_max², not s_max. Both defects die with the double itself, which the theorem below no longer needs.

**Setting.** S the sheet (a closed branch-free surface at spin ½, F plaquettes, Euler characteristic χ), Y a piece (spin-1 plaquettes, S ∪ Y closed; T its rest links, O its off-S links, each off-S link in at least two plaquettes of Y), N(Y) := W(S∪Y)/((3a₁)^{|Y|}W(S)). For a vertex v let G_v be the vertex graph of S ∪ Y at v: its vertices the links of S ∪ Y at v, its edges the plaquettes at v, each edge coloured by its plaquette's spin (½ on S, 1 on Y). The sheet's star at v is a cycle C_v of d_v spin-½ edges through the sheet's links L_S(v); Y's star Γ_v is a graph of spin-1 edges on R_v ∪ O_v, R_v := L_S(v) ∩ {links of Y at v} the rest links Y uses at v (m_v := |R_v|; k_v, used in "The rate" below and throughout the tables, is the number of Y-plaquettes at v, |E(Γ_v)| — one symbol each since the correction of 2026-10-31 23:3x on iii-73's referee read, the statement having used k_v for both), O_v the off-S links at v. **Γ_v is simple**: two distinct plaquettes at v span distinct pairs of links (the hypothesis keeps the doubled-edge case out of the local sweep; Theorem L's proof does not use it — iii-73, 23:0x). For a graph G with edge spins, S(G) := ∫Π_{(i,j)∈E(G)} χ_{j_{ij}}(U_i^{−1}U_j) dU is its star integral.

**Theorem L (the local bound).** For every piece Y,

  |N(Y)| ≤ Π_{v : Γ_v ≠ ∅} r_v,  with  r_v² = S(Γ_v) off the sheet,  r_v² = 2^{m_v−2}·S(C^{½}_{m_v}(R_v) ∪ Γ_v) on it,

where C^{½}_k(R_v) is the k-cycle of spin-½ edges on R_v in the sheet's cyclic order (for k = 1 a loop, χ_½(1) = 2, so that r_v² = S(Γ_v) there too; for k = 2 a doubled spin-½ edge).

*Proof.* (i) Theorem V holds with mixed spins: for a closed complex D whose plaquettes carry spins j_q, I(D) := ∫Π_q χ_{j_q}(U_q) dU expands as in §23 with the Haar projector onto the invariants of ⊗_q V_{j_q} at every link; the characters are real, the contraction pairwise, and |I(D)| ≤ Π_v √S(G_v) with every edge of G_v carrying its plaquette's spin. (ii) W(S∪Y) = (2u)^F(3a₁)^{|Y|}·I(S∪Y) and W(S) = (2u)^F·2^{χ−F}, so N(Y) = I(S∪Y)/2^{χ−F}; and 2^{χ−F} = Π_{v∈S} 2^{1−d_v/2} = Π_{v∈S} √S(C^{½}_{d_v}) by the cycle law at spin ½ (the sheet's own vertex-norm bound is an equality). (iii) At a sheet vertex with Γ_v = ∅ the numerator's factor is √S(C_{d_v}) and cancels. At a sheet vertex with Γ_v ≠ ∅, the sheet links at v outside R_v have degree two in G_v and no spin-1 edge; integrating them, an arc of a sheet plaquettes between consecutive rest links contributes 2^{1−a}χ_½ of the two rest links' relative rotation, so S(G_v) = 2^{m_v−d_v}·S(C^{½}_{m_v}(R_v) ∪ Γ_v), and dividing by S(C_{d_v}) = 2^{2−d_v} gives r_v². Off the sheet G_v = Γ_v. ∎

**What it removes.** No double, no cut constant C(T), no channel factor: a multiply-used rest link is a vertex of higher degree in Γ_v and nothing else; the doubled stars of §23 never enter, because every G_v is simple. The global Cauchy–Schwarz of J4 (sheet against piece) is replaced by one Frobenius inequality per vertex, with the sheet's star integrated exactly at each.

**Known answers** (the engine: the binary icosahedral 11-design in exact Z[φ] arithmetic, `records/surface-gas/design_star.py`, every value an exact rational whose φ-part vanishes; met first: the cycle laws at both spins, K₄ = 1/36, K_{2,2,2} = 16981/202500 at spin 1 and 73/324 at spin ½); all six values below — W₃, W₄, W₅, the two-walls star 1/2, the chord 1, the disc vertex 1/3 — MET on the Yang–Mills seat's C engine with a spin per edge, `STDLIB/build/aperanton/junction_cert.2026-10-29e.txt`, two roads sharing no line). The chord (a two-cycle at spin ½ with a spin-1 edge): S = 1, r = 1 — the lone wall reads ≤ 1 (exact 1). The disc: the two-plaquette boundary vertex (two-cycle at ½, a spin-1 path through the interior link) reads 1/3 and the interior vertex C₄(1) reads 1/9, so an n-plaquette disc reads 3^{1−n} exactly. The m-spines: r_u² = 2^{m−2}·S(W_m) with W_m = C_m(½) + m spokes(1), and **S(W₃) = 1/9, S(W₄) = 7/108, S(W₅) = 5/162**, so N ≤ 2/9, 7/27, 20/81 = E(m)/3^m — the exact values of S13, J2 and the census. Two lone walls on one link (iii-ac's known answer, 1): at each end r² = 2·S(C₃(½) on ℓ, a, b + spin-1 edges (ℓ,a), (ℓ,b)) = 2·(1/2) = 1. The cube on one link: C₃(1) = 1/3 at all eight vertices, (1/3)⁴ = 1/81, exact (J7). The box: √S(K_{2,2,2})·(4S(W₄))³ = 0.28961·(7/27)³ = 0.005047 — J4's own number again — against the exact 221/3^{11} = 0.001248: the one reference object where the bound is loose (a factor 4.05, the centre's sign cancellation that no vertex-wise bound sees).

**The rate.** With k_v the number of Y-plaquettes at v, Σ_v k_v = 4|Y|, so |N(Y)| ≤ Π_v r_v = Π_v (r_v^{1/k_v})^{k_v} ≤ θ^{4|Y|} and

  γ ≤ 3·θ⁴,  θ := sup_v r_v^{1/k_v} over the admissible local configurations;  graded, γ(s) ≤ 3·θ(s)⁴ with θ(s) the supremum over configurations with k_v ≤ s.

θ ≥ 1 by the chord. The per-corner rates in hand: chord 1; W₃-vertex (2/9)^{1/6} = 0.778; W₄-vertex (7/27)^{1/8} = 0.845; W₅-vertex (20/81)^{1/10} = 0.869; the disc's vertices 0.760; C₃(1) 0.833; K₄ 0.742; K_{2,2,2} 0.902; K_{2,2,1,1} 0.919. A dense vertex enters only through the 2k_v-th root of its star: the full star of ℤ⁴, K_{2,2,2,2} with twenty-four plaquettes, as S^{1/48}, so a cut bound suffices there (S(K_{2,2,2,2}) ≤ S of the half C₄ × K₂ with its rungs quadrupled, by the operator form of §22, read on the exact P₆ road). **THE CONJECTURE θ = 1** — adding Y's spin-1 edges to the sheet's cycle never raises the star integral, and a simple spin-1 star never exceeds 1 — is J8's |N(Y)| ≤ 1 for every piece, now a FINITE LOCAL CHECK over the admissible configurations (a simple subgraph of the cocktail-party graph K_{2,2,2,2} on the eight directions, with the sheet's cycle on its rest links); it is stated as a conjecture until that check is complete.

**Status.** The sub-gas numbers 0.4709 / 0.4759 stand; the full gas's number waits on θ(s) from the local sweep, not on any double; every threshold remains conditional on S12 as iii-ac states it tonight (proved where every exchange set has a fibre leaf, open on full-rank leafless sets, the abstract class failing by either sign). Nothing above β = 0.2452 is claimed.

**§24, added 2026-10-29 21:53 EDT — THE GENERAL FORM, THE SECOND READING, AND WHAT THE TAIL WAITS ON.** (a) THE GENERAL FORM, from the Yang–Mills seat's second reading (signed, one edge case named): the theorem's statement is r_v² := S(G_v(S∪Y)) / S(G_v(S)); the closed form 2^{k−2}S(C_k(½)(R_v) ∪ Γ_v) is its evaluation when the sheet's star at v is one cycle. For a universal constant the J1 expansion at the vertex gives r_v² ≤ Σ_{E ⊆ R} 3^{c_v(E)}·E(|E|)·√S(Γ_v ∪_E Γ_v*), the two copies of Γ_v joined by a spin-1 rung at every vertex of E, c_v(E) the corners among the rest links of E (the factor 3 per corner per chain of J3 — iii-73's caveat of 2026-11-01 00:0x on signing (6): as first displayed the count was written corner-free), with Σ_{E} E(|E|) = 4^{m−1} when no corner is present; a universal bound therefore follows from the lemma *a simple spin-1 star integral is at most one* (true in every case read — the triangle 1/3, K₄ 1/36, K_{2,2,2} 0.084, K_{2,2,1,1} 0.110, K_{3,3} = 0 — and unproved; a sum of squares, bounded by its rank, with no inequality yet that sees the cancellation), and reads θ ≤ 2^{(m−1)/k} on the dense vertices it would need (1.5 at k = 12, below the divergence line's 1.63), useless below k = 9. **§24, added 2026-10-29 22:07 EDT — THE SPECIMEN DECIDED AT THE DESK, AND THE PER-CORNER RATE IS NOT MONOTONE.** (a) THE SPECIMEN. **§24, added 2026-10-30 05:3x EDT (read from `date`; the trunk seat) — THE OPERATOR'S SIDENOTE ASSESSED, THE TAIL-CLOSURE PRICE, AND A CONJECTURE ENTERED UNDER ΕΠΟΧΗ.** (a) THE SIDENOTE (05:2x: knot theory, braid theory and cohomology tie to mass in III; use them for verification or a non-credentialist proof). The tie is real and load-bearing already in two places. ΑΓΚΥΛΗ's chord law (T-774, ΑΓΚΥΛΗ II–III: the surface gas's vertex law at a vertex whose lone walls form a chord diagram D on the vertex cycle is the sl₂ weight system of D in the fundamental representation, `r(D) = Σ_{S⊆chords} (−1)^{w−|S|} 2^{|S|+L(S)} / (2·3^w)`, a function of the crossing graph alone; 130 theorems in VertexLaw.lean, arm 130) is the SECOND ROAD under this section's sparse wall values — the four-cube specimen's N = 1 met by its piece evaluator (IV.6), the lone-wall column of the local table met line by line — and it is where the kernel reads the gas's vertices. Cohomology is in the weights themselves: a leaf weighs u^F·2^χ (the Euler characteristic), the memory of a sheet is H¹ (ΥΠΕΡΑΓΩΓΗ), the local theorem prices a piece by a product over vertices (a cochain-level factorisation), and the gap's mass lives in ΜΑΖΑ's Laplacian. What the sidenote could buy the tail was tried at the desk and does not come free: at a rest link carrying d chords the Haar projector is onto `Inv(V_½⊗V_½⊗V_1^{⊗d})`, of dimension Rio(d) + Rio(d+1) (2, 4, 9, 21, 51 for d = 2..6), the Weingarten-weighted sum over pairings of 2 + 2d spin-½ legs — not the 2^d terms a chord law with distinct points would give — so the chord law's 2^w closed form does not extend to the dense rows, and the intertwiner engine's projector contraction IS that Weingarten sum done efficiently. The TASKS row of 05:2x staked the extension as a polynomial second road for the dense core; that stake is CORRECTED here, dated (law 18): the second road for the dense rows is a second exact engine (the Python projector road, `mixed_star.py`, which met the intertwiner engine on 15,949 wall rows), not a chord-diagram formula. (b) THE TAIL-CLOSURE PRICE (`kp_tail_probe.py`, the corrected float road, F₀ = 20): with the tail exact through k = 16 and the Frobenius sieve's proven γ(s) = 3·rate_F(s)⁴ beyond (rate_F = 1.4448 at k = 17 falling to 1.2532 at k = 24; `frobenius_retire.awk`), β* = 0.4707; exact through 14, 0.4670; through 12, 0.4615; through 10, 0.4534; every tail row under θ(6), 0.4764. The sieve's census (`frobenius_retire.census.2026-10-30a.txt`): 36,953 of 976,774 rows retired; the largest un-retired per-corner rate 1.5425 at k = 9, 1.5337 (10), 1.5224 (11), 1.5131 (12), 1.5087 (13), 1.5017 (14), 1.4986 (15), 1.4987 (16), 1.4448 (17), 1.3984 (18), 1.3582 (19), 1.3230 (20), 1.2920 (21), 1.2301 (22), 1.2406 (23), 1.2532 (24) — so γ(9..24) = 16.98 today against the staked 4.6125. **THE HONEST NUMBER, PROVED CONSTANTS ONLY, READ BY THE CERTIFICATE (`kp_cert_table_scan_page/tree.2026-10-30a.txt`): β* = 0.3956 on the page's dressing (PASS at 0.3956 for α ∈ [0.0002, 0.0003], refused at 0.3958) and 0.3978 on the tree's; 0.2452 PASS in both.** Above Theorem 8.7's 0.2452; below Part F's 0.45. The float road's stage prices (located, not certified): 0.4337 once every row under pass 1's cap is read, 0.4458 through k = 14, 0.4707 once every above-cap row through k = 16 is read — the 50 rows refused for want of a split at 3 GB are all at k = 17..24, where the sieve closes them (γ(17+) = 13.07) — and 0.4764 under the stake. (c) THE SIEVE RE-READ (law 33). `frobenius_retire.awk` is sound: S(G) is the contraction of one Haar projector per link along the plaquette edges; ‖AB‖_F ≤ ‖A‖_F‖B‖_F node by node and Cauchy–Schwarz at the last gives |S| ≤ Π_l √R_l with R_l = dim Inv of the legs at l (Riordan numbers for spin-1 legs, the sheet's two spin-½ legs by V_½⊗V_½ = V_0 ⊕ V_1); its h = 1 branch is unreachable (a rest link carries two sheet legs; the true dimension there is 0); its m = 1 case over-estimates (the loop's legs counted at the link give √(Rio(d) + Rio(d + 1)) ≥ √Rio(d), the loop's factor 2 against 2^{m−2}), so every table entry is an upper bound. It is loose on dense stars because ‖P‖_F = √R where ‖P‖_op = 1. (d) THE SCHEDULE AT THE MEASURED RATE. Pass 1b: twelve shards at about 80 MB private each, dearest rows first (the kill-switch's order), 2,751 rows read by 06:45, maximum 1.068165 at k = 13, m = 8; ends about 13:30. The dense core: `local_tail_dense.sh` cut at k ≤ 16 (k ≥ 17 rows buy the exact θ, not the number), three engines at about 400 MB private — the core rows' splits are small — 88 rows by 06:45, maximum 1.034634; 2,318 core rows and about 4,000 k ≤ 16 rest rows, 40 + 36 engine-hours. The six sixteen-chord classes: class 1 single-process since 05:18 (one prime done by 06:40, about 10:50 in all); classes 2–7 as four `ONLYPRIME` processes each with `class_prime_recon.sh` reconstructing from three residues and the fourth prime verifying (KAT: K₄'s own four residues → 1/36 VERIFIED; a bent residue refused), about 1.4 hours per class. The proved-constants number reaches 0.4707 when every above-cap row through k = 16 is read: about twenty hours of wall time at six to nine engines, so tomorrow, not tonight. (e) RUN AM. (f) THE NEXT INSTRUMENT, NAMED. A sharper proven per-row bound: contract node by node paying ‖·‖_F at the first and last node only and, between them, the operator norm of each projector REALIGNED across its legs toward the cluster and away (‖Σ_τ τ⊗τ̄‖_op ≤ Σ_τ ‖τ‖²_op, a fraction of R when the invariants are entangled across the cut: K₄ at spin 1 reads 1/9 this way against the sieve's 1 and the exact 1/36). Its falsifier: a read row above its bound. Not started tonight. Nothing above β = 0.2452 is claimed.

## 25. THE CERTIFIED REALIGNED BOUND — what the certificate needs, measured; the unread rows closed without the engine; the referees' signatures and corrections; the table-mode number (2026-10-31 23:4x EDT, read from `date`; the trunk seat iii-43, with iii-73 and iii-ac as second readers)

**(a) What the certificate needs, measured before anything was built.** The table mode's number is a function of sixteen upper bounds, r2max(k) for k = 9..24, and the Kotecký–Preiss sum weighs the small sizes most: a hypothetical table was run through the certificate (`STDLIB/build/aperanton/kp_cert_table_sensitivity.2026-10-31a.txt`, 22:2x–23:0x; hypothetical, no record of the gas) to read which entries the bar β* ≥ 0.45 turns on. With k = 9..14 at per-corner 1.09 and k ≥ 17 at the Frobenius sieve as it stands: k = 15 and 16 at 1.48 PASS 0.4500 (page dressing; 1.49 refused; the tree dressing passes 1.49), at the sieve's 1.4986 refused; with k = 15, 16 at 1.45, k = 13 and 14 at 1.45 PASS and at 1.50 refused; with k = 9..16 all at 1.09, 0.4700 passes in both dressings and 0.4800 is refused. The 36,944 rows the Frobenius sieve had retired sit in the table at their sieve bound, up to the stake's 1.1135 per corner: with k = 9..15 at 1.1136 and k = 16 at 1.30 the certificate still passes 0.4700 in both dressings (the advisor's reading of 23:0x, run as a control). So the number turns on three things only: the k = 13 and 14 entries below 1.45, the k = 15 and 16 entries below 1.48, and nothing at k ≥ 17 — where the Frobenius sieve gives 1.5087, 1.5017, 1.4986, 1.4987: a factor between 1.2 and 3 on the worst unread rows, not the 300 engine-hours of reading them exactly.

**(b) Pass 1d, the retired rows.** The 36,944 sieve-retired rows (survey cost 1.78·10¹⁰ multiply-adds in all) were read exactly in sixty seconds on four engines (`local_tail_pass1d.sh`, 23:00:31–23:01:33), so no row of the table rests on the sieve at k ≤ 12 any more; the k = 9..12 entries are exact maxima (1.080620, 1.065357, 1.072972, 1.080160 per corner at ids 421860, 628861, 950205, 1077888).

**(c) Lemma R (the realigned operator-norm bound).** Let G be a star graph on the links of a vertex (chords at spin 1, half-edges at spin ½), S(G) its star integral: the contraction of one Haar projector P_l per link l, onto the invariants of the legs at l, along the edges of G, each edge two strands (the a- and b-indices of its character). Contract the links in any order 1..n: with C_1 = P_1 and C_t = C_{t−1}M_t, where M_t is P_t realigned as a matrix from its strands toward the links already contracted to its strands toward the rest,

  |S| ≤ ‖P_1‖_F · Π_{t=2}^{n−1} ‖M_t‖_op · ‖P_n‖_F,  ‖P_l‖_F = √R_l, R_l = dim Inv at l,

because ‖C_t‖_F ≤ ‖C_{t−1}‖_F‖M_t‖_op at every merge (a matrix product, the network having no self-loop) and the last link closes by Cauchy–Schwarz. The Frobenius sieve (§24, |S| ≤ Π_l √R_l) is the case ‖M_t‖_op ≤ ‖M_t‖_F = √R_t at every link. Writing P_t = Σ_{a,b} (G⁻¹)_{ab}|τ_a⟩⟨τ_b| over an integer basis τ of the invariants with Gram matrix G, M_t = Σ_{ab}(G⁻¹)_{ab} T_a ⊗ T_b with T_a the unfolding of τ_a along the split, and the block Cauchy–Schwarz ‖Σ_a A_a ⊗ B_a‖²_op ≤ ‖Σ_a A_aA_aᵀ‖_op‖Σ_a B_aᵀB_a‖_op gives

  ‖M_t‖_op² ≤ λ_max(Tr_N P_t) · λ_max(Tr_S P_t),

the two partial traces of the projector over the two sides of the split — real symmetric positive semidefinite rational matrices of trace R_t. Every quantity is exact: in the real basis (e_x, i·e_y, e_z) of each spin-1 leg the metric is diag(1, −1, 1), the ε-block and the (1; ½, ½) coupling σ^aε are real integer tensors after a scalar on each block, the basis change is unitary so no norm moves; G⁻¹ is the exact inverse of an integer Gram matrix; and λ_max(A) ≤ μ is CERTIFIED by the exact LDLᵀ decomposition of μI − A (every pivot positive, Sylvester) at a μ one part in 2²⁰ above a floating estimate, or by the exact Gershgorin row sum where the matrix exceeds 200 rows. The bound is carried squared, bound2 = 4^{m−2}R_1R_nΠ_t μ_{A,t}μ_{B,t} ≥ r2², a rational the table takes as it takes the sieve's integer. The contraction order comes from the floating prototype of §24 (any order is valid; the prototype chooses a good one). What is classical (W = 0): ‖AB‖_F ≤ ‖A‖_F‖B‖_op, the block Cauchy–Schwarz, Sylvester's criterion, Gershgorin. What this page earns: the bound applied to the star integrals of the finite family, its exact instrument, and the census below.

**(d) The instrument.** `STDLIB/build/aperanton/surface-gas/realign_cert.py` (pure Python, `fractions.Fraction`, no numerical library; KAT, NEGCTL and ROWS modes). Its known answers (23:1x): the projectors of the seven smallest leg types idempotent with trace R exactly (d = 2, 3, 4 at h = 0; d = 0..3 at h = 2); seven stars with exact S inside exact² ≤ bound2S ≤ Frobenius² — K₄ at spin 1 bound 1/9 against the exact 1/36 and the sieve's 1; C₃ and C₄ exact (1/3, 1/9); the chord exact (1); W₃ 1/6 against 1/9; K₄ of walls 2.0 against the exact 3271/3600 [corrected dated 2026-11-03 08:34 EST: the KAT line had carried the sheet's r2 = S/4 = 3271/14400 as if it were S — the star integral of C₄(½) + K₄(1) is 3271/3600, found on two roads by the eight-cell's S7 instrument (the exact projectors through the inertia door) and the 600-cell design average; the inequality held either way, the bound 2.0 stands, the honest slack is 2.20, not 8.8; the other six anchors are S itself and meet]; K₂,₂,₂ 1.0 against 16981/202500 — `REALIGN KAT HELD: 7 known answers`. ## 27. THE EVEN STARS — Theorem V at spin ½ on the finite family of a leaf's vertex stars; a preregistration written before the instrument exists (2026-11-01 03:0x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat iii-43; the first act after THE FAR SHORE §4.0)

**The question.** §25 (l) names the one staked input of Theorem 8.9: Lemma S12 on the full-rank leafless remainder. §23 (i) (Theorem V with mixed spins, spin ½ included, signed) gives, for every all-½ leaf X, |W_½(X)| ≤ (2u)^{|X|} Π_v √S_½(G_v), G_v the star of X at the lattice vertex v — automatically a simple even subgraph of K_{2,2,2,2} (a leaf is a set of plaquettes; a Z₂-cycle has every link at even multiplicity). The even subgraphs are the cycle space of K_{2,2,2,2}, 2^{24 − 8 + 1} = 131,072 of them, finitely many orbits under the hyperoctahedral group B₄ of order 384. This section stakes the table before it is computed, and states what the table can and cannot decide.

**The instrument (to be written after this section lands): `STDLIB/build/aperanton/surface-gas/even_stars.py`, exact.** With SU(2) the unit quaternions, tr(U_i⁻¹U_j) = 2⟨u_i, u_j⟩ in ℝ⁴, so S_½(G) = 2^{|E|} ∫_{(S³)^V} Π_{(i,j)∈E} ⟨u_i, u_j⟩, and the moments of the uniform measure on S³ are Wick sums with the constants of n = 4: E[u_{a₁}⋯u_{a_{2k}}] = (Σ_{pairings} Π δ)/(4·6⋯(2k+2)) = (Σ_{pairings} Π δ)/(2^k (k+1)!). Hence, exactly,

  S_½(G) = 2^{|E|} · Σ_{M} 4^{L(M)} / Π_h 2^{k_h}(k_h + 1)!,

the sum over a choice M of a perfect matching of the incident edges at every vertex h of G (k_h = deg(h)/2), L(M) the number of closed loops the matchings and the edges form. THE READING OF THE SUM: a matching at a half-axis of degree 4 is exactly a resolution of that link's four plaquettes into two corner pairs, and L(M) is the number of sheet vertices over v in that resolution — so the star integral is the sum over the vertex's resolutions of 4^{m_v}, weighted. A second road for the known answers: the Schur recursion along a cycle (each integrated vertex halves the trace), and the engine's own value at K_{2,2,2}.

**The known answers (KATs the instrument must meet before any table line is read).** (K1) the cycle C_d, d = 3..8: S_½ = 2^{2−d} (Schur, d − 1 times, then tr 1 = 2); (K2) K_{2,2,2}, the full three-dimensional star (six half-axes, twelve edges, every degree 4): S_½ = 73/324 (§24's known answer); (K3) the empty subgraph: 1; (K4) a disjoint union of two stars on disjoint half-axes multiplies.

**The stakes, written before the run.**
- (E1) K1–K4 MET.
- (E2) THE PINCH STAR — two cycles of lengths a and b sharing one half-axis (one link of multiplicity 4 at v): S_½ = 24·2^{−(a+b)}, so e_v² := S_½(G_v)/2^{2 − d_v} = 6 and e_v = √6 = 2.449 (derived at the desk: the three matchings at the shared vertex read loops 2, 2, 1 — 16 + 16 + 4 = 36; every other vertex 1/4).
- (E3) CONSEQUENCE, a theorem once E2 is read: Theorem V's bound on a single pinched sphere is 6u^F against the exact 4u^F (the two ends of the pinched link each carry e = √6, and V − |X| = 0): the bound is NOT TIGHT at a contact, by the factor 3/2 per pinched link, so the Theorem-V road alone cannot close S12 — it cannot even reproduce the proved generic case — and S12's closing is the resolution-sum road of ΠΕΝΤΑΠΥΛΟΝ 18.6 (the fibre-leafless exchange sets), with this table as its LOCAL ALPHABET.
- (E4) THE MAXIMUM EXCESS over all even stars, and the orbit attaining it: UNGUESSED (the dense K_{2,2,2} reads e = 15.2; whether a denser star of K_{2,2,2,2} exceeds it is the table's to say).
- (E5) The number of orbits of even subgraphs under B₄: UNGUESSED (a few hundred expected).
- (E6) SPARSITY, the question the table serves: which even stars occur as the stars of Z₂-MINIMAL leaves of the lattice. Staked: every star of a leaf of the census to F = 22 (the contact leaves, 46,816 at F = 22) has every half-axis of degree at most 4 and e_v ≤ √6 — i.e. no census leaf carries a star denser than a single pinch at any vertex; a double contact at one vertex (two multiplicity-4 links at v) would refute it. UNGUESSED beyond the census: whether minimality FORBIDS a star with e_v > 2^{m_v − 1} for every resolution — the lemma S12's remainder needs, stated here as the object, not claimed.

**What the table cannot do, said once.** It bounds; it does not prove |Z| ≤ 4. The honest road to S12's remainder is 18.6's: Z(X) − 4 is the signed sum over the fibre-leafless exchange sets, zero on every lattice-like pinch read, 452/81 on an abstract K₄-junction complex that the lattice's local laws exclude; the lemma to prove is that a Z₂-minimal LATTICE leaf's leafless exchange sets sum to at most zero — a statement about which local configurations ℤ⁴ admits, which is why the star table comes first.

**Falsifiers.** E2's number (an instrument that reads the pinch star off 24·2^{−(a+b)} is wrong or the desk is); K1–K4; a census leaf with a vertex star of degree 6 or a double contact (E6 refuted, and the sparsity lemma's hypothesis narrowed by the example).

**§27 RESULTS 1 (2026-11-01 03:2x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat; the instruments `STDLIB/build/aperanton/surface-gas/even_stars.py`, `even_stars_table.py`, `leaf_stars.py`; the records `even_stars.table.2026-11-01a.txt` and `leaf_stars.2026-11-01a.txt`; copies in `records/surface-gas/`).**
- **E1 MET.** C_d = 2^{2−d} at d = 3..8; K_{2,2,2} = 73/324 (N = 10,512 over 729 matchings, m_max = 4); the empty graph 1; the disjoint union multiplies.
- **E2 REFUTED, the desk corrected (law 18).** The pinch star (two cycles sharing one half-axis) reads S_½ = 1/8 at |E| = 7, so e_v² = 4 and e_v = 2, not 24·2^{−(a+b)} and √6: at the shared half-axis only ONE of the three matchings separates the two cycles (loops 2), the other two join them (loops 1), so N = 16 + 4 + 4 = 24, not 36. m_max = 2.
- **E3 WITHDRAWN AS DERIVED, then found again one vertex up.** With e = 2 = 2^{m−1} at both ends of a pinched link, Theorem V's bound on a single pinched sphere is u^F·2^{V−|X|}·Π e_v = u^F·2^0·4 = 4u^F EXACTLY — Theorem V is TIGHT on the single pinch (the budget ratio ρ = e/2^{m_max−1} = 1). But at a vertex where TWO contact links meet (two half-axes of degree 4, the orbits deg = 40402220, 22402240, 42402220, 40402222 of the table) the star reads e = 2√2 with m_max = 2 — ρ = √2 — and such vertices exist on the census's two-contact leaves (E6 below), whose exact value is Z = 4 (ΠΕΝΤΑΠΥΛΟΝ 18.6: every two-contact leaf at F = 20 reads 4). So Theorem V is loose by √2 at every such vertex while the truth is the sheet value: THE THEOREM-V ROAD ALONE CANNOT CLOSE S12 — not for the dense end only, but already at a double contact that the census holds — and the closing road is the resolution sum's (18.6: Z − 4 is the signed sum over the fibre-leafless exchange sets), with this table as its local alphabet. The conclusion of E3 stands; its derivation is replaced by the instrument's.
- **E4 READ.** The maximal excess over all even stars is at the FULL K_{2,2,2,2} (all 24 edges, every half-axis of degree 6): S_½ = 581761/746496, N = 85,784,150,016 over 15⁸ = 2,562,890,625 matchings, e² = 2382893056/729, e = 1807.96, m_max = 8, ρ = 14.12 — the maximal ρ as well. K_{2,2,2} reads e = 15.2, m_max = 4, ρ = 1.9.
- **E5 READ.** 131,072 even subgraphs fall into 560 orbits under B₄; 90 of them are TAME (ρ ≤ 1): the cycles, the disjoint unions of cycles, and the single-contact stars (one half-axis of degree 4: e = 2, m_max = 2, ρ = 1, 12 orbits) — every star where the vertex splitting pays for the excess exactly.
- **E6 HELD at F = 18, REFUTED at F = 20.** The 576 one-contact leaves of F = 18 carry 5 star orbits (three cycle lengths, the single-contact star 1,152 times, a disjoint pair of cycles 576 times), every vertex at ρ = 1 — held. The 7,112 EVEN sets of the F = 20 listing (the two-contact profile 36/0/2) carry 10 orbits, the maximal half-axis degree 4 everywhere, and 9,916 of their 146,073 vertices (6.8 %) are DOUBLE-CONTACT vertices with ρ = √2 — the sparsity stake as written is FALSE: a Z₂-minimal leaf does carry stars beyond a single pinch at one vertex. What survives: no census leaf carries a half-axis of degree 6, nor a star with ρ > √2, nor any dense star (K_{2,2,2}'s class absent); whether minimality forbids degree 6 and ρ above √2 beyond F = 20 is UNGUESSED, and is the question the resolution-sum road asks of the lattice's local laws.
- **The number is unmoved.** Nothing here touches β* = 0.4764 / 0.4816; §25 (l) stands as written; the far shore §4.0's dense-end obstruction (73/324) stands, its derivation of the pinch's √6 corrected to 2 by this record.

**(m) THE NECK (2026-11-01 04:4x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat iii-43; found by the seat iii-73's read of the crossing campaign's W1 at 04:3x — an objection to a bond lemma that is also a reading of this gas's own spin accounting; entered the hour it was found, before the certificate is touched).** The dressing prices a lone wall with the leaf at spin ½ on BOTH sides of the wall's attachment cycle (S7, the factor 3a₁; S8's crossing law at spin 1), and the leaf sum prices the leaf at ONE spin (S2, the single higher spin in σ(F)). S2 is a theorem about a branch-free closed surface, every link of multiplicity two; a dressed leaf is not branch-free at the wall's attachment cycle, where the multiplicity is three, and there the balance is j ⊗ j′ ⊗ k ∋ 0, which admits j′ ≠ j: a wall whose attachment cycle SEPARATES the leaf admits the two sides at different spins — ½ and 3/2 with the wall at 1 (½ ⊗ 3/2 ⊗ 1 ∋ 0), or at 2. THE SMALLEST INSTANCE: a lone wall p′ whose four links all lie on the leaf has an attachment cycle ∂p′ bounding a disc of the leaf other than p′ itself, and the least such disc is the five-plaquette cup (the other five faces of a unit cube on p′); so the smaller side has at least five plaquettes, and the configuration's weight against the same leaf with the same wall at uniform ½ is at most of the order (2a_{3/2}/a_½)⁵ by §23 (i), Theorem V with mixed spins — about 10⁻¹¹ at β = 0.4764 (a_{3/2}/a_½ = I₄/I₂ ≈ 4.7·10⁻³ there), and smaller for every longer attachment cycle and every higher spin.
