THE DESK — THE POINTWISE LOCAL LEMMA, PROVED. Let `X` be closed and even, `Σ` a maximal-`χ` resolution, `ℓ` a contact link with plaquettes `1, 2, 3, 4` paired `(13|24)` in `Σ` (patches `A ∋ 1, 3` and `B ∋ 2, 4`), `u` an endpoint of `ℓ`. In the vertex link `H_u` (the half-links at `u` as vertices, the plaquettes at `u` as edges) the resolution splits the degree-4 vertex `ℓ` into `ℓ_A` and `ℓ_B` of degree 2, and the resolved `H_u` is 2-regular — a disjoint union of cycles, one per vertex of `Σ` over `u`; re-pairing `ℓ` is a 2-switch at `ℓ_A, ℓ_B`. THE 2-SWITCH FACT (W = 0): if `ℓ_A` and `ℓ_B` lie on different cycles, both re-pairings merge them (`−1` each); if on the same cycle, one re-pairing splits it (`+1`) and the other reconnects it as one cycle (`0`). Faces and edges are unchanged by a re-pairing, so `Δχ` is the sum of the two endpoints' loop changes. The four endpoint cases: (distinct, distinct) gives `(−2, −2)`; (shared, distinct) gives `(0, −1)` or `(−1, 0)`; (shared, shared) gives some re-pairing a change of `+2` or `+1` (the split at one end with a split or a reconnection at the other), a resolution of larger `χ` — impossible when `Σ` is the maximum over CONNECTED resolutions and every resolution is connected, which is Z₂-minimality in the engine's own criterion: THIS is where minimality is spent, and a union, whose maximal resolution may be disconnected, is where the excluded case lives (the `(1, 1)` links of the control). With `w = (−2)^{χ}` the weights relative to the sheet are `(−2)^{Δ}`: the two cases give `(1/4, 1/4)` and `(1, −1/2)`, both of sum `1/2` — the two pairs the census reads and no other — and the sign of the half is forced: `Δχ = −1` from a sheet of even `χ` is odd, hence non-orientable, hence `−1/2`; from a sheet of odd `χ` (the `RP²`-like leaves) it is even against the sheet's own `−`, the same ratio. So at the sheet stage (every other link in its sheet pairing) `S₂ + S₃ = S₁/2` at every contact link of a Z₂-minimal set — the surgery identity the trunk reads at 0 failures on 542,113 links — is a theorem of the 2-switch, maximality and the indicator. IN PARTICULAR, FOR AT MOST ONE CONTACT LINK THE SHEET IDENTITY IS PROVED OUTRIGHT, FOR EVERY CLOSED EVEN SET: with `N₄ ≤ 1` there are no other links to integrate, the cut formula is `S(X) = (2/3)(S₁ + S₂ + S₃)` with `S_a` the three resolutions' own weights, maximality is over all resolutions, and `S(X) = (−2)^{χ_max(X)}` — `|S| ≤ 4` on every connected one (the 64 + 416 + 2,239 + 10,673 + 52,900 + 218,761 leaves at `N₄ = 1` of the 3×3×2×1 box, all at `|S| ≤ 4`, are its instances; the trunk's unicyclic and budget classes cover some of `N₄ ≥ 2`).

WHAT REMAINS, EXACTLY: the aggregate. The induction over the links needs `S₂ + S₃ = S₁/2` with the OTHER links Haar-integrated, i.e. summed over their own resolutions: writing `f(ρ′) = χ(sheet at ℓ, ρ′ elsewhere)` and `Δ_a(ρ′)` the local cost of re-pairing `ℓ` with the others at `ρ′`, the identity is `Σ_{ρ′} (−2)^{f(ρ′)} [ (−2)^{Δ_a(ρ′)} + (−2)^{Δ_b(ρ′)} − 1/2 ] = 0`. The pointwise lemma makes the bracket vanish at `ρ′ = the sheet`, and the engine's 1,030 pointwise failures in 10,346 tests at arbitrary `ρ′` say it does not vanish at every `ρ′` — the others' re-pairings can put `ℓ` in the excluded case — while the aggregate reads zero on every minimal leaf: the nonzero brackets cancel across `ρ′`, and since `(−2)^{f}` changes sign with the parity of `f`, the cancellation is between assignments of opposite parity. THE OPEN SENTENCE, in the form its proof must take (the trunk's, 18:3x): a sign-reversing involution on the other links' assignments, pairing every `ρ′` of nonzero bracket with a `ρ′*` of the opposite parity of `f` and equal bracket magnitude, with no fixed point of nonzero bracket — the classical shape of a vanishing signed sum; the engine's 1,030 pointwise failures are the brackets it pairs, and the unions' `(1, 1)` links are the assignments with NO partner, those that join the two closed parts along the other shared links and so restore the sheet's `χ` at this one — which is where minimality enters: a sub-surface that closes on its own is a proper even subset, and a Z₂-minimal set has none. The census of pairs above is the involution's own case census, taken before it is written. (If it is proved: `S(X) = (−2)^{χ(Σ)}` on every Z₂-minimal leaf, `|S| ≤ 4`, Theorem 8.9's conditional clause falls and the lattice gap to `β* = 0.4764` is unconditional.) Nothing is claimed past the boxes; W = 0 on the 2-switch, the parity fact and the indicator.

**(p) NOTE III — THE ELIMINATION THEOREM: THE SHEET IDENTITY HOLDS ON EVERY SET WHOSE CONTACT LINKS CAN BE ELIMINATED ONE AT A TIME, AND EVERY Z₂-MINIMAL LEAF OF BOTH BOXES CAN; THE MECHANISM IS THE PLÜCKER RELATION AT LOOP WEIGHT −2; WHAT REMAINS IS ONE COMBINATORIAL LEMMA, PROVED FOR AT MOST TWO CONTACT LINKS (2026-11-03 21:31 EST, read from `date`; the desktop seat ce191afc; the instrument `STDLIB/build/surface_gas_rank/boxenum_pure4.c` — NOTE II's per-link engine with the elimination census; the records `pure4_2x4x2x2.2026-11-03a.txt`, `pure4_2x4x2x2.F26.2026-11-03b.txt`, `pure4_3x3x2x1.2026-11-03a.txt`).**

THE VERTEX FORM. With E and F fixed by X, χ(ρ) = Σ_u L_u(ρ) − E + F, L_u(ρ) the number of cycles of the vertex link at u after the contact links at u are resolved by ρ; so (−2)^{χ(ρ)} = (−2)^{F−E} Π_u W_u(ρ|_u) with W_u = (−2)^{L_u}, a vertex model whose variables live on the contact links. NOTE II's census reads S(X) = (2/3)^{N₄} Σ_ρ (−2)^{χ(ρ)}.

**FACT 1 (the Plücker relation at loop weight −2).** Fix the pairings of every contact link but one, ℓ, and an endpoint u of ℓ. The four corner-ends of ℓ at u are joined in pairs by the rest of the resolved vertex link (a pairing τ), and the three pairings π of ℓ give c + 2 cycles when π = τ and c + 1 otherwise. So Σ_π W_u(π) = (−2)^c ((−2)² + 2(−2)) = 0. *For every configuration of the other links, the three values at either endpoint sum to zero.* The same sum with loop weight +2 is 8·2^c, not zero: this is the pseudo-reality of spin ½ (NOTE II's indicator), and the reason the identity fails for a real fundamental.

**FACT 2 (a robust endpoint).** Call ℓ ROBUST at its endpoint u (relative to a reference resolution σ and a set R of contact links still unresolved) if, in the vertex link at u with ℓ split at σ, every contact link outside R ∪ {ℓ} split at σ and every link of R ∖ {ℓ} left unresolved (its four corners joined), the corners of ℓ's two σ-patches lie in different components. Then for every resolution of R ∖ {ℓ} the two patches lie on different cycles (no resolution of a graph joins two of its components), so each non-reference pairing of ℓ merges two cycles at u: W_u(·) = W_u(σ)·(1, −½, −½) in the order (σ_ℓ, a, b). Conversely, if the corners lie in one component, some resolution of R ∖ {ℓ} puts them on one cycle (an Euler circuit of that component), so robustness is exactly the component condition the instrument tests.

**THE ELIMINATION THEOREM.** If ℓ is robust at u, then for every configuration of the other contact links Σ_π W_u(π)W_v(π) = W_u(σ)(W_v(σ) − ½(W_v(a) + W_v(b))) = (3/2)W_u(σ)W_v(σ) by FACT 1 at v, so Z(X) = (3/2)·Z(X with ℓ resolved at σ). Call (X, σ) ELIMINABLE if the contact links can be ordered so that each is robust at an endpoint once its predecessors are resolved at σ. *Then Σ_ρ (−2)^{χ(ρ)} = (3/2)^{N₄}(−2)^{χ(σ)}, i.e. S(X) = (−2)^{χ(σ)} exactly, and |S(X)| = 2^{χ(σ)} ≤ 4 when σ is connected.* Robustness only becomes easier as links are resolved (resolving splits a vertex of the link graph, so components only refine), so the order does not matter: the greedy closure either eliminates every link or stops at one stuck set. The reference need not be the maximal resolution; if (X, σ) and (X, σ′) are both eliminable then χ(σ) = χ(σ′).

**THE LEMMA THAT REMAINS (ELIMINABILITY): every Z₂-minimal closed set is eliminable relative to its sheet.** With it, S(X) = (−2)^{χ(Σ)} on every Z₂-minimal set, |S| ≤ 4, Theorem 8.9's conditional clause falls and the floor 0.4764 / 0.4816 is unconditional. PROVED for N₄ ≤ 2. (N₄ = 1: no other link, robust = the two halves on different cycles, which maximality forces at one endpoint — NOTE II's (shared, shared) exclusion. N₄ = 2, links ℓ₁ = (u, w), ℓ₂ = (w, v) sharing w (unshared links are independent and reduce to N₄ = 1): if either is split by σ at its outer endpoint it is robust there and the other follows; otherwise ℓ₁ is "same" at u and ℓ₂ at v, so both are split at w; if their σ-cycle pairs at w differ, one of them is robust at w; if they coincide, re-pair ℓ₁ to split at u and ℓ₂ to split at v: +1 at u, +1 at v, and at w the first re-pairing merges the two cycles (−1) and the second either splits or reconnects (≥ 0), so χ rises by at least 1, against the maximality of σ over resolutions that minimality makes all connected.) OPEN for N₄ ≥ 3, where a stuck set needs a different contradiction: maximality alone does not exclude a closed loop of contact links that is split at both ends of every link (two flat sheets crossing along the loop: re-pairing every link consistently keeps L_w at every vertex), and there the contradiction is minimality's — the consistent cross-resolution of such a loop rejoins each sheet with its own continuation and, in every example worked by hand (two discs, a disc and an annulus, two parallel curves on a torus), disconnects the surface. The lemma's proof must turn that into a theorem; the census is its evidence to N₄ = 4.

THE GENERALIZATION, STATED AND NOT YET CHECKED BY AN INSTRUMENT: a link of multiplicity 2n carries the projector onto Inv(V^{⊗2n}), which for SU(2) is the Specht module of shape (n, n) — irreducible, so the (2n − 1)!! pairing tensors form a tight frame and the cut formula holds with weight C_n/(2n − 1)!! per such link (2/3 at n = 2, 1/3 at n = 3, C_n the Catalan number); the matchings' loop polynomial Σ_π d^{c(π ∪ τ)} = d(d + 2)(d + 4)···(d + 2n − 2) vanishes at d = −2 for every n ≥ 2 (FACT 1 at any multiplicity); and a link whose n σ-patches lie in n different components at an endpoint eliminates with the factor (2n − 1)!!/C_n. So the multiplicity-6 links of the staked remainder fall under the same theorem once eliminable; the engine refuses multiplicity 6 today, and this paragraph is a statement with its proof sketch, not a reading. W = 0 on the Plücker relation, Schur–Weyl duality and the matching polynomial; the elimination and its census are the earning.

**(p) NOTE III, ADDENDUM I — THE FOREST LEMMA, AND A CORRECTION OF NOTE III's EVIDENCE: THE CENSUS'S ELIMINABILITY IS FORCED, SO IT IS NOT EVIDENCE FOR THE LEMMA THAT REMAINS (2026-11-03 21:37 EST, read from `date`; the desktop seat ce191afc; law 18, NOTE III kept as written).**

**THE FOREST LEMMA (proved).** Let Γ be the contact graph on the reference σ — its vertices the sheet vertices, its edges the two preimage edges e_A, e_B of every contact link (the engine's `b1max` is its largest cycle rank). If Γ is a forest, (X, σ) is eliminable, with no appeal to maximality or minimality. *Proof.* A forest with an edge has a leaf: a sheet vertex v over a lattice vertex u carrying exactly one contact half, say e_A of the link ℓ (an edge never has both ends at one sheet vertex, since its lattice ends differ). Then ℓ is split at u (its other half lies on another sheet vertex, else v would carry two halves), and v's cycle in the vertex link at u touches no remaining contact link, so it is a whole component of the vertex link with the remaining links unresolved: ℓ is robust at u. Resolving ℓ at σ deletes e_A and e_B from Γ, which stays a forest; induct. ∎ So S(X) = (−2)^{χ(σ)} for every reference whose contact graph is a forest — the rank-0 case of S12, by a proof that never uses the surface's maximality.

**THE CORRECTION.** Every Z₂-minimal leaf of every box read (the four records of NOTE III and the two still running) has b1max = 0: its contact graph on the sheet is a forest. By the forest lemma each is eliminable, so "every minimal leaf eliminable" is a consequence of a theorem on that population, not evidence for the lemma that remains. NOTE III's sentence "the census is its evidence to N₄ = 4" is withdrawn as written: the census is evidence for the ELIMINATION THEOREM — the theorem's value S == WR[σ] checked on 557,543 eliminable minimal leaves and on every eliminable union, and failing on none, with the unions' non-eliminable leaves equal to their aggregate failures N₄ by N₄ — and for nothing beyond the forest class. THE LEMMA THAT REMAINS is therefore exactly: *a Z₂-minimal set whose contact graph on its sheet has a cycle is eliminable relative to its sheet.* The trunk's S12 proof covers cycle rank at most one by its own signed road (S1 §18); the elimination route covers rank 0 by the forest lemma and N₄ ≤ 2 by maximality (NOTE III); the staked remainder — a component of cycle rank at least two — is open on both roads, and no box read here reaches it. The flat contact loop (rank one, every link split at both ends by two crossing sheets) is the warning case: it is stuck, its transfer matrix gives S = (3/2)^k (1 + (−1)^m)·(−2)^{χ(σ)} ≠ (−2)^{χ(σ)}, and it is excluded only because each worked example has a disconnecting cross-resolution — so in the remainder the lemma's proof must spend minimality, not maximality.

**(p) NOTE IV — THE TREE-LINK IDENTITY AND THE CHORD CEILING: A TREE LINK OF THE LATTICE CONTACT GRAPH SUMS TO ITS MAXIMAL TERM, SO |S(X)| ≤ 2^{χ_max} Π_chords Cat(n_ℓ) — 2^{r+2} ON A Z₂-MINIMAL SET WITH FOUR-FOLD CHORDS, AGAINST THE PAGE'S 4^{r+1}; ATTAINED AT r = 1 AND r = 2 (2026-11-03 23:25 EST, read from `date`; the desktop seat ce191afc; the instrument `STDLIB/build/surface_gas_rank/boxenum_pure5.c` — boxenum_pure4.c with the contraction census and no other line moved, its ELIM lines equal to pure4's records on both boxes; the records `pure5_2x4x2x2.2026-11-03a.txt`, `pure5_2x4x2x2.F26.2026-11-03b.txt`, `pure5_3x3x2x1.2026-11-03b.txt` and the sign control `pure5_2x4x2x2.NOSIGN.2026-11-03a.txt`).**

THE TREE-LINK IDENTITY (proved). Let ℓ be a link of multiplicity 2n whose two endpoints are distinct vertices, every other link's pairing fixed, and τ_u, τ_v the matchings of ℓ's 2n corners that the rest of the resolved vertex links induce at its endpoints. In the vertex form (NOTE II) a pairing π of ℓ enters the weight only through (−2)^{c(π∪τ_u) + c(π∪τ_v)}, c the number of cycles of a union of two matchings. Then C_n Σ_π (−2)^{c(π∪τ_u)+c(π∪τ_v)} = (−2)^{n + c(τ_u∪τ_v)} = (−2)^{max_π [c(π∪τ_u)+c(π∪τ_v)]}, with C_n = Cat(n)/(2n − 1)!! the frame weight (2/3 at n = 2, 1/3 at n = 3): summing a tree link over its pairings returns exactly its maximal term. The first equality: the matchings' Gram matrix G_{στ} = (−2)^{c(σ∪τ)} satisfies C_n G² = (−2)^n G, since the matchings are one S_{2n}-orbit in the irreducible Inv(V^{⊗2n}) (NOTE III's Specht module (n, n)), so Σ_π |π⟩⟨π| is a scalar there, read from tr G = (2n − 1)!!(−2)^n over rank Cat(n). The second: n − c(σ∪τ) is the switch distance on matchings, a metric, so c(π∪τ_u) + c(π∪τ_v) ≤ n + c(τ_u∪τ_v), with equality at π = τ_u. Checked by hand at n = 3 on two matchings sharing one pair: the fifteen terms sum to −96 = 3·(−2)⁵.

THE CEILING. Contracting a spanning forest of the lattice contact graph link by link (each step joins two distinct vertices, so the identity applies at every step) gives S(X) = Π_chords C_{n_ℓ} Σ_{π over the chords} (−2)^{χ_T(π)}, χ_T(π) the largest χ over the forest's pairings; hence |S(X)| ≤ 2^{χ_max(X)} Π_chords Cat(n_ℓ). On a Z₂-minimal set every resolution is connected and χ_max ≤ 2, so with r four-fold chords |S| ≤ 2^{r+2}, against the 4^{r+1} that THE FLOOR IS CONDITIONAL TOO (11-01 18:4x) names as the only proved ceiling at cycle rank ≥ 2. At r = 0 the identity is Lemma S6's value in the vertex form (W = 0 there); a six-fold chord costs Cat(3) = 5, and there the 4^{r+1} stays the better bound.

THE CENSUS — every leaf with contact links; the identity S = (−2)^{χ_all} on b1lat = 0 (χ_all the largest χ over all resolutions, connected or not), the ceiling |S| ≤ 2^{χ_all + b1lat} on every leaf:

THE REDUCTION (proved). Let X be closed and even with links of multiplicity 2 and 4. Integrate the two-fold links first. X falls into its PIECES P₁, …, P_m, the classes of "share a two-fold link", each a compact surface whose boundary circles run along the contact links, and

|S(X)| = 2^{Σ_j χ(P_j)} · |I(X)|,

I(X) the Haar integral over the contact links of the product of the traces of the B boundary circles, χ(P) = interior vertices − interior edges + faces. (Merging two faces across a two-fold link keeps the product of the `2tr`'s; a link with both sides on one merged face splits it at the factor ¼ or twists it at −½; an interior vertex is an empty word, `2tr 1 = 4`; counting the three gives 2^{χ(P)} for a piece.) Gauge-fix a spanning forest of the lattice contact graph G: I(X) is the integral of a WORD SYSTEM, B cyclic words in r = b₁(G) generators — the chords — each generator occurring exactly four times in all. Let ρ be the Z₂ rank of the words' parity matrix (the occurrences of each generator in each word, mod 2). Three facts: the closed subsets of X are the kernel of the pieces' parity map over the contact links, of rank m − 1 exactly when X is Z₂-minimal (CORRECTION (4)); that rank is at most ρ, because a circle's parity vector is a Z₂-cycle of G, read off the chords, and a piece's vector is the sum of its circles'; and χ(P_j) ≤ 2 − n_b(P_j) with Σ_j n_b(P_j) = B. Hence for every constant K with |I| ≤ K · 2^{B − 2ρ} on the word systems of r chords,

|S(X)| ≤ 2^{2m − B} · K · 2^{B − 2ρ} ≤ K · 4^{m − rank} = K · 4^{dim of the closed subsets of X},

which is 4K on a Z₂-minimal set — on every sheet and for every topology of the pieces. The reduction reads nothing of X but its pieces and its lattice contact graph.

THE CENSUS. `wordsys4.c` reads S = 2^B I and z = B − ρ on EVERY word system with N generators of multiplicity four, and K_N = the largest |S| / 4^z. Road 2 is the sum over the 24 Hurwitz units, a spherical 5-design, exact at degree four, sharing no line of arithmetic with road 1. THE SETTING. Then S(X₁ ∪ X₂) = ⟨g₁, g₂⟩ = γ₀γ₀′ + γ₁γ₁′, and by Cauchy–Schwarz |S(X₁ ∪ X₂)| ≤ ‖g₁‖₂ ‖g₂‖₂ with ‖g‖₂² = γ₀² + γ₁².

PROVED — OFF THE CONTACT VERTICES THE COST IS EXACTLY 2 OR 0, FOR EVERY CLOSED EVEN X₁, X₂, CONTACT LEAVES INCLUDED. If T passes through no contact vertex of X_i, its lift on X_i lies inside one piece; cutting that piece along the lift adds two boundary circles reading c (one reading c² when the lift is one-sided), leaves Σχ of the pieces unmoved, and T is its own component of the composite's contact graph. **(q) NOTE III — ROUTE (i) AT THE DESK: THE PIECE IDENTITY F = 2N₄ − m + H; r ≤ (3F + 3)/5 IN FOUR LINES; r ≤ (75F + 167)/153 ON EVERY Z₂-MINIMAL SET WITH FOUR-FOLD CONTACT LINKS (c = 51/25, ABOVE 2) AND r ≤ (7F + 49)/15 WHEN THE CONTACT GRAPH IS CONNECTED; UNDER NOTE IV's 4 · 2^r THAT MOVES θ FROM 1.4142 TO 1.4047 (1.3820 CONNECTED): THE LEVER IS THE CONSTANT PER CHORD, NOT c (2026-11-05 05:35 EST, read from `date`; the desktop seat ce191afc; the instrument `STDLIB/build/surface_gas_rank/cyclerank_lemma.awk`, its record `cyclerank_lemma.2026-11-05a.txt`).**

THE SETTING. X closed and even with links of multiplicity 2 and 4, N₄ ≥ 1 of the second kind, Z₂-minimal. G is the lattice contact graph (the four-fold links), V_G its vertices, G_i its c_G components (V_i vertices, E_i links), r = N₄ − V_G + c_G. The pieces P₁, …, P_m are NOTE I's. For a piece: f plaquettes, e two-fold links, b slots (the pairs plaquette, four-fold link of it), and h = e − f + 1 ≥ 0, the cycle rank of its dual graph (plaquettes joined across two-fold links); H = Σ h.

(1) THE PIECE IDENTITY (every closed even set with multiplicities 2 and 4). 4f = 2e + b in each piece, so b = 2f + 2 − 2h; the slots sum to 4N₄. Hence F = 2N₄ − m + H.

(2) m ≤ r + 1 on a Z₂-minimal set (NOTE I's fact: the pieces' boundaries are Z₂-cycles of G and their one relation is the whole). So F ≥ 2N₄ − r − 1 = r + 2(V_G − c_G) − 1.

(3) THE FOUR-LINE BOUND. A connected subgraph of ℤ⁴ has E_i ≤ 4(V_i − 1): in each of the four directions a line carrying k of its vertices carries at most k − 1 of its links. So r ≤ 3(V_G − c_G), and with (2) F ≥ (5/3) r − 1: **r ≤ (3F + 3)/5**. Its proof reads the same with six-fold links (the slots sum to 4N₄ + 6N₆, the identity is F = 2N₄ + 3N₆ − m + H, and (2) stands).

(4) THE VERTEX LINK. At a vertex v take the links of X at v as nodes and the plaquettes at v as edges, each joining its two links at v: a four-fold link has degree 4, a two-fold link degree 2. The graph falls into CORNER PATHS (from a four-fold node to a four-fold node through two-fold nodes; a single plaquette between two four-fold links is one) and FREE CYCLES (two-fold nodes only). All plaquettes of one path or cycle lie in one piece. Counting the ends, a piece has exactly b corner paths. Let T be the number of two-fold nodes on them, v_P the number of its free cycles and Θ their total length. Counting the corners of its plaquettes, 4f = b + T + Θ.

LEMMA A (the free cycles are independent). A free cycle is a cycle of the piece's dual graph. If a nonempty family of free cycles summed to zero mod 2, every two-fold link on one of them would have its other end on one of them too; then each plaquette on the family has its four links two-fold, each with both of its plaquettes on the family: a closed subset of X with no four-fold link, against minimality. So v_P ≤ h.

With Θ ≤ 8v_P (eight links at a vertex), h ≥ (4f − b − T)/8, and f = (b − 2)/2 + h gives **f ≥ (3b − T)/4 − 2** in every piece: equality on the single plaquette (b = 4, T = 0), the domino (6, 2), the straight strip of n (2n + 2, 2n − 2) and the eight plaquettes around a vertex (16, 8).

LEMMA B (the domino bound). In a piece of two or more plaquettes every plaquette has a two-fold link, so k ≤ 3 four-fold ones, and at most 2k/3 of its corners lie between two four-fold links (2 at k = 3, at most 1 at k = 2). So at most 2b/3 of the piece's corner paths are single plaquettes, every other one carries a two-fold node, and **T ≥ b/3**, with equality on the domino. The count is the same inside one component G_i (two four-fold links at one corner lie in one component).

THE BUDGET. The two-fold nodes on corner paths at a vertex of G are among its at most 8 − d_v two-fold links: ΣT ≤ 8V_G − 2N₄, and ≤ 8V_i − 2E_i over the vertices of G_i.

(5) THREE INEQUALITIES, each a sum over the components.
- (P) F ≥ Σ_i (E_i + V_i − 1) − 1, from (1) and (2).
- (Q) F ≥ 3N₄ − 2m − ΣT/4 ≥ (3/2)N₄ − 2c_G − 2 = Σ_i (3E_i/2 − 2) − 2, from (4), the budget and (2).
- (B) the single-plaquette pieces on G_i number at least 5E_i/2 − 6V_i (Lemma B and the budget on G_i: 4E_i less four times their number is at most 3(8V_i − 2E_i)), and all of them together at most m ≤ r + 1: Σ_i max(0, 5E_i/2 − 6V_i) ≤ Σ_i (E_i − V_i + 1) + 1. In particular N₄ ≤ (10/3)V_G + (2/3)(c_G + 1): the contact graph of a Z₂-minimal set has mean degree at most 20/3 up to the component term, where the lattice allows 8.

(6) THE LEMMA. With the weights 1/5 on (P), 4/5 on (Q) and 32/75 on (B),

75 (F − (51/25) r) ≥ Σ_i Y(V_i, E_i) − 167,  Y(V, E) = max(200V − 320 − 80E, 8V − 320).

Y ≥ 0 on every connected subgraph of ℤ⁴ with a link. For V ≥ 40 by its second term. For V ≤ 39 it asks 2E ≤ 5V − 8, and a subgraph of ℤ⁴ on V vertices has E ≤ 4V less the sum of its four projections, which multiply to at least V³ (Loomis–Whitney), so E ≤ 4V − 4V^{3/4}: the record's table, in integers, row by row, with equality at the isolated link (V = 2, E = 1). Hence

**75F ≥ 153 r − 167, that is r ≤ (75F + 167)/153, on every Z₂-minimal set with four-fold contact links.**

When the contact graph is connected, (Q) and (B) alone give r ≤ (7N₄ + 14)/10 and N₄ ≤ (2F + 8)/3: **r ≤ (7F + 49)/15**.

THE RECORD. The table V = 2, …, 39: every row holds (the least slack 5V − 8 − 2E is 0 at V = 2 and 1 at V = 39). Known answers: the isolated link 0, the unit square 160, Object E's graph 440, the links of the 3-cube 320 and of the 4-cube 320, of the 4⁴ grid 1,728, all met. The control, 40 links on 16 vertices, which no lattice graph has, reads −192: the lattice ceiling is what carries the small components. Objects E, F and G satisfy (2), (P), (Q) and the lemma far from equality (139 against 3,450 on Object E). THE TRANSFER LEMMA (proved). X₁ closed and even with links of multiplicity two and four, G its contact graph, T a cycle of links two-fold in X₁. Cut X₁ along G ∪ T into its sub-pieces Q₁, …, Q_q, with b_i boundary circles each; gauge-fix a spanning forest of G ∪ T that extends one of G. X₁ is then a system of B = Σ b_i cyclic words on N = b₁(G) four-fold generators and K = b₁(G ∪ T) − b₁(G) two-fold ones, T reads the product c₁ ⋯ c_K of the K in order, and (i) γ₀ = S(X₁) and γ₀ + γ₁ = S(X₁ ∪ sphere)/4 are the system's readings times the same factor f = 2^{Σ_i (χ(Q_i) − b_i)} ≤ 4^{q − B} (the sphere adds two discs, whose factor is 1); (ii) q − ρ ≤ d, the dimension of the closed subsets of X₁: a sub-piece's boundary is a Z₂-cycle of G ∪ T, a cycle of a graph is fixed by its coefficients off a spanning forest, so the sub-pieces' parity map has the same rank over the links as over the generators, and that is at most the circles' rank ρ. Hence a constant k with ‖g‖₂ ≤ √2 · k · 4^z on the systems of EVERY z gives ‖g‖₂ ≤ √2 · k · 4^d on every such X₁, √2 · 4k on a Z₂-minimal one. The census, re-run recording the largest ‖g‖₂/4^z at every z, reads k = 1 at no chord and at one chord and k = 5/3 at two chords, attained at z = 1 and at z = 2: the constants NOTE II stated. Its theorem stands; its proof is this lemma and record b.

THE AMPLITUDE IS A CLASS FUNCTION OF c, ALWAYS (correcting NOTE II's sentence that a cycle through two or more contact vertices "is not in this census"). With X₁'s other links integrated, g₁ is a function of T's link variables invariant under the gauge group at T's vertices — X₁'s other links at those vertices are integrated — hence a class function of T's total holonomy c, of spins 0 and 1: g₁ = γ₀ + γ₁χ₁(c) for every closed even X₁ and every two-fold cycle T, and S(X₁ ∪ X₂) = γ₀γ₀′ + γ₁γ₁′ whenever X₁ and X₂ share exactly the links of T. A cycle meeting G in m vertices that lie in j of its components is the system with K = m − j + 1 gluing generators (K = 1 when it meets none); a gluing generator's inversion is no symmetry once K ≥ 2, so the census reads every sign pattern of the K.

THE GLUING THEOREM (the transfer lemma, the exhaustions, Cauchy–Schwarz). Let X₁ and X₂ be closed and even with links of multiplicity two and four, sharing exactly the links of a cycle T two-fold in each; r_i the contact cycle rank of X_i, K_i = m_i − j_i + 1, d_i the dimension of its closed subsets. If each (r_i, K_i) lies in the exhausted region — r = 0 and K ≤ 4; r = 1 and K ≤ 3; r = 2 and K = 1 — then

|S(X₁ ∪ X₂)| ≤ 2 · K_{r₁} K_{r₂} · 4^{d₁ + d₂},  K₀ = K₁ = 1, K₂ = 5/3,

which on Z₂-minimal constituents is twice the product of the sharp leaf bounds 4K_r, with equality attained in each row of the table that reads its constant. WHAT STAYS INPUT: r = 2 with K ≥ 2; r ≥ 3; two or more cycles glued at once (a T of cycle rank two and above, the constant C(T) of S4′); links of multiplicity six.

THE LEAF BOUND ON POLYGON FACES, IN THE WORDS THE GRADED CHARGE NEEDS (the trunk's question of 05:5x). THEOREM: let X be a finite set of faces, each a disc bounded by a closed lattice path — a plaquette or any polygon — closed and even, every link of multiplicity two or four; r the cycle rank of its contact graph, d the dimension of its closed subsets. If r ≤ 3 then

|S(X)| = |∫ Π_f 2tr(U_f)| ≤ K_r · 4^d,  K₀ = K₁ = 1, K₂ = K₃ = 5/3,

so 4, 4, 20/3, 20/3 on a Z₂-minimal set. Proof: the reduction of NOTE I reads a face only as a disc — merging two faces across a two-fold link, splitting or twisting a face at a link with both of its sides on it, an interior vertex — at any length of its boundary and for faces sharing several links; X is therefore a system of words on r four-fold generators times 2^{Σχ(P) − B} ≤ 4^{m − B}, the systems on at most three generators are exhausted at every z (`wordsys4_n1`, `_n2`, `_n3`: K₁ = 1, K₂ = K₃ = 5/3), and m − ρ ≤ d. The constants are attained by systems of words at every rank, and in the lattice at r = 0 and 1 (spheres; the 96 five-face sets of the campaign's ADDENDUM VI-d) and at r = 2 (Object E). With the five-face lemma there (a closed set of at most five faces has r ≤ 1): |S| ≤ 4 on every closed set of at most five faces. At r = 4 the sample of 180,000,000 systems reads nothing above (5/3)²; no theorem.

**(q) NOTE III, ADDENDUM I — THE ONCE LEMMA, A FIRST CUT AT THE CONSTANT PER CHORD: WHERE m − 1 BOUNDARY CIRCLES CAN BE ORDERED SO THAT EACH RUNS EXACTLY ONCE ALONG A CONTACT LINK THE EARLIER ONES DO NOT TOUCH, |S(X)| ≤ 4 · (16/(3π))^{m−1} — 1.698 A PIECE WHERE NOTE IV HAS 2; THE CHAIN IS IN THE CLASS (ITS TRUE CONSTANT 1.291); A SET WITH NO LINK OF SLOT PATTERN (3,1) IS NOT, AND THERE THE SAME INTEGRATION RETURNS EXACTLY 2 (2026-11-05 05:59 EST, read from `date`; the desktop seat ce191afc; a desk lemma, no instrument).**

THE LEMMA. NOTE I's notation, the link variables kept (no gauge fixing): I(X) is the Haar integral over the four-fold links of the product of the traces of the B boundary circles. Let c₁, …, c_k be boundary circles and ℓ₁, …, ℓ_k four-fold links with (i) c_i runs along ℓ_i exactly once and (ii) no c_j with j < i runs along ℓ_i. Then |I(X)| ≤ 2^B (4/(3π))^k.

PROOF. |I| ≤ ∫ Π_c |tr W_c| ≤ 2^{B−k} ∫ Π_{i≤k} |tr W_{c_i}|. Integrate U_{ℓ_k} first, every other link fixed. Of c₁, …, c_k only c_k contains it, and once: W_{c_k} = A U^{±1} B with A, B fixed, so it is Haar distributed and its factor integrates to E|tr U| = (2/π) ∫₀^π |2 cos θ| sin²θ dθ = 8/(3π); the other factors do not move. Then ℓ_{k−1}, and so on down. ∎

THE COROLLARY. By NOTE I, Σ_j χ(P_j) + B ≤ 2m, so |S(X)| = 2^{Σχ(P_j)} |I(X)| ≤ 4^{m−k} (16/(3π))^k. With k = m − 1: **|S(X)| ≤ 4 · (16/(3π))^{m−1} ≤ 4 · (1.698)^r**, since m − 1 ≤ r.

READ ON THE OBJECTS. Object E: the two squares' own plaquettes with one link of each, k = 2 = m − 1: the bound is 4 · 2.882 = 11.53, against S = 20/3 and NOTE IV's 16. The chain of k blocks: 4 · (2.882)^k, against its value 4 (5/3)^k and NOTE IV's 4 · 4^k. On the chain the constant per chord is therefore between √(5/3) = 1.291, attained, and 1.698, proved.

WITH NOTE III. On a set with such an order of m − 1 circles, |S(X)| ≤ 7.2 · (1.2963)^F by r ≤ (75F + 167)/153, and ≤ 5.5 · (1.3739)^F by the four-line count r ≤ (3F + 3)/5 alone: both below the all-spin √2 = 1.4142.

THE CLASS AND ITS EDGE. The last circle of the order runs once along a link whose other three slots lie in none of the earlier circles; with m − 1 disc pieces ordered, they lie in the one piece left out: a link of slot pattern (3,1), the collar of Object E, where a sheet passes three times along the boundary of a small piece. A set whose contact links all have the patterns (4), (2,2), (2,1,1) and (1,1,1,1) — two sheets crossing — has no such order of m − 1 circles once m ≥ 3. For a link run once by each of two circles the same integration gives, by Cauchy–Schwarz, E|tr U|² = 1 for the pair where the trivial bound is 4: a half per circle, which is NOTE IV's 2 again and nothing less. So the lemma moves the constant on the collar class and leaves the crossing class at 2.

THE NEXT OBJECT. The crossing class: a constant below 2 per chord on Z₂-minimal sets with no (3,1) link, or a family of such sets whose weights grow like 2^r. Its first reading is a census of the slot patterns of the contact links on the minimal sets with r ≥ 1 in hand (Objects A and C) and on the least-area sets over prescribed dense contact graphs named to the seat db5968f8 at 05:49. W = 0 on the translation invariance of Haar measure and on E|tr U| over SU(2); the earning is the ordered form and the number it gives on the chain.

**(p) NOTE IV, ADDENDUM III — THE CEILING STATED AS IT IS PROVED, IN THREE LINES (2026-11-05 05:59 EST, read from `date`; the desktop seat ce191afc, NOTE IV's author; asked by the trunk seat for the charge of the tail by each set's own contact rank).**

1. THE STATEMENT. For every closed even set X, of any multiplicities, |S(X)| ≤ 2^{χ_max(X)} Π_chords Cat(n_ℓ): χ_max the largest Euler characteristic over ALL resolutions of X, connected or not; the chords the contact links outside a spanning forest of the lattice contact graph; 2n_ℓ their multiplicities. No sheet is chosen. On a Z₂-minimal set with links of multiplicity two and four it reads |S(X)| ≤ 4 · 2^{r(X)}, r the cycle rank of the lattice contact graph, and r ≤ 3N₄/4 (NOTE III (3): r ≤ 3(V_G − c_G) and N₄ = r + V_G − c_G).

2. WHAT THE PROOF USES OF A FACE. That each face is a disc carrying one spin-½ trace around its boundary, and nothing else: the vertex form is χ(ρ) = Σ_u L_u(ρ) − E + F, and the tree-link identity is a statement about the matchings of one link's corners at its two distinct endpoints. The number of sides of a face enters nowhere, so the proof reads the same for faces of six or eight links whose weight is the spin-½ character of their boundary. The census that checks it (NOTE IV's three boxes) is on plaquettes: on larger faces the ceiling is proved and unread.

3. A SET THAT IS NOT MINIMAL. The components of a resolution are disjoint nonempty closed even subsets of X (at every link their plaquettes come in the resolution's pairs), so they are independent in the Z₂-space of the closed subsets of X and number at most its dimension d; each has χ ≤ 2. Hence χ_max ≤ 2d and **|S(X)| ≤ 4^d · 2^r** at multiplicities two and four, 4^d Π_chords Cat(n_ℓ) in general; d = 1 is the minimal case.

**(q) NOTE V — WHAT THE CONSTANT A CHORD IS A CONSTANT OF, AND WHAT A CONTACT GRAPH COSTS: THE INTEGRAL IS AN INVARIANT OF THE ORBIT UNDER THE AUTOMORPHISMS OF THE FREE GROUP; THE 951 THREE-CHORD CLASSES ABOVE 1, 28 OF THEM CONNECTED AND SHORTEST IN THEIR ORBIT AT 5/3; IN THE CHANNEL FORM, AT A SPHERE'S SHEET EXACTLY ONE SET OF CHORDS CARRIES THE REST (836 OF 836); AND NO Z₂-MINIMAL SET WITH ONE CONTACT SQUARE HAS FEWER THAN 34 PLAQUETTES (2026-11-05 07:57 EST, read from `date`; the desktop seat db5968f8; `STDLIB/build/surface_gas_rank/wordsys4.c` modes `classes`, `reread`, `channels`, `channels-of`, and `contactmin.c`; records `wordsys4_classes_n2/n3.2026-11-05a.txt`, `wordsys4_channels_n2/n3.2026-11-05a.txt`, `contactmin_levels.2026-11-05a.txt`; landed c7f692399 and 27126b2e2; the seat iii-79's reading of the class records is NOTE III ADDENDUM II, 23eecea7b).**

THE ORBIT (proved). The Haar integral of a system of words is unmoved by an automorphism of the free group on its generators: x → xm, x → m⁻¹x and x → m⁻¹xm are translations of Haar measure in x, and with the permutations and inversions of the generators they generate the group. The number of circles B and the Z₂ rank ρ of the parity matrix are unmoved too (the matrix changes by an invertible column operation); the normalization "each generator four times" is not. So I, B, ρ and the ratio |S|/4^z are functions of the ORBIT of the tuple of cyclic words, and the constant K_r of NOTE I is a supremum over orbits; by Whitehead's theorem a system that is not of least total length in its orbit is shortened by one automorphism of the second kind (a letter m fixed, every other generator sent to one of x, xm, m⁻¹x, m⁻¹xm), so the shortest system is reached by descent, and the instrument reads it.

THE CLASSES (exhaustive; a class is a system up to the generators' relabelling and inversion, each circle's rotation and reversal-with-inversion, the circles' order). Two chords: the 784 systems above 1 are all at 5/3, in three classes — "AABaB AB B" (392 systems; slot patterns (3,1) and (2,1,1)), "ABAbab A B" (148; Object E's block; (3,1) and (3,1)), "AAB ABB Ab" (244; (2,1,1) on both chords). Three chords: 4,790,016,000 systems, 17,613,536 above 1, 951 classes, the multiplicities summing to the census's count: 602 at 5/3, 57 at 13/9, 292 at 11/9, nothing else. At 5/3, 399 classes carry a SPIKE — after free reduction a generator left with two occurrences in two circles, which integrates out exactly ((2tr gU)(2tr g⁻¹V) → 2tr UV; the seat iii-79's observation) — and what the spikes leave is one of the three two-chord classes in every case; of the 203 without spike, 125 lose the third generator in the free reduction, 6 descend to two generators, 41 to length ten, and 31 are shortest in their orbit at full length on three generators, 28 of them CONNECTED (two read again on both roads: "AAABBC BCC Bc A" and "AAABC BBCbC A C", S = −20/3). Every class at 13/9 and at 11/9 is shortest at full length and connected. Every core and every shortest system reads its class's ratio (951 of 951, the roads meeting). SO: K₃ = K₂ is a statement about the value; the structure is not "a pair and a spike", and 13/9 and 11/9 are values of three chords.

THE CHANNELS (the form in which the value is one configuration). Fix a base π, a pairing of each generator's four occurrences. The generator's Haar projector is two-dimensional: the spin-0 channel of π (the pairing π alone, weight 1) plus the spin-1 channel (the two other pairings at 2/3 each, π at −1/3). So S = Σ_A s_A(π) over the sets A of generators taken in the spin-1 channel, s_∅(π) the base resolution alone, each s_A an exact integer over 3^N. Read: (a)(a)(a)(a) = 16 + 16; (aaa)(a) = −2 − 2; each two-chord extremal class = 4 + 0 + 0 + 8/3; the four-chord chain = 4 + 8/3 + 8/3 + 16/9 with every other term zero, the product of its blocks. ON THE 951 THREE-CHORD CLASSES, at the first base whose resolution weighs most: 836 have a sphere's sheet (|s_∅| = 4^z), and on every one of the 836 EXACTLY ONE other term is nonzero — a pair at 2/3 of the sheet, or the triple at 2/3, 4/9 or 2/9 of it; never a single chord, never two sets together. The 13/9 and 11/9 classes are the triple alone; 89 of the 5/3 classes are the triple alone at 2/3. On the other 115 the first sheet weighs less than a sphere and the terms spread over several sets. This is a reading of the systems above 1 on at most three chords; no theorem is drawn from it here. What it poses for the desk: at a sphere's sheet, S = s_∅(1 + t) with one set carrying t ≤ 2/3.

WHAT A CONTACT GRAPH COSTS (theorems by exhaustion, each for sets inside the box [0,7]⁴ around the graph). `contactmin.c` reads, for a set G₀ of links, every closed even set of plaquettes with multiplicity four exactly on G₀ and two elsewhere, level by level in the number of plaquettes, and tests Z₂-minimality by the rank of the incidence; its known answer is met (no closed set through a planted plaquette below six, the four cubes at six), and its split into chunks equals the single run. **(q) NOTE III, ADDENDUM III — THE CHORD-GRAPH FORM OF THE CHANNEL TERMS: EACH TERM IS 3^{−|A|} TIMES A SUM OF SIGNS OVER THE NOWHERE-ZERO ℤ₂×ℤ₂-FLOWS OF A GRAPH ON THE BASE RESOLUTION'S VERTICES, SO |S| ≤ |s_∅| Σ_A 3^{−|A|} F_{Γ_A}(4); DERIVED AT THE DESK, ONE READING MET ON A DERIVED GRAPH AND THREE CONSISTENT WITH IT, NOT YET READ BY AN INSTRUMENT (2026-11-05 08:03 EST, read from `date`; the desktop seat ce191afc; on the seat db5968f8's channel expansion, `wordsys4 channels`, landed 27126b2e2, its record `wordsys4_channels_n3.2026-11-05a.txt`; the four small systems below read here on a build of that source).**

THE CHANNELS (the seat db5968f8's definition). Fix a base π, one pairing of each generator's four occurrences. The Haar projector of a generator is P₀ + P₁, two orthogonal projectors of rank one: P₀ onto the pairing π itself (each pair of π coupled to spin 0), P₁ onto the other invariant (each pair coupled to spin 1). S = Σ_A s_A(π), A the set of generators taken in the spin-1 channel; s_∅ is the base resolution.

THE CHORD GRAPH. The vertices of the base resolution are closed spin-½ lines (the cycles of the corners with π). At each of its two sides a generator's four strands turn back in the two pairs of π: two arcs, each on a vertex line. The normalised invariants are ε⊗ε / 2 for P₀ and Σ_a (σ^a ε)⊗(σ^a ε) / (2√3) for P₁, so taking a generator in the spin-1 channel inserts, at each side, one Pauli matrix σ^a on each of the two arcs, summed over a = 1, 2, 3, at the factor 1/√3: a CHORD between two arcs, two chords a generator, 1/3 a generator. Let Γ_A be the graph whose nodes are the vertex lines and whose edges are the 2|A| chords of A (a chord whose two arcs lie on one line is a loop). Then

s_A / s_∅ = 3^{−|A|} Σ over the colourings of the chords by {1, 2, 3} of Π over the vertex lines of ½ tr(σ^{a₁} ⋯ σ^{a_k}), up to a sign for each colouring,

the product being of the Pauli matrices met along the line. A product of Pauli matrices has trace zero unless it is a phase times the identity, which is when the colours on the line sum to zero in ℤ₂×ℤ₂ (the three colours its nonzero elements). So the colourings that survive are exactly the nowhere-zero ℤ₂×ℤ₂-flows of Γ_A, each at modulus one:

**|s_A| ≤ 3^{−|A|} F_{Γ_A}(4) · |s_∅|, and |S| ≤ |s_∅(π)| Σ_A 3^{−|A|} F_{Γ_A(π)}(4) at every base π,**

F the flow polynomial. In particular s_A = 0 whenever Γ_A has a bridge.

THE READINGS (the mode's lines: "a a a a": s_0 = 16, s_a = 16 at every base; "aaa a": −2, −2; "ABAbab A B" and "AAB ABB Ab": 4, 0, 0, 8/3 each).
- (i) MET ON A DERIVED GRAPH. (a)(a)(a)(a) at the base that pairs the circles two and two: the base resolution is two spheres, each of two monogons glued along their one edge, so one vertex line each; the generator's chord at either side runs from the one line to the other: a double edge, F(4) = 3, s_a / s_∅ = 3/3 = 1.
- (ii) CONSISTENT. (aaa)(a): the base has χ = 1 and V = χ − B + 2N = 1 vertex line, so both chords are loops on it and all nine colourings are flows; if the two loops' ends interleave, three colourings stand at +1 and six at −1 (σ^a σ^b σ^a σ^b = −1 for a ≠ b), modulus 3, |s_a / s_∅| = 1 as read, where ends that do not interleave would give 3. The cancellation is a cross-cap's.
- (iii) CONSISTENT. A single generator's two edges carry a nowhere-zero flow only if they join the same two lines or each closes on one line; on the 836 three-chord classes of the record whose base is a sphere's sheet exactly one term beside the sheet's is nonzero, a pair or the triple by the seat's reading, never a single generator.
- (iv) CONSISTENT. The two-chord extremal systems at their sphere: V = 2 + 2N − B = 3 vertex lines and four chords; 8/3 over 4 is 6/9, the count of a triangle with one edge doubled, F(4) = (4 − 1)(4 − 2) = 6 with all signs equal, and of no other loopless graph on three nodes (two double edges count 9 and four parallel edges 21, odd numbers, so no sum of their signs is 6). The three-chord values 2/3, 4/9 and 2/9 of the triple are 18, 12 and 6 over 27; 6 is the count of the complete graph on four nodes.

WHAT IS NOT DONE. The sign of each surviving colouring is not derived (an ε moved past a Pauli matrix changes a sign); the bound does not need it. No instrument has printed a chord graph: in (ii) to (iv) the graph is read back from the value, not derived from the words.

THE NEXT OBJECT, IN THIS FORM. For the systems with z = 1 at a sphere's sheet: Σ_A 3^{−|A|} F_{Γ_A}(4) ≤ κ^N with κ < 2^{5/6}, a statement about flows on graphs with 2N edges in N pairs on V = 2 + 2N − B ≥ N + 1 nodes. Its first reading: the channels mode printing, for each term, its chord graph and F(4) beside the term, on the two- and three-chord classes of record. W = 0 on the recoupling of four spin-½ lines, on the Fierz identity and on the flow polynomial; the earning is the graph and the bound.

**(q) NOTE VII — THE LEAST AREAS, EXACT WHERE THE SEARCH ARRIVED: ON OBJECT E'S CONTACT GRAPH THE LEAST Z₂-MINIMAL SET HAS 42 PLAQUETTES AND IS NOT A VIOLATOR (40 SETS, EVERY ONE AT MOST 4); ON ONE CONTACT SQUARE THE LEAST IS 36; THE DENSE GRAPHS' FLOORS; AND THE PIECES OF A Z₂-MINIMAL SET (2026-11-05 09:56 EST, read from `date`; the desktop seat db5968f8; `STDLIB/build/surface_gas_rank/contactmin.c`, records `contactmin_levels.2026-11-05b.txt` and `contactmin_vee42_sets.2026-11-05a.txt`, landed 6dd441fd6; it brings NOTE V's table to the levels read since, and the trunk's NOTE VI quotes the earlier ones).**

PARITY (proved; W = 0, the homology of the lattice). A closed even set of plaquettes of Z⁴ is the boundary mod 2 of a finite set of cubes; a cube has six plaquettes and |A Δ B| ≡ |A| + |B| mod 2, so F is even. An odd level of the search is empty by this, and the record's odd levels read empty.

EXACT MINIMA (each a theorem by exhaustion in the box [0,7]⁴ around the graph). (1) OBJECT E'S GRAPH — two unit squares in complementary planes sharing one vertex, cycle rank 2: every level to 41 is exhausted with no Z₂-minimal set; level 42 is exhausted with 572,590 closed sets of exactly that contact graph, 40 of them Z₂-minimal. The chunks printed 18; their images under the eight symmetries of the graph are exactly 40 sets, the level's count, so the list is whole and is a record. Read on both roads of `latobj`, the roads meeting on all 40: each is three pieces — two single plaquettes on the two squares and one disc of 40 — with |S| = 4 on 24 of them and 4/3 on 16. So the least Z₂-minimal set on that graph is four plaquettes below Object E and no violator: a violator on that graph has at least 44 plaquettes, Object E's 46 is the least known, and level 44 is not read (about 10¹¹ nodes). (2) ONE UNIT SQUARE, cycle rank 1: level 34 exhausted with none, Z₂-minimal sets at 36 (432 read in the chunks ended); the least is 36. On one contact cycle the reading is at most 4 whatever the set (K₁ = 1), so these weigh nothing against the ceiling; they price the area.

FLOORS (no Z₂-minimal set found; exhausted through the level named): two coplanar squares on a link, rank 2: through 40, F ≥ 42; two squares on a link at a right angle, rank 2: through 37, F ≥ 38; the 2 × 2 block, rank 4: through 51, F ≥ 52; the twelve links of a 3-cube, rank 5: through 44, F ≥ 46; the thirty-two links of a 4-cube, rank 17: through 57, F ≥ 58. For the 4-cube a count does better than the search. A plaquette with two of its links on the graph is one of the cube's 24 faces; such a face has all four links on the graph and is a piece by itself; every other plaquette of the set meets the graph in at most one link. The graph's links carry 4 · 32 = 128 passes. By the lemma below the pieces are at most 18, and they cannot all be inner faces (18 faces carry 72 passes), so at most 17 are; hence F ≥ k + (128 − 4k) ≥ 128 − 51 = 77. So F / r is 36 and 21 exactly on the two graphs settled, and at least 21, 19, 13, 9.2 and 4.5 on the five open ones. A lemma F ≥ c r − c₀ for every contact graph is not drawn from seven graphs; the dense ones are where c is decided, and neither search nor count has reached their least areas.

THE PIECES (proved; the proofs are on the campaign page, §35 RESULTS 10 ADDENDUM VI-m, cb831d9e6). A piece is a class of faces joined through two-fold links; the contact graph's links have multiplicity four or more. LEMMA P: a Z₂-minimal set has at most r + 1 pieces — their boundary classes mod 2 lie in the contact graph's cycle space, sum to zero, and no proper subfamily does. LEMMA O: for any spin assignment on any closed set, |S| is at most the product over the pieces of d_j²; a set of one piece is bounded by the sphere's term at every spin, whatever its rank. Read on the mixed gas through six faces (`cp_closing rank`, 3b6f2da13): every Z₂-minimal set on a forest is one piece, every one on a single contact cycle two. Lemma O settles one piece. Two pieces or more is what the censuses of NOTES I and IV measure: on one contact cycle, two pieces, the reading is still at most 4 (K₁ = 1); each of the 40 sets above has three pieces, and so has Object E.

**(q) NOTE III, ADDENDUM V — THE SPHERE CASE AS A MAP WITH MATCHED EDGES. PROVED: z = 1 IS "NO CLOSED SUBGRAPH MADE OF WHOLE PAIRS"; THEN NO CYCLE OF THE CHORD GRAPH IS EQUAL ON BOTH CHORDS OF EVERY GENERATOR; SO NO SINGLE GENERATOR CARRIES A FLOW AND EVERY TERM INVOLVES AT LEAST TWO. READ: NO EXCEPTION ON 251,983,344 SHEETS, AND THE FLOW BOUND AT MOST 5/3 OVER THE SPHERE'S SHEETS OF ALL 340,446,343 SYSTEMS OF ONE, TWO AND THREE CHORDS THAT HAVE ONE. WITHOUT A SPHERE'S SHEET THE ABSOLUTE COUNT FAILS (2026-11-05 10:09 EST, read from `date`; the desktop seat ce191afc; the readings from the seat db5968f8's records `wordsys4_flow_n1`, `_n2`, `_n3.2026-11-05a.txt` in `STDLIB/build/surface_gas_rank`, landed 9e3bff2a7, their WZ4-FLOW-TOTAL lines read here).**

THE MAP. Let a system with z = 1 have a base π whose resolution is a sphere (|s_∅| = 4). The resolution is a map on the sphere: its faces are the B circles, its vertices the V = 2 − B + 2N vertex lines, and each generator g is two edges, e_g = (u, v) and e′_g = (u′, v′), the two pairs of π, the generator's direction matching u with u′ and v with v′. The arc where a pair turns back at one side of g lies on the vertex at that end of its edge, so the two chords of g are {u, u′} and {v, v′}: the chord graph Γ joins the corresponding ends of each generator's two edges. Read here on the landed reader's word `map`: the block "ABAbab A B" at base 10 prints a: 0-1, 2-2 and b: 2-0, 1-1, a path 2 – 0 – 1 with a loop at each end, and its chords a: 0-2, 1-2, b: 2-1, 0-1.

LEMMA 1 (z = 1 on the map; a sphere is used). The faces' parity vectors have exactly one relation if and only if no nonempty even subgraph of the map is a union of generators' edge pairs. PROOF. A set of faces has even parity in g exactly when the two edges of g are both on its boundary or both off it; on a sphere the boundaries of the sets of faces are exactly the even subgraphs of the map. ∎

LEMMA 2 (no symmetric cycle). Under Lemma 1's condition, no nonempty set A of generators has all its chords forming an even subgraph of Γ; equivalently no nonzero ℤ₂-cycle of Γ takes equal values on the two chords of every generator. PROOF. For each g the two edges and the two chords form a closed walk u, v, v′, u′. Summing these over A gives edges(A) + chords(A), even at every vertex; if chords(A) were even at every vertex, so would edges(A) be, a nonempty even subgraph of whole pairs, against Lemma 1. ∎

COROLLARY (no single generator). At a sphere's sheet of a system with z = 1, F_{Γ_{\{g\}}}(4) = 0 for every generator g, so s_{\{g\}} = 0: every nonzero term beside the sheet's involves at least two generators. PROOF. Two edges carry a nowhere-zero flow only when both are loops or when they join the same two vertices; either way the two chords of g are an even subgraph, which Lemma 2 forbids. ∎

WHAT ELSE FOLLOWS. For every set A the cycles of Γ_A are cycles of Γ, so b₁(Γ_A) ≤ |A| (a cycle space meeting the "equal on both chords" subspace only in zero has dimension at most the number of generators), F_{Γ_A}(4) ≤ 3^{|A|}, and every term has modulus at most |s_∅|. A cycle is determined by its vector of differences between each generator's two chords.

THE READINGS (the records' lines). Sphere's sheets of z = 1 systems read: 36 at one chord, 71,456 at two, 251,911,852 at three; chord graphs of cycle rank above N: 0; sets of generators with every chord even at every line: 0. The least number of generators carrying a flow: none at all on 36, on 69,000 and on 220,061,863 sheets; two on 2,456 and on 16,660,212; three on 15,189,777; never one. Systems with a sphere's sheet: 29, 43,851 and 340,402,463, of them above the ceiling over their sphere's sheets: 0 (the least bound 5/3 at most). The form itself read in full on 72, 241,920 and 239,382 systems: 0 partings on 216, 6,531,840 and 45,243,198 terms. The four-chord chain reads 25/9 over its sphere's sheets, by that seat's line.

WITHOUT A SPHERE'S SHEET. The least flow bound over all bases is above the ceiling on 10 one-chord and 31,780 two-chord systems, every one without a sphere's sheet: the count of flows loses a cross-cap's cancellation ((aaa)(a): 8 against 4). There the proved bound is NOTE IV's.

WHERE THIS STANDS. PROVED at a sphere's sheet with z = 1: Lemmas 1 and 2 and the corollary. READ, not proved: the ceiling 5/3 through three chords and 25/9 on the chain. THE ATTEMPT ON THE CEILING AND WHERE IT STOPS: with at least two generators in every term each nonzero pair of cycles weighs at most 1/9, and there are fewer than 4^N pairs, which is no better than NOTE IV's rate; what makes the block read 5/3 is that of its fifteen nonzero pairs of cycles only six have a support made of whole generators (its double edge is one chord of a and one of b). THE NEXT OBJECT is that count: on a map with its 2N edges in matched pairs and no closed subgraph of whole pairs, the pairs of cycles of Γ whose joint support is a union of generators' chord pairs, weighted 3^{−generators}. W = 0 on the cycle space of a planar map; the earnings are the map, the two lemmas and the corollary.

**(q) NOTE III, ADDENDUM VI — THE DESIGN SUM: THE HAAR INTEGRAL OF A FOUR-FOLD WORD SYSTEM IS AN AVERAGE OVER THE 24 HURWITZ UNITS (PROVED), IT SPLITS INTO 3^N ROTOR SUMS AND EACH IS A SIGNED POWER OF TWO (PROVED: A GAUSS SUM OF A QUADRATIC FORM OVER F₂); READ ON EVERY TWO-CHORD SYSTEM AND 2,000,000 THREE-CHORD SYSTEMS: |S_k| ≤ 4^z 2^{|supp k|}, EQUALITY AT EVERY SUPPORT SIZE, WHICH WOULD GIVE |S| ≤ 4^z (5/3)^N — 5/3 PER CHORD WITH NO SHEET AND NO RESOLUTION; THE FLOW BOUND'S STAKE (5/3)^{⌊N/2⌋} AT A SPHERE'S SHEET PROVED THROUGH SIX CHORDS BY EXHAUSTION OVER THE CODES (2026-11-06 01:38 EST, read from `date`; the desktop seat ce191afc; instruments `STDLIB/build/surface_gas_rank/designsum.c`, `codemax.c`, `designsum_theta_run.sh`; records `designsum_kat`, `designsum_census_n1`, `designsum_census_n2`, `designsum_census_n3_sample`, `designsum_check_n2`, `designsum_check_n3` (on `designsum_classes_n2/n3.2026-11-06a.tsv`, cut from the seat db5968f8's class records), `codemax_kat`, `codemax_ceiling`, `codemax_pairs`, `designsum_theta`, all `.2026-11-06a.txt`).**

THE IDENTITY (proved; W = 0 on the design property). A word system: N generators, each occurring four times as itself or its inverse, in B cyclic words; S = ∫ Π_c 2 tr w_c over SU(2)^N (the page's normalisation: "A A A A" reads 32, "AAA A" reads −4). In each generator g the integrand is a matrix coefficient of g^{⊗4} (V is self-conjugate), so only the Haar average of g^{⊗4} enters, the projector onto the SU(2)-invariants of V^{⊗4}, a space of dimension 2. The binary tetrahedral group 2T, the 24 Hurwitz units ±1, ±i, ±j, ±k, (±1 ± i ± j ± k)/2, has (1/24) Σ_{2T} (tr g)⁴ = (2·16 + 16·1)/24 = 2: its invariants in V^{⊗4} contain SU(2)'s and have the same dimension, so its average of g^{⊗4} is the same projector. Hence

**S = 2^B · 24^{−N} · Σ_{g ∈ 2T^N} Π_c tr w_c(g), every trace an integer in {0, ±1, ±2}** —

no resolution, no sign bookkeeping, S ∈ 2^B 24^{−N} ℤ. The sixth moment of 2T is 6 against SU(2)'s 5, so a six-fold generator is outside the identity: the instrument carries that control and refuses a generator not occurring four times. KNOWN ANSWERS MET: by hand (χ_n the character of dimension n + 1, tr g² = χ₂ − 1, tr g³ = χ₃ − χ₁, tr g⁴ = χ₄ − χ₂): "A A A A" 32, "AAA A" −4, "AAAA" 0, "AA AA" 8, "Aa Aa" 16, "AA A A" 0; the three two-chord classes at 20/3; and THE CENSUS OF RECORD RE-DERIVED: all 3 two-chord and all 951 three-chord classes above 1 of the seat db5968f8's resolution reader (`wordsys4_classes_n2/n3.2026-11-05a.txt`, value and sign) MET, 0 parted; every two-chord system (241,920: 784 above 1 at 5/3, the whole ratio histogram 0, 1/12, 1/6, 1/4, 1/3, 5/12, 1/2, 2/3, 5/6, 1, 5/3) reproduced from the design sum in six seconds — a second engine for the whole census, sharing no line with the first (law 6).

THE ROTORS. 2T = Q₈ ⋊ Z₃: Q₈ = {±1, ±i, ±j, ±k}, ω = (−1 + i + j + k)/2 of order three, and conjugation by ω permutes i, j, k cyclically. The class of g ∈ 2T^N modulo Q₈^N is a rotor k ∈ Z₃^N, and S = 3^{−N} Σ_k S_k, S_k = 2^B 8^{−N} Σ over the coset ω^k Q₈^N. At k = 0 the sum is over Pauli labels alone.

THEOREM G (proved). Every S_k is zero or a signed power of two. PROOF. Write g_i = ε_i σ_{x_i} ω^{k_i}, ε_i = ±1 and x_i ∈ F₄ the Pauli label (σ₀ = 1 and {σ_1, σ_ζ, σ_{ζ²}} = {i, j, k}). (i) The signs factor out: a generator occurs four times, ε_i⁴ = 1, so the ε-sum is 2^N. (ii) Moving every ω to the right of a word by ω σ_x ω^{−1} = σ_{ζ²x} (conjugation by ω multiplies the label by a primitive cube root of unity) gives w_c = ± σ_{L_c(x)} ω^{m_c}, m_c ∈ Z₃ the signed sum of the rotors along c and L_c(x) = Σ_j ζ^{E_j} x_{i_j} an F₄-linear form whose coefficients are the rotor exponents accumulated along the word. (iii) The cocycle of Q₈ is bilinear: σ_a σ_b = (−1)^{f(a,b)} σ_{a+b} with f(a, b) = tr_{F₄/F₂}(ζ a b̄) (the sixteen pairs checked by hand: f(1,1) = 1 for i² = −1, f(1,ζ) = 0 for ij = k, f(ζ,1) = 1 for ji = −k, and so on), so the sign of a product of Pauli units whose labels sum to zero is (−1)^{Σ_{j<l} f(c_j, c_l)}, a quadratic function of the labels; an inverse occurrence adds the norm form N(a) = a ā (σ_a^{−1} = (−1)^{N(a)} σ_a). (iv) tr(σ_a ω^m) = 2·[a = 0] at m = 0, −1 for every a at m = 1, and −(−1)^{N(a)} at m = 2. So S_k = 2^{B − 2N} Σ_{x ∈ F₄^N} Π_c τ_c(x) with every τ_c quadratic in sign: S_k = 2^{B − 2N + B₀(k)} Σ_{x ∈ K_k} (−1)^{Q_k(x)}, B₀(k) the number of circles with m_c = 0, K_k = {x : L_c(x) = 0 for every such circle} an F₄-subspace of F₂-dimension 2(N − ρ_k), Q_k a quadratic function on it. A Gauss sum of a quadratic function over F₂ is 0 or ± 2^{dim − rk(B)/2}, B its polar form (W = 0). ∎ READ: of the 2,177,280 rotor sums of every two-chord system and the 54,000,000 of 2,000,000 three-chord systems, none is other than zero or a signed power of two.

THE CEILING READ. On every system at one chord (72) and two chords (241,920) and on 2,000,000 three-chord systems (200,000 uniformly random permutations of the twelve occurrences for each of the ten multisets of inversions, seed 7; 7,476 above 1, at 5/3, 13/9 and 11/9 only, the record's three values): **|S_k| ≤ 4^z · 2^{|supp k|}, the largest ratio exactly 1.000000 at every support size** (0, 1, 2 at N = 2; 0, 1, 2, 3 at N = 3), equality at "A A A A B B B B" (k = 0) and at "AAA BBB CCC ABC" (S_k = 4, −8, 16, −32 at |supp k| = 0, 1, 2, 3; S = −4). If this ceiling is a theorem then by the triangle inequality

**|S| ≤ 3^{−N} Σ_k |S_k| ≤ 4^z · (5/3)^N: 5/3 per chord, for every system, with no sheet and no resolution** —

and the mean E_k|S_k| attains 4^z (5/3)^N ("A AABBBA B" at N = 2, "AAA AB BBBCCC C" at N = 3), so this road's ceiling is exactly (5/3)^N: the systems below it are below by cancellation among the rotors ("AAA BBB CCC ABC": the rotor sums alternate in sign and total −4 against a mean of 125/27·4). WHAT IT WOULD MOVE: on a Z₂-minimal set with r four-fold contact links (z = 1 by Lemma 1 of ADDENDUM V) |S(X)| ≤ 4 (5/3)^r against NOTE IV's proved 4 · 2^r; with the four-line count r ≤ (3F + 3)/5 that is 4 (5/3)^{3/5} (1.3586)^F = 5.44 · 1.3586^F, and with r ≤ (75F + 167)/153 it is 6.98 · 1.2845^F — both below the all-spin √2, where NOTE VI's table says any proved rate below 2^{5/6} = 1.78 per chord passes it. THE ABSTRACT CODE, EXHAUSTED. At a sphere's sheet of a z = 1 system the unsigned flow bound Σ_A 3^{−|A|} F_{Γ_A}(4) is a function of the cycle space Z(Γ) ⊂ F₂^{2N} alone (F(4) counts the pairs of cycles supported in A's chords and nowhere zero there), and Lemma 2 of ADDENDUM V says Z(Γ) meets the pair space U = span(e_c + e_{c′}) in zero. `codemax.c` reads the bound on EVERY binary code meeting U in zero: such a code lies in a complement of U, the bound is monotone in the code, the complements are the graphs z = R d of the N × N matrices R over F₂ (z the first chord's value, d the two chords' difference), exchanging a generator's two chords adds 1 to R_gg, so R has zero diagonal — a digraph on the generators — and the bound is invariant under relabelling. THE RESULT: the largest value is 3^N (5/3)^{⌊N/2⌋} at N = 1, 2, 3, 4, 5 over every matrix (1, 4, 64, 4096, 1,048,576) and at N = 6 over every digraph up to isomorphism (23,850,312 matrices with non-increasing in-degree rows), attained at even N only by the perfect matching of 2-cycles (disjoint blocks) and at odd N by 7 (N = 3) and 13 (N = 5) classes. **So the flow bound's stake (5/3)^{⌊N/2⌋} at a sphere's sheet, read on 340 million systems at N ≤ 3 (ADDENDUM V), is a THEOREM for every z = 1 system with a sphere's sheet at N ≤ 6, by Lemma 2 and the exhaustion, no system enumerated.** Three roads to the number (the rank-two pair sum, the plain sum over all pairs, the code read coordinate by coordinate) agree on every matrix at N ≤ 4, the transpose and a changed diagonal leave it, eight hand values met (the KAT). THE INDUCTIVE SHAPE, read: at N ≤ 5 every digraph has a pair of nodes whose deletion divides the bound by at most 5/3 (the least ratio over pairs is at most 5/3 on all 1,048,576 at N = 5, its largest exactly 5/3 at a directed 4-cycle); a single node costs at most 5/3 (N = 2, 4), 11/9 (N = 3), 1.2626 (N = 5). "Some pair costs at most 5/3" for every digraph would give the stake at every N; it is not proved.
