THE THEOREM. Let B cyclic words in N four-fold generators be given, A one generator, k = κ e_A with κ ∈ {1, 2}. Then |S_k| ≤ 2 · 4^z, z = B − ρ. With ADDENDUM VI's |S_0| ≤ 4^z this is the ceiling |S_k| ≤ 4^z 2^{|supp k|} for every rotor of support at most one, hence E_k|S_k| ≤ 4^z over those rotors; the rate 5/3 a chord needs the ceiling at every support and is not touched by this.

THE SETTING (ADDENDUM VI, one generator rotated). The circles with rotor sum m_c = 0 are C₀ (B₀ of them, the empties among them) and the rest C₁ (B₁ = B − B₀); K_k = {x ∈ F₄^N : L_c(x) = 0, c ∈ C₀} has F₂-dimension 2(N − ρ_k), ρ_k the F₄-rank of the twisted rows of C₀; |S_k| = 2^{B + B₀ − 2ρ_k − r/2} or 0, r the rank of the sign's polar form on K_k. So |S_k| ≤ 2^{B + B₀ − 2ρ_k}, and the ceiling is immediate wherever need := 2(ρ − ρ_k) − B₁ − 1 ≤ 0. Three facts carry the case analysis. (i) Σ_c P_c = 0 over all circles — every generator occurs four times — so any one circle's parity row is the sum of the others'. (ii) The A-column of every C₀ row is zero: along a circle the rotor exponent walks Z₃ by ±κ at each A-letter, a circle with m_c = 0 is a closed walk, the A-letters standing at level ℓ each contribute ζ^{2κℓ}, and their number has the parity of the walk's winding, the same at every level — so the coefficient is 0 or 1 + ζ + ζ² = 0. (iii) THE (1,1) LEMMA: a circle u₀ A u₁ A⁻¹ has twisted row P_{u₀} + ζ^{±2κ} P_{u₁}, and if that row lies in R ⊗ F₄ for an F₂-space R then P_{u₀} + P_{u₁} ∈ R (the coefficient is ζ or 1 + ζ; the row's two F₂-components are P_{u₀}, P_{u₁} or P_{u₀} + P_{u₁}, P_{u₁}). Throughout, R is the span of the A-free rows, which are untwisted, so ρ_k ≥ dim R; p_c the parity of a circle's non-A letters.

THE CASE ANALYSIS (the four occurrences of A over the circles, (n⁺, n⁻) per circle; m_c = κ(n⁺ − n⁻) mod 3). I. (2,2) in one circle: B₁ = 0, the A-column zero, ρ = dim R by (i), ρ_k ≥ dim R: need ≤ −1. II. (1,1) + (1,1): B₁ = 0; ρ = dim R + [p₁ ∉ R] by (i), and (iii) makes the first twisted row leave R ⊗ F₄ whenever p₁ ∉ R, so ρ_k ≥ ρ: need ≤ −1. III. (3,0) + (1,0) (and signs reversed): B₁ = 1; the rows (p*, 1) and (p₁, 1) sum into R ⊕ 0, so ρ = dim R + 1 exactly; ρ_k ≥ dim R: need ≤ 0. IV. (1,1) + (2,0): B₁ = 1; ρ = dim R + [p* ∉ R] ≤ ρ_k by (iii): need ≤ −2. V. (1,1) + (1,0) + (0,1), and (1,1) + (1,0) + (1,0): B₁ = 2; the two single-A rows sum to (p*, 0) modulo R ⊕ 0, so ρ = dim R + 1 + [p* ∉ R] ≤ ρ_k + 1: need ≤ −1. VI. (2,0) + (2,0), (2,0) + (0,2), (0,2) + (0,2): B₁ = 2; ρ ≤ dim R + 1 = ρ_k + 1: need ≤ −1. VII. (2,1) + (1,0) and its signs: B₁ = 2, both rows odd in A and summing into R ⊕ 0: ρ = dim R + 1 = ρ_k + 1: need ≤ −1. VIII. (4,0), (3,1), (1,3), (0,4): B₁ = 1, the row's parity in R: ρ = ρ_k: need = −2. IX. (2,0) + (1,0) + (1,0) and its signs: B₁ = 3; ρ ≤ dim R + 2 = ρ_k + 2: need ≤ 0. X. the empty case of no A at all does not arise (A occurs four times). XI. once in each of four circles c_i = A^{s_i} v_i: B₁ = 4, C₀ the A-free circles, ρ_k = dim R, ρ = dim R + d with d the dimension of the four rows (p_i, 1) modulo R ⊕ 0, at most 3 by (i): need = 2d − 5 ≤ 1, and need = 1 exactly when d = 3, i.e. when the three sums p₁ + p₂, p₁ + p₃, p₂ + p₃ all lie outside R. So the ceiling is a rank statement in every case but XI, and in XI with d = 3 it asks r ≥ 2: the sign is not an affine function on K_k.

CASE XI. There K_k = F₄ e_A ⊕ K′, K′ = {x′ ∈ F₄^{G′} : the A-free circles' parities vanish}, and the sign does not see x_A: the A-letter's cross term is tr(ζ² x_A · conj(Σ_i p_i(x′))) with Σ_i p_i = 0 on K′, and the N(x_A) terms come in pairs (for κ = 1 twice per inverse letter, for κ = 2 once per letter, four letters). So S_k = 2^{2B−2N−2} Σ_{x′ ∈ K′} (−1)^{Ψ(x′)}, Ψ the parity of the A-free circles' signs, of the four segments' signs (v_i(σ_{x′}) = (−1)^{Ψ_i} σ_{p_i(x′)}) and of N(p_i) over the segments whose A-letter stands in the coset of m = 2. The same Ψ gives the diagonal rotor on its locus — K₀ is the x′ ∈ K′ with p₁ = p₂ = p₃ = p₄ — so S_0 = 2^{2B−2N} Σ_{p all equal} (−1)^Ψ. And the Haar integral over A with the rest on Q₈, H_A = (S_0 + S_{e_A} + S_{2e_A})/3 = (S_0 + 2S_k)/3 (S_{e_A} = S_{2e_A} by the automorphism of 2T that fixes Q₈ and exchanges ω with ω⁻¹, conjugation by (1 + i)/√2), has its own evaluation: with the four A-occurrences in separate circles the integrand is Π_i tr(g^{s_i} σ_{a_i}) up to the signs above, tr(g σ_a) = ±2 g_a is twice a coordinate of the unit quaternion g, and E g_a⁴ = 1/8, E g_a² g_b² = 1/24 on S³ (W = 0): the integral is 2 when a₁ = a₂ = a₃ = a₄, 2/3 when the labels form two pairs, 0 otherwise — and since Σ a_i = 0 the only other shape is four distinct labels. Hence 3H_A = 2^{2B−2N−2}(6 Σ_eq + 2 Σ_pairs) of (−1)^Ψ over the loci. Set against S_0 + 2S_k = 2^{2B−2N−2}(6 Σ_eq + 2 Σ_pairs + 2 Σ_4d): THE FOUR-DISTINCT SUM VANISHES, Σ_{x′ ∈ K′, p(x′) four distinct} (−1)^{Ψ(x′)} = 0, for every system of case XI. When d = 3 the values of (p₁, p₂, p₃) on K′ fill an F₄-space of dimension 2 or 3 in which 3/8 of the points have four distinct coordinates, so the locus is 3/8 of K′ and nonempty, and a Ψ identically zero would sum to its size there. So Ψ is not identically zero on K′; being quadratic, its sum over K′ is then 0 or at most |K′|/2 in magnitude, and |S_k| ≤ 2^{2B−2N−2} |K′|/2 = 2 · 4^z (2^{2B−2N−2} = 4 · 4^z/|K′| at d = 3). ∎

READ. The need histogram at |supp k| = 1: need ≤ 0 on all 144 rotors at one chord and all 967,680 nonzero rotor sums at two chords (the first need = 0 at "AAA A B B B B"); at three chords (the sample of record, seed 7) need = 1 on 5,668 of 1,200,000 and ≤ 0 on the rest, and every one of the 5,668 has its rotated generator once in each of four circles, none otherwise — the instrument's own check of the case analysis on every rotor it reads. The identity: on the 3 case-XI generators of one chord, the 20,160 of two chords and the 24,911 of the three-chord sample, S_0 = 2^{2B−2N} Σ_eq, S_e = S_{2e} = 2^{2B−2N−2} Σ_{K′}, 3H = 2^{2B−2N−2}(6Σ_eq + 2Σ_pairs) and Σ_4d = 0 all MET, BROKEN on none; the four-distinct locus is empty on every one- and two-chord system (there K′ has one coordinate) and nonempty on 2,834 generators of the three-chord sample. By hand on "AB AC AD ABCD BB CC DD" (four chords, d = 3): |K′| = 64, the loci (count, signed sum) all equal (4, 4), two pairs (36, −12), four distinct (24, 0): S_0 = 256 = 4^z, S_e = −128 = −4^z/2, the rotor's polar form of rank 6 on K′; on "ABCB AACB ABC C" (C rotated, the first need = 1 of the sample): (1, 1), (9, −9), (6, 0), S_0 = 4^z, S_e = −2 · 4^z, at the ceiling. The one-generator Haar sums H_g = (S_0 + S_e + S_{2e})/3, read against 4^z on 72 + 483,840 + 600,000 readings: none above, 39 + 207,654 + 181,552 at equality, the largest ratio 1.000000 — a reading beyond the theorem (|H_g| ≤ 4^z would give the sign condition at equality) and not proved. The instrument's correction: `rotor_read` had log2|S_k| short by the number of empty circles (an empty circle carries 2 tr 1 = 4); no landed reading had an empty circle (the census writes none); corrected, with the comment.

W = 0: the fourth moments of the uniform unit quaternion, the Gauss sum of a quadratic form over F₂, the outer automorphism of 2T, the rank over F₄ of a matrix read over F₂; the Weingarten resolution of the chord would give the same identity term by term. The earnings: the case analysis with its three facts, the vanishing of the four-distinct sum and its use.

THE NEXT OBJECT: two generators rotated. The data at three chords: need = 2 on 16 of 2,400,000 rotors of support two ("AB ABBBA AC CCC", k = 12) and need = 1 on 1,298 — the rank inequality there asks r ≥ 4 and r ≥ 2. The same program: the pair of distributions of A and A′ (the twisted rows now carry both walks), the rank bound by (i)–(iii), and where it is not enough the Haar identity on A with A′ held in its coset, 3H_A^{(κ′)} = S_{(0,κ′)} + S_{(1,κ′)} + S_{(2,κ′)}, whose moment side is Σ over the three pairings of (h_i · h_j)(h_k · h_l) times 2/3 with h_i ∈ 2T (tr(g h) = 2 g · h̄, the same fourth moments) — the `loci` reader given the second generator's coset. Attacked at the desk first, as this one was: which pairs of distributions can have need > 0.

**(q) NOTE III, ADDENDUM VIII — THE SIGN'S POLAR FORM WRITTEN OUT: A SESQUILINEAR FORM β OVER F₄ WHOSE RANK ON K_k IS THE HALF-RANK OF EVERY ROTOR SUM (PROVED; MET ON 7,941,780 ROTORS); THE CEILING AS ONE RANK INEQUALITY ON A BORDERED MATRIX, rank M_k ≥ 2ρ − |supp k| (PROVED EQUIVALENT); THE TRANSLATION SYMMETRY AND THE FREEZING LEMMA (PROVED); THE DIAGONAL AND CORNER LEMMAS FOR A ROTATED GENERATOR ALONE IN ITS CIRCLES (PROVED BY EXHAUSTION OVER THE 60 PATTERNS); THE CORNER THEOREM rank ≥ 2t FOR SEPARATED ROTORS (PROVED), WHICH SETTLES THE m-FOLD CASE III AT EVERY SUPPORT WITH EQUALITY; THE FROBENIUS LEMMA BEHIND ADDENDUM VII (PROVED); THE HONEST Z₃-COVER'S PAIRING BLOCK-DIAGONAL (PROVED) AND ITS DESIGN SUM THE PRODUCT S₀ S_k S_{−k} (READ ON 7 COVERS); THE BOUNDS need ≤ 2t + 1 (SEPARATED) READ ON 2,253,644 ROTORS AND THE MIXED CASE OPEN WITH need UP TO 3 EXHIBITED (2026-11-06 08:36 EST, read from `date`; the desktop seat ce191afc; `designsum.c` grown by the modes `beta`, `betacensus` (to four chords), `cornerkat`, `cover`, six generators; records `designsum_beta_n2`, `designsum_beta_n3_sample`, `designsum_beta_n4_sample`, `designsum_beta_tripleIII`, `designsum_cornerkat`, `designsum_cover`, all `.2026-11-06b.txt`).**

THE FORM (proved). With σ_aσ_b = (−1)^{f(a,b)}σ_{a+b}, f(a,b) = tr(ζ a b̄), σ_a⁻¹ = (−1)^{N(a)}σ_a, the commutator (−1)^{⟨a,b⟩}, ⟨a,b⟩ = tr(a b̄), and the levels E_j of the letters (an occurrence's coefficient is ζ^{2E_j}), the sign Q_k on K_k is a quadratic function over F₂ whose polar form is tr β(x, y) for the sesquilinear form — F₄-linear in x, conjugate-linear in y —

β(x, y) = Σ_{c ∈ C₁} (ζ + [m_c = 2]) L_c(x) conj L_c(y) + Σ_c Σ_{j<l ∈ c} ζ^{2(E_l − E_j)} x_{g_l} conj y_{g_j} + Σ_g n_g⁻ x_g conj y_g.

(The per-circle polar is f(L_c(x), L_c(y)) + Σ_j f(x_{g_j}, y_{g_j}) + the crossing sum + the inverse letters' ⟨x_g, y_g⟩; the middle terms cancel over the system, each generator occurring four times; on K_k the C₀ circles' f(L_c, L_c) vanish.) Since K_k is an F₄-space and tr(a) = tr(ζa) = 0 only at a = 0, the F₂-rank of the polar form is twice the F₄-rank of β on K_k: |S_k| = 2^{B + B₀ − 2ρ_k − rank β|K_k} or 0, and THE CEILING READS rank_{F₄}(β|K_k) ≥ need := 2(ρ − ρ_k) − B₁ − |supp k|. Read on every rotor of every two-chord system (2,177,280), on 5,400,000 three-chord rotors (seed 7) and 364,500 four-chord rotors (300 systems a multiset, seed 7): the rank of β on the kernel of the C₀ rows met the half-rank read from the power of two on all, parted on none.

THE BORDERED FORM (proved). Writing 2[a = 0] = ½ Σ_b (−1)^{tr(a b̄)}, −(−1)^{N(a)} = ½ Σ_b (−1)^{N(b) + tr(a b̄)} and −1 = ½ Σ_b (−1)^{N(b)} over b ∈ F₄, the rotor sum is the Gauss sum over F₄^{N+B} of Q°(x) + Σ_{m_c ≠ 0} N(b_c) + Σ_{m_c ≠ 1} tr(L_c(x) b̄_c), Q° the cocycle sign alone; so |S_k| = 2^{2B − rank M_k} with M_k = [[β°, Λ^T], [Λ̄, Δ]], β° = ζ Σ_c L_c conj L_c + the crossing and inverse terms over all circles, Λ the rows L_c at m_c ≠ 1 (zero rows at m_c = 1), Δ = diag[m_c ≠ 0]; block elimination gives rank M_k = B₁ + 2ρ_k + rank(β|K_k), and THE CEILING IS rank_{F₄} M_k ≥ 2ρ − |supp k| — at k = 0 the border is P with a zero corner and rank M₀ ≥ 2ρ is the dependency count; rotating m generators may lose at most m.

THE TRANSLATION SYMMETRY (proved). Conjugating every generator's element by σ_t ∈ Q₈ preserves the product of traces and moves the label of a rotated generator g by (1 + ζ^{2k_g}) t, an unrotated one not at all (2T/{±1} is the affine group of F₄, the rotor its multiplier). So v_k = Σ_{g ∈ supp k} (1 + ζ^{2k_g}) e_g lies in K_k, in the radical of β, with Q_k(x + t v_k) = Q_k(x): for k ≠ 0, rank(β|K_k) ≤ dim K_k − 1, and THE FREEZING LEMMA: S_{(κ, k′)} equals the rotor-k′ design sum of the system with A replaced by the constant ω^κ (or by any element of its coset), the one-generator reduction behind Addendum VII's slices. Only one generator freezes at a time: the constant breaks the symmetry for the next.

THE DIAGONAL AND CORNER LEMMAS (proved by exhaustion). Let A be rotated with no other rotated generator in its circles. Then e_A ∈ K_k (Addendum VII's fact (ii)) and, pattern by pattern — n ≤ 4 letters A^{±} in order with n + 1 slots of other letters, κ ∈ {1, 2}, every sign pattern, 60 in all (`cornerkat`) — a circle's part of β(e_A, e_A) is ζ^κ when n is odd and 0 when n is even, so the four occurrences sum to zero: β(e_A, e_A) = 0; and its part of β(e_A, y) is ζ^κ conj Σ_j P_{u_j}(y) on the slots when m_c ≠ 0, plus one multiple of conj L′_c(y) when m_c = 0, which vanishes on K_k: β(e_A, y) = ζ^κ conj π_A(y) on K_k, π_A the sum of the parities of A's circles over the other generators. Read on 995,328 + 1,279,608 + 39,776 such generators of the censuses: no exception.

THE CORNER THEOREM (proved). If every rotated generator is separated, K_k = F₄^{supp k} ⊕ K″ with β zero on the first summand, its rows and columns there the units times the functionals π_A, so in the block form [[0, U], [V, β″]] the rank is at least rank U + rank V = 2t, t = dim_{F₄} span{π_A|K″}. HENCE THE m-FOLD CASE III: every rotated generator A with its four occurrences as (3,0) + (1,0) in two circles of its own (signs reversed allowed), the (3,0) rows' twisted forms in the span of the unrotated rows — then ρ_k = dim R, ρ = dim R + m + t with t the rank of the m functionals π_A = p_{AAA} + p_A modulo R, and need = 2t exactly: THE CEILING HOLDS AT EVERY SUPPORT, WITH EQUALITY WHENEVER S_k ≠ 0. The witness at three: "AAADD AD BBBEE BE CCC CDE" at k = (1,1,1,0,0): ρ = 5, ρ_k = 0, B₁ = 3, need = 4, t = 2, rank 4, |S_k| = 32 = 8 · 4^z (record `designsum_beta_tripleIII`); need is not bounded by the support — it is 2m − 2 here.

THE FROBENIUS LEMMA (proved; Addendum VII's mechanism in general). Conjugation by τ = (1 + i)/√2 fixes Q₈ as a set and acts on the labels by the Frobenius (i, j, k ↦ i, k, −j), so Q_{−k}(x̄) = Q_k(x) termwise and K_{−k} = conj K_k. When K_k is Frobenius-stable, S_k = S_{−k} gives Σ_{x ∈ K_k, δ(x) = 1} (−1)^{Q_k(x)} = 0 for δ = Q_{−k} − Q_k = Σ_c Σ_{j<l, E_j ≠ E_l} f(x_{g_j}, x_{g_l}) + Σ_{m_c = 2} N(L_c^{(k)}) + Σ_{m_c = 1} N(L_c^{(−k)}): so Q_k is not identically zero wherever δ is not — rank ≥ 1 or S_k = 0. In Addendum VII's case δ = Σ_i N(p_i), the indicator of the four-distinct locus.

THE HONEST COVER. For a rotor with every m_c = 0 the Z₃-cover X̂ (three vertices, 3N generators, 3B circles, the walk shifting the sheet) has parity rank ρ + 2ρ_k, its cocycles decompose K₀ ⊕ K_k ⊕ K_{−k} under the deck action, and the deck-invariance of the cover's pairing (β(ζ^i x, ζ^j y) = ζ^{i−j} β(x, y)) makes it block-diagonal: rank β(X̂) = rank β₀ + 2 rank β_k (proved). READ on every honest two-chord cover with a six-generator lift the instrument holds — "AAA AB BBB" at (2,1), "AAaa BBbb", "AaAa BbBb", "ABab ABab" at both rotors, "AABB aabb", "ABAB abab" — the cover's diagonal design sum is the product S₀(X) S_k(X) S_{−k}(X) (7 of 7, `cover`); on a branched cover it is not ("AAA AB BBB" at (1,1): 256 against 1024).

THE BOUNDS READ. On every rotor whose rotated generators are all separated, need ≤ 2t + 1: 981,504 of 981,504 at two chords, 1,234,708 of 1,234,708 at three, 37,432 of 37,432 at four; so the corner theorem leaves at most one unit there, and that unit is Addendum VII's at support one (t = 0, need = 1). On the mixed rotors need ≤ 1 at two chords (953,856) and three (3,965,292), but not in general: the triple case-III system above at k = (0,1,1,1,0) — C and D sharing "CDE" — has need 3 with the ceiling holding (rank 3). The tally by (support, separated, need, t, rank) is in the records; every need ≥ 1 rotor read has rank ≥ need, as the ceiling says.

W = 0: the polar form of a quadratic function, the Gauss sum of a bordered form, the affine group AGL(1, F₄) ≅ A₄, the Frobenius of F₄, the isotypic decomposition of a cyclic cover's cochains. The earnings: the form β and its rank identity, the bordered inequality, the four lemmas and the corner theorem, the case-III family at every support.

THE NEXT OBJECT: rank M_k ≥ 2ρ − |supp k| in general. (a) The separated case: need ≤ 2t + 1 as a theorem (the span of the rotated rows modulo the unrotated against the span of the π_A), and at need = 2t + 1 the last unit from the Frobenius lemma on K‴ = the corner functionals' kernel — Addendum VII's four-distinct argument is the case t = 0. (b) The mixed case: a corner for a generator whose circles hold another rotated one, where the twisted rows see x_A and β(e_A, e_A) ≠ 0 — "AAA AB BBB" at (1,1) reads β = (x_A + x_B) conj(x_A + x_B), the mixed C₁ circle's (ζ + [m = 2]) L_c conj L_c against the (3,0) circles' ζ on the diagonal. The instrument: `beta "WORDS"` prints every rotor's form on K_k with its rank, the diagonal and corner of each separated generator; `betacensus N [sample seed]` tallies. Attacked at the desk next.

THE SEPARATED CASE REDUCED (read 08:45, the instrument's `betacensus` with the remainder on K‴ = {x ∈ K_k : x_T = 0, π_A(x) = 0}; records `designsum_beta_n2/_n3_sample/_n4_sample.2026-11-06c.txt`). On every separated rotor with need ≥ 1 of the three censuses (196 + 5,864 + 454): need ≤ 2t + 1; K‴ is Frobenius-stable; and wherever need = 2t + 1 (5,668 + 430) the difference δ = Q_{−k} − Q_k is nonzero on K‴ and the rank of β on K‴ is at least 1 — the Frobenius lemma's two hypotheses hold exactly where the corner theorem leaves a unit, and the lemma supplies it. So the separated case rests on three rank statements, each read without exception and none yet proved: (i) need ≤ 2t + 1 — the untwisted rank of the rotated circles' rows modulo the unrotated against the twisted C₀ rows and the π_A; (ii) the span of the C₀ rows, the rotated coordinates and the π_A is closed under the Frobenius; (iii) δ is not identically zero on K‴ when need = 2t + 1. The mixed case has its own object: the within-component rank of a contact component Γ of the rotated generators (each component carries its own translation vector v_Γ with β(x, v_Γ) a unit times π_Γ(x″), so rank ≥ 2 t_Γ by the same block argument), where "AAA AB BBB" at (1,1) has one component, t_Γ = 0 and rank 1 from the mixed C₁ circle's (ζ + [m = 2]) L_c conj L_c against the triples' ζ on the diagonal.

CORRECTED (, read from `date`; the record `designsum_beta_xi2_seven.2026-11-06c.txt`, 15f95a7e1): statement (i) is FALSE as written. So the separated case rests on the corner theorem and on the rank of the frozen sign on K‴, which exceeds one when that sign splits; what is read without exception is the ceiling itself (the (i)-(iii) bounds of the paragraph above describe the three censuses to four chords only). THE NEXT OBJECT, sharpened: the rank of the frozen sign on K‴ against need − 2t — the frozen system is a decorated system (the rotated generators constants ω^{±1}) at rotor zero, and the needed rank is 2(ρ − ρ‴) − B₁ − m with ρ‴ the rank of the C₀ rows and the π_A; the per-generator four-distinct unit when the frozen sign splits, and the coupling through shared unrotated generators when it does not.

READ AT FIVE CHORDS (09:28, read from `date`; record `designsum_beta_n5_sample.2026-11-06c.txt`; the word store widened to 28 letters first, 6d5d92426 — its 16 had let the census's long circles overrun it from five chords on): 2,100 systems, 510,300 rotors, THE CEILING BROKEN ON NONE; the rank identity met on all; the diagonal and corner lemmas on all 25,112 separated generators; need up to 2 at support two and 1 at support three. THE BLOCK STRUCTURE OF THE REMAINDER: on every separated rotor where the corner theorem leaves a unit (need − 2t = 1; 392 rotors) the corner kernel K‴ is the direct sum of the rotated generators' domains — the parts of K‴ supported on each generator's own unrotated letters — with no cross term of β between domains, and the rank of β on K‴ is the sum of the domains' ranks, 1 to 4; cross terms between domains occur only where no unit is needed (need − 2t ≤ 0, 30 rotors). THE BRANCHED COVER, THE SLACK STEPS AND THE UNITS (16:1x EST, read from `date`; records `designsum_beta_n2`, `designsum_beta_n3_sample`, `designsum_beta_n4_sample`, `designsum_covercensus_n2`, all `.2026-11-06e.txt`; `designsum.c` grown by the modes `gram`, `coverrank`, `covercensus` and the tallies `mono`, `units`, `crossing`, `cup alone`). PROVED AT THE DESK — THE BRANCHED COVER: for EVERY rotor k the Z₃-cover X̂_k (a C₀ circle lifts to three circles, a circle with m_c ≠ 0 to ONE circle of three passes) is a four-fold word system on 3N generators; on the ζ²-eigenspace of the deck action a long circle's row is (1 + ζ^{2m_c} + ζ^{4m_c}) L_c = 0, so that eigenspace of the cover's rotor-0 kernel is K_k (and the ζ-eigenspace K_{−k}); the cover's rotor-0 form is deck-invariant, hence block-diagonal β₀ ⊕ β_k ⊕ β_{−k}, and a long circle's cross-pass crossing terms sum to ζ^{m_c} L_c conj L_c = (ζ + [m_c = 2]) L_c conj L_c exactly. Hence dim K₀(X̂_k) = dim K₀ + 2 dim K_k and rank β(X̂_k)|K₀(X̂_k) = slack₀(X) + 2 rank(β_k|K_k) — read on all 2,177,280 two-chord rotors (433,216 honest, 1,744,064 branched), parted on none; the Gauss sums do not factor on a branched cover (Addendum VIII's 256 against 1024, the linear terms), the polar forms do. So K_k/F₄v_k = H¹(X̂_k; F₄)^{ζ²}, v_k the twisted coboundary of the three vertices' eigenvector, and β_k is the cup pairing of twisted 1-cocycles against the cover's fundamental 2-cycle (every edge in four cell-sides) plus the inverse letters' diagonal Σ_g n_g⁻ x_g conj y_g: THE CEILING IS A DUALITY-DEFECT BOUND ON A FOUR-FOLD 2-COMPLEX. The diagonal is load-bearing — on the mixed-orientation two-chord systems its removal moves the rank both ways ("AABaB AB B" at (0,1): 1 → 0; "ABAbab A B" at (1,0): 0 → 1) — so the pairing is the quadratic refinement's, not the cup product's alone. REFUTED: the slack's per-step monotonicity (one more rotation costs at most one unit of the bordered rank) fails on the three two-chord classes at 5/3 — "ABAbab A B", "AABaB AB B", "AAB ABB Ab" read slack 0 → 1 → 0, the first rotation free, the second costing two: the ceiling is a global budget, and a one-step induction is dead. READ instead: every downward step is EXACTLY ONE unit of slack, never two — 20,616 of 2,903,040 steps at two chords, 1,346,220 of 108,000,000 at three, 246,632 of 14,580,000 at four, all between nonzero sums — so a rotation costs at most two units of bordered rank (read, not proved). REFUTED: the decomposition of β into the crossing form plus the rank-one terms λ_c L_c conj L_c of the circles with m_c ≠ 0, with the crossing form alone carrying 2(ρ − ρ_k) − m — its rank reads 0 against 1 ("ABAbab A B" at (1,0)) and 1 against 3 ("AAB ABB Ab" at (1,0)): those terms add rank, they are not a cost. THE UNITS, READ (the separated rotors with need ≥ 1): for A once in each of four circles, A's part of δ = Q_{−k} − Q_k collapses, at either κ, to Σ_{c ∋ A} N(p_c) — the circle's mixed-level pairs and the norm of its twisted row combine into the norm of its label sum p_c over the other generators (proved at the desk) — which on K‴, where Σ_c p_c = π_A = 0, is Addendum VII's four-distinct indicator. On EVERY rotor where the corner theorem leaves a unit (need − 2t = 1: 57,948 at three chords, 6,084 at four) exactly one rotated generator is once-in-four with that locus meeting K‴ (and its F₂-shared part), and on every rotor needing none (need − 2t ≤ 0: 1,732 and 418) no generator is; K‴ Frobenius-stable on all of them. So the separated budget reads need − 2t = n₄, the number of case-XI generators whose four-distinct locus meets K‴, wherever it is nonnegative (equality on all; the seven-chord witness 2 = 2), and the separated case stands on three objects: (I) need − 2t ≤ n₄, a counting statement over the rotated generators' distributions with their interaction through shared unrotated letters; (S) K‴ Frobenius-stable; (II) the n₄ units independent — at n₄ = 1 (S) and the Frobenius lemma already give the unit, since δ|K‴ = δ_{A*} ≢ 0 there; at n₄ ≥ 2 the block structure of 09:28. MIXED: need ≤ 2 through four chords (12 rotors at three, 6 at four), rank ≥ need on all, the mechanism open. THE NEXT OBJECT is (I) at the desk, then (S); the instruments `betacensus` (tallies `units`, `separated remainder`, `mono`), `gram "WORDS" k` (a rotor's kernel basis and Gram matrix), `coverrank`/`covercensus` (the cover's two identities).

THE ONCE-IN-FOUR SEPARATED CASE, PROVED; THE STABILITY READ WHOLE (16:5x EST, read from `date`; records `designsum_beta_n3_allsep`, `designsum_beta_n4_allsep`, `designsum_beta_n5_allsep`, all `.2026-11-06h.txt` — the tallies now on every separated rotor, the zero rotor included; an f- and a g-set of this hour were WITHDRAWN before landing: the instrument's key table of 512 had filled and dropped every later key in silence, caught by a four-chord unit count reading 2,886 against the e-record's 6,084 — the table is 8,192 and refuses by name when full, and every h-record reads COMPLETE). THE COUNTING STATEMENT AT ONE GENERATOR, EXACT. Let A be rotated alone and once in each of four circles c₁..c₄ (case XI), S the free circles' parity rows over the unrotated generators, P_i the R-part of c_i's parity row. Every circle's parity row summed over the system is zero (each generator four times), so π_A = P₁ + P₂ + P₃ + P₄ ∈ span S: t = 0, the sign blind to x_A, K‴ = K″ = ker S. The full rows e_A + P_i give ρ = rank S + 1 + q with q the rank of the three differences P₁ + P_i (i = 2, 3, 4) modulo S, and ρ_k = rank S (no C₀ circle of A), B₁ = 4: need = 2(q + 1) − 4 − 1 = 2q − 3, and since the three differences sum to π_A ∈ S, q ≤ 2 — Addendum VII's need ≤ 1 with its exact value. When q = 2 the pair (P₁ + P₂, P₁ + P₃) is onto F₄² on K‴, so for every x the four values p_i(x) are p₁(x) + {0, a, b, a + b} and the four-distinct locus is the fibre over the six pairs of distinct nonzero (a, b): three eighths of K‴ (the 3/8 of Addendum VII), nonempty; when q ≤ 1 need ≤ −1. THE ALL-ONCE-IN-FOUR SEPARATED CASE. Let A₁..A_m be rotated, separated, each once in each of four circles. Then ρ = rank S + m + q with q the rank of the 3m differences modulo S, ρ_k = rank S, B₁ = 4m, need = 2q − 3m; the π_A are the sums of each generator's three differences, Σ_A π_A ∈ S, t = rank{π_A} modulo S, K‴ = ker(S ∪ {π_A}) is rational (u = 0) and the differences restricted to it have rank q − t; each generator's three restricted differences have rank q_A ≤ 2 (their sum vanishes on K‴), and rank is subadditive: q − t ≤ Σ_A q_A. Hence need − 2t = 2(q − t) − 3m ≤ Σ_A (2q_A − 3) ≤ Σ_A [q_A = 2] = n₄, since 2q_A − 3 ∈ {−3, −1, 1} and, K‴ being rational, q_A = 2 is exactly the nonemptiness of A's four-distinct locus on K‴. THE UNITS ARE INDEPENDENT, AND THE CASE IS PROVED. The freezing slice of a once-in-four generator is K‴ itself (Q_k(x e_A) ≡ 0 at either κ: the four circles' traces, −1 or −(−1)^{N(x)}, multiply to a constant), so S_k = 4^m Σ_{K‴} (−1)^{Q_k}, and the sign is blind to every x_A. Addendum VII's four-distinct identity holds for each such A in this company — the Haar integral over A alone, the other generators' elements fixed, with A's segments' products in Q₈ since A's circles hold no other rotated generator — and the sums over the others' labels factor through the corner: Σ_{x ∈ K‴, δ_A(x) = 1} (−1)^{Q_k(x)} = 0 for every A with a nonempty locus. Now let r = rank(β|K‴) and suppose S_k ≠ 0. A Hermitian form of rank r over F₄ is Σ_{j ≤ r} ℓ_j conj ℓ_j for independent functionals ℓ_j, and Q_k − Σ_j N(ℓ_j) has zero polar form, hence is affine, hence (S_k ≠ 0) in the image: Q_k = Σ_j N(ℓ_j + a_j) + const. Let L = span{ℓ_j} and V_A = F₄-span of A's three differences on K‴ (dimension q_A = 2 for A ∈ 𝒜, the generators with a live locus). If L ∩ V_A = 0 the functionals (P₁ + P₂, P₁ + P₃, ℓ₁, …, ℓ_r) are independent and the locus sum factorizes as (|K‴|/4^{r+2}) · 6 · (−2)^r ≠ 0, against the identity; so every V_A with A ∈ 𝒜 meets L. Put U = Σ_{A ∈ 𝒜} V_A (dimension w₄ ≤ 2n₄) and L′ = L ∩ U: each V_A meets L′, so its image in U/L′ has dimension at most one, dim U/L′ ≤ n₄, and r ≥ dim L′ ≥ w₄ − n₄. With w = q − t = dim Σ_{all A} W_A ≤ w₄ + (m − n₄) and w₄ ≤ 2n₄ ≤ n₄ + m this gives r ≥ w₄ − n₄ ≥ 2w − 3m = need − 2t, hence rank(β|K_k) = 2t + r ≥ need: THE CEILING HOLDS ON EVERY SEPARATED ROTOR WHOSE ROTATED GENERATORS ARE EACH ONCE IN EACH OF FOUR CIRCLES, at every support — the seven-chord witness (two generators, need − 2t = 2 = r) is its first instance beyond one unit. W = 0 for the classification of Hermitian forms over a finite field and the Haar fourth moments; the earnings are the exact count need = 2q − 3, the subadditive account, and the locus-identity argument for the rank. What remains of the separated case is the distributions I–X in company — a generator with a C₀ circle brings a twisted row in place of a parity row, and THE STABILITY READ WHOLE: over ALL separated rotors (need of either sign; 5,000 systems a multiset at three chords, 200 at four, 10 at five, seed 11) the corner kernel K‴ is Frobenius-stable on every rotor with need − 2t ≥ 0 and unstable only at need − 2t ∈ {−1, −2, −3} — 9,536 of 358,720 such rotors at three chords, 1,934 of 28,096 at four, 320 of 2,398 at five, the instability defect u = dim(K‴ + conj K‴) − dim K‴ equal to one on every one of them, always from a C₀ circle of a rotated generator whose segment parities are independent ("A ABBCABCCACB" at k = 2 has K‴ = F₄(0, ζ, 1)); a first reading from the saturated table had put them at −2 alone and is corrected here. The account need − 2t ≤ n₄ − u holds on every cell of the three records (and need − 2t ≤ n₄ − 2u does not: the −1 cells carry u = 1), and on every rotor needing a unit (need − 2t = 1: 1,356, 286 and 32) exactly one rotated generator is once-in-four with its four-distinct locus meeting K‴, u = 0 — the frozen account there reads (D, s) = (0, 1) or (2, 0). THE NEXT OBJECT: the same subadditive account for a generator in distributions I–X — its C₀ circles' twisted rows and their instability u_A against its parity rows, Addendum VII's facts (ii) and (iii) as the local lemmas — and the independence of two four-distinct units.

THE PER-GENERATOR ACCOUNT, READ BY DISTRIBUTION (17:0x EST, read from `date`; records `designsum_beta_n3_pergen`, `designsum_beta_n4_pergen`, `designsum_beta_n5_pergen`, all `.2026-11-06i.txt`, TALLY-COMPLETE). The separated case's general road, set up in the same subadditive form as the once-in-four proof: with F_g the full parity rows of g's circles and T the twisted rows and corner functionals of every rotated generator, need − 2t = 2[rank(S ∪ ⋃F_g) − rank(S ∪ ⋃T_g)] − B₁ − m ≤ Σ_g c_g with c_g := 2 r_g − B₁(g) − 1, r_g the rank of F_g on K‴ ⊕ F₄e_g (the once-in-four case has r_g = 1 + q_g, c_g = 2q_g − 3). But a generator with a C₀ circle brings a twisted row L = Σ_j ζ^{2jκ} P^{(j)} over its level parities; when its kernel is irrational (u_g = rank(all rows ∪ conj T_g) − rank(all rows) = 1) the parity row Σ_j P^{(j)} survives on K‴ and r_g counts it, while in the exact count the twisted row and the parity row cancel — so the honest subadditivity is need − 2t ≤ Σ_g (c_g − 2u_g). READ on 141,348 generator readings over 692,118 rotors (2,000 / 100 / 6 systems a multiset at three, four, five chords, seed 13): need − 2t ≤ Σ_g (c_g − 2u_g) on every rotor, the gap even (0 on 140,056, 2 on 18,838, 4 on 32); on every generator c_g ≤ 1, u_g ≤ 1 and c_g ≤ [δ_g live on K‴] + u_g; c_g = 1 occurs exactly on the once-in-four distributions ((1,0)(1,0)(1,0)(1,0) and its sign variants) with a live four-distinct locus, where it is the unit the proof above supplies, and on the distributions with a (1,1) circle ((1,1)(1,0)(0,1), (1,1)(1,0)(1,0), (1,1)(1,1)) with u_g = 1, where c_g − 2u_g = −1 and no unit is needed; every other distribution — (2,0)-, (2,1)-, (2,2)-, (3,0)-, (3,1)-, (4,0)-type circles in any company — reads c_g ≤ 0. Hence the general separated case stands on two lemmas and the rank argument: (i) need − 2t ≤ Σ_g (c_g − 2u_g); (ii) c_g ≤ [live] + u_g for each of Addendum VII's distributions, read exact here; then need − 2t ≤ n₄ − Σ u_g, and the locus identities of the live generators give rank(β|K‴) ≥ n₄ as above (a live generator with q_A = 1 on an irrational K‴ still forces span{ℓ_j} ∩ V_A ≠ 0, the locus being a union of fibres of one functional). THE NEXT OBJECT: (i) and (ii) at the desk — (i) by the telescoping of the two ranks generator by generator with the conj-closure of each twisted row (its level-parity rows P^{(0)} + P^{(1)}, P^{(1)} + P^{(2)}), (ii) by Addendum VII's facts (ii) and (iii) per distribution — then the mixed case.

THE EXACT FORM OF THE ACCOUNT, AND LEMMA (i′) (17:1x EST, read from `date`; records `designsum_beta_n3_pergen`, `designsum_beta_n4_pergen`, `designsum_beta_n5_pergen`, all `.2026-11-06j.txt`, TALLY-COMPLETE, the i-records re-read with one more key). Write 𝒫 for all the parity rows (rank ρ) and 𝒯 for the K‴ rows (the free and the twisted C₀ rows, the corner functionals; rank ρ_k + t). Adding the twisted rows to the parity rows and peeling the parity rows off generator by generator gives the IDENTITY need − 2t = Σ_g 2 rank(F_g mod (𝒯 ∪ F_{<g})) − 2Δ − B₁ − m with Δ := rank(𝒯 ∪ 𝒫) − ρ, the number of twisted rows and corner functionals independent of every parity row; each summand is at most 2 r_g, so need − 2t ≤ Σ_g c_g − 2Δ exactly, and the sharper account of the paragraph above is the case Δ ≥ Σ_g u_g: LEMMA (i′). READ on every rotor of the three records (no refusal by name): Δ − Σ_g u_g ∈ {0, 1, 2, 3} on 76,904 / 73,164 / 8,754 / 104 rotors, Δ itself 0 to 3. LEMMA R1 (walls and surface pieces with vertex-disjoint cycles, every assignment, exact). Let S be a branch-free closed connected set of polygon faces (every link in exactly two faces) and Δ a decoration of S by pieces P₁, …, P_p, each a face or a connected set of faces off S with every off-S link in exactly two faces of the piece and every S-link in exactly one (walls and surface pieces), the pieces pairwise without a common link, their attachment circles simple cycles of the lattice, pairwise vertex-disjoint, m of them in all. Cut S along the attachment cycles: the regions R₁, …, R_n (n ≤ m + 1), each a connected surface with boundary. Give R_i the spin j_i and P_l the spin κ_l. Then, exactly,

  W = ± Π_i d_{j_i}^{χ(R_i)} a_{j_i}^{F(R_i)} · Π_l d_{κ_l}^{χ(P_l)} a_{κ_l}^{F(P_l)} · Π_b N_b,

the product over the attachment cycles b, each N_b one of ∫χ_{j}(z)χ_{j′}(z)χ_κ(z)dz = 1 or 0 (b's two sides in two regions), ∫χ_j(z)²χ_κ(z)dz = 1 or 0 and ∫χ_j(z²)χ_κ(z)dz = ±1 or 0 (b in one region: its two traversals in two circles of the cut region, or one circle running twice round it; χ_j(z²) = Σ_{k ≤ 2j}(−1)^{2j−k}χ_k(z)), the sign that of (n) on a non-orientable part — WHICH of the last two is the cut region's boundary along b: two circles or one (the desktop seat db5968f8's NOTE VIII ADDENDUM I, e5a6a9e04, correcting its own first reading of 02:3x); in every case |N_b| ≤ 1, which is all the corollary uses. PROOF. A region's interior links lie in exactly two of its faces, so it integrates to its piece formula d^{χ−F} Π_b χ(z_b) (S4′ step 1, the gluing ∫χ_j(AU)χ_j(U⁻¹B)dU = χ_j(AB)/d_j face by face, with the sign of (n) at a cross-cap), and the activities d_j a_j a face turn d^{χ−F} into d^χ a^F; a piece likewise. What is left is one chord a cycle — all but one link of each cycle gauge-fixed through the cycle's own vertices, which no other cycle touches, so the fixings do not interact and the chords are independent variables — under the product of the regions' and the pieces' boundary characters, which factorises over the cycles. ∎ At the uniform assignment (every region at ½, a wall at 1, a surface piece at κ) R1 is S7 and S9. Its label table, taken: two regions ∫χ_{j₁}χ_{j₂}χ_κ; one orientable region ∫χ_j²χ_κ; one non-orientable region, or a one-sided cycle, ∫χ_j(z²)χ_κ(z). NOT READ by that seat then: Lemma R2 (Theorem V at every spin), which is §24 (i), below, and which it read and signed at 03:2x with the sentence the proof wants, entered there.] [AND AT 03:1x THE SAME SEAT, ASKED WHETHER ITS (i) READS THE SAME ON CYCLES OF DIFFERENT PIECES: yes — a cycle's fixings spend the gauge freedom at its own vertices and nothing else, so cycles sharing no vertex, whichever pieces they bound, have disjoint fixings and one free holonomy each; SIGNED FOR THE WHOLE DECORATION, which is R1 as it stands.]

LEMMA R1′ (the regions' sizes). FOR VERTEX-DISJOINT CYCLES: a region bounded by wall cycles alone has at least four plaquettes, and five unless it is an annulus between two of them: with m_R walls on its boundary, F(R) + m_R ≥ 6 (R and its walls form a closed even set, the least being the cube's six faces, (m′) (1)), and 4F(R) = (boundary links) + 2(interior links) ≥ 4m_R + 2(F(R) − 1) (a connected region of F faces has at least F − 1 interior links), so F(R) ≥ max(6 − m_R, 2m_R − 1) ≥ 4, with equality only at m_R = 2. A region bounded by a surface piece's cycle may be one plaquette (the cup's, N0 (i)). [READ 2026-11-06 02:18 by the desktop seat db5968f8 (its message of 03:0x): the counting arm SIGNED (the boundary links each in one plaquette of R and at least 4m_R, the interior in two, a spanning tree of the faces' adjacency giving F − 1 interior links); the other arm at m_R = 1 is contactmin's own known answer, no closed set below six holding a planted plaquette, SIGNED by machine; at m_R = 2 that seat sharpens it — F = 3 would need two interior links joining three plaquettes into a tube, which the cubic lattice has not, a tube's girth being four — so four, the cube's band; at m_R ≥ 3 the counting arm is the larger.] IN GENERAL (cycles meeting at vertices) a wall-bounded region may be ONE plaquette — N0 (ii), four walls on its four edges — and then its four boundary pieces are distinct and each covers exactly one of its edges: two adjacent edges of a plaquette span that plaquette and two opposite ones the plaquette between them, so a wall covering two edges of a one-plaquette region would be the region itself. A region of two plaquettes (a domino) has six boundary links, pairwise in one face or collinear at every vertex, so six distinct walls each covering one; a region of F ≥ 2 plaquettes has at least six boundary links; and what the charging in R4 uses of a wall-only region is ε_h(F(R))/m(R) ≤ ε_h(1)/8, which a region of F ≥ 2 satisfies whatever m(R) is, ε_h(2) = 1.5·10⁻⁵ against ε_h(1)/8 = 6.9·10⁻⁴ — so the count of links a wall covers enters nowhere.

COROLLARY R1″ (the half-integral classes with vertex-disjoint cycles, priced per cycle). Across each attachment cycle the two sides' spins differ by an integer (the pieces at integer spins, as the dressing has them), and the regions are joined through the cycles, so every assignment has all its regions half-integral or all integral. The INTEGRAL assignments are the dressing of a leaf at integer spins — the third input of THE PROOF, "rests at raised or integer spin" — and are not priced here. The HALF-INTEGRAL assignments other than the uniform one, against the dressing's own bound for the configuration (Σ_{κ ≥ 1} d_κ a_κ a wall, Σ_κ d_κ^{χ(P)} a_κ^{F(P)} a surface piece, on W(S)), cost at most Π_R (1 + ε_h(F(R))) − 1 with ε_h(F) := Σ_{j ≥ 3/2, j half-integral} ((2j + 1)/2)(a_j/a_½)^F — a region at j costs (d_j/2)^{χ(R)}(a_j/a_½)^{F(R)} ≤ ((2j + 1)/2)(a_j/a_½)^{F(R)} against its ½ value, χ(R) ≤ 1 for a connected surface with boundary — and with vertex-disjoint cycles that product is at most Π_walls (1 + ε_h(4))² · Π_{surface pieces} (1 + ε_h(1))^{2s}: charge each region to one piece on its boundary, a surface piece if there is one (it borders at most 2s regions, each of at least one plaquette), else a wall (a wall borders at most two regions, each of at least four plaquettes by R1′). ∎ At β = 0.3650 (a_½ = 0.090748, a_{3/2} = 2.5076·10⁻⁴, a_{5/2} = 2.78·10⁻⁷): ε_h(4) = 1.17·10⁻¹⁰, ε_h(1) = 5.54·10⁻³. The general case is R4 below.

THE VERTEX-NORM THEOREM AT EVERY SPIN is §24's proof, part (i), on this page since 2026-10-29: for a closed complex D whose plaquettes carry spins j_q, I(D) := ∫Π_q χ_{j_q}(U_q) dU expands with the Haar projector onto the invariants of ⊗_q V_{j_q} at every link, the characters are real, the contraction pairwise, and |I(D)| ≤ Π_v √S(G_v) with every edge of G_v carrying its plaquette's spin. The second landing restated it as "Lemma R2"; the name is withdrawn and §24 (i) is cited. [READ 2026-11-06 03:2x by the desktop seat db5968f8, SIGNED, with the sentence the proof wants at its third place (‖A_v‖² = S(G_v)), where Theorem V's written step used the real orthogonal basis of spin 1, which half-integer spins do not have: no real basis is used — with P_ℓ = Σ_λ |u^λ⟩⟨u^λ| a tail link enters A_v through u^λ and a head link through ū^λ, Σ_λ A_v(λ)\overline{A_v(λ)} gives per link P_ℓ[x, x′] or P_ℓ[y′, y], and each corner contracts the ket and bra copies to the trace of the two links' matrices in the plaquette's traversal order, χ_{j_q}(O_ℓ⁻¹O_{ℓ′}) with the links oriented away from v, whatever the plaquette's sense; S(G_v) does not depend on the per-link orientation, U → U⁻¹ being measure-preserving link by link. Also checked by that seat: the no-self-loop condition is the lattice's, the Frobenius chain is spin-blind, and a link whose invariant space is empty gives I(D) = 0.]

LEMMA R3 (THE MIXED STAR BOUND). Let v be a vertex of S and G_v the vertex graph of S ∪ Δ at v (its vertices the links at v, its edges the faces at v, each at its face's spin). S's faces at v form c_v cycles (c_v = 1, or 2 at a pinch: eight links at a vertex of ℤ⁴ hold at most two cycles of three); a wall at v is a chord joining its two spokes (S-links); a surface piece's faces at v form paths through off-S links from spoke to spoke (its off-S links in two of its faces, its spokes in one) and cycles that touch no spoke; the pieces pairwise link-disjoint, so each spoke carries one chord or one path; m_v ≤ 4 chords and paths in all. The 2m_v spokes cut the cycles into arcs, an arc's L edges all in one region at that region's spin j_a (adjacent arcs may share a region). Let G′ be the graph on the 2m_v spokes with one edge per arc, at the arc's spin, and one chord per chord or path, at the piece's spin — trivalent. Then

  (a) S(G_v) = Π_arcs d_{j_a}^{1−L_a} · Π_paths d_κ^{1−L′} · Π_{off-S cycles} d_κ^{2−L} · S(G′)
    (§24 (iii)'s contraction at every spin: an interior link of an arc or a path lies in two faces at one spin and ∫χ_j(AU)χ_j(U⁻¹B)dU = χ_j(AB)/d_j);
  (b) S(G′) = Σ_ι |⟨Γ′(ι)⟩|², the sum over orthonormal bases of the intertwiner spaces at the spokes of the squared moduli of the spin-network evaluations (each spoke's Haar integral is the projector Σ_ι |ι⟩⟨ι| onto the invariants of its legs' tensor product, and the contraction of the projectors over the edges is this sum; W = 0); at a spoke with arcs j_in, j_out and chord κ the space is one-dimensional when κ ∈ j_in ⊗ j_out and empty otherwise, so S(G′) = |⟨Γ′⟩|² or 0;
  (c) THE PATH BOUND. Let P be a Hamiltonian path of G′ (its spokes p₁, …, p_{2m} in order, its edges e_k = p_k p_{k+1}), and call an interior spoke p_k a START when its non-P edge's other end comes later along P and an END when it came earlier. Then

    |⟨Γ′⟩| ≤ 2^{1−m} · Π_{e ∈ P} (2/d_e)^{π(e)/2},   π(e_k) := [p_k is an END] + [p_{k+1} is a START],

  the two end spokes paying nothing. So S(G′) ≤ 2^{2−2m} · min_P Π_{e ∈ P} (2/d_e)^{π(e)} ≤ 2^{2−2m}: THE MIXED STAR IS AT MOST THE UNIFORM BOUND, with a factor 2/d_e for every payment on a raised edge. On one cycle with chords the Hamiltonian paths along the cycle are its cuts, the cut arc being the edge left out; there a START pays its in-arc and an END its out-arc, and the first spoke after the cut is a start, the last an end.

PROOF of (c). ⟨Γ′⟩ is the composite, from p₁ to p_{2m}, of the spokes' intertwiners: ι_{p₁} a vector in the tensor product of its three legs' spaces (a map ℂ → V_{e₁} ⊗ V_{c} ⊗ V_{c′}, of operator norm its length, one); an interior ι_{p_k} a map from the incoming edge's space, tensored with the spaces of the non-P edges open at that point, to the outgoing edge's — at a START ι: V_{e_{k−1}} → V_{e_k} ⊗ V_κ, at an END ι: V_{e_{k−1}} ⊗ V_κ → V_{e_k}, the non-P edge's two legs contracted by the invariant pairing; ι_{p_{2m}} a map to ℂ, of norm one. At a START ι†ι ∈ End(V_{e_{k−1}}) is equivariant, hence a scalar, of trace ‖ι‖²_F = 1: ‖ι‖_op = d_{e_{k−1}}^{−1/2}; at an END ιι† = id/d_{e_k} likewise and ‖ι‖_op = d_{e_k}^{−1/2}. So each interior spoke pays one adjacent edge of P, a START its in-edge and an END its out-edge: the payments π(e). The 2m − 2 payments give Π d_{paid}^{−1/2} = 2^{1−m} Π (2/d_{paid})^{1/2}, and |⟨Γ′⟩| ≤ the product of the operator norms. ∎ At m = 1 (one wall or one path) S(G′) = 1 exactly, so (a) is the piece formula at the vertex and R1's region factors are the stars' — the cup's vertex reads 1/8 = (1/4)·(d_{3/2}/2)^{1−2}.

THE KNOWN ANSWERS OF R3 (star_mixed.c; exact rationals from three primes ≡ 1 mod 20, the fourth verifying, a fourth prime reconstructing where the two-prime bound refused). Two chords side by side, all arcs at 3/2: 1/16 = (1/4)(1/2)², the between-arc paid twice, equality; the between-arcs at ½ and the chords' own arcs at 3/2: 1/4 — no payment on a chord's own arc, equality; the between-arcs at 3/2: 1/16, equality. On the 8-cycle, four chords pairwise crossing: uniform 169/46656, c = 13/27; all at 3/2, 418609/46656000000; alternating, 1/746496; two crossing pairs: uniform 1/5184 = 2^{−6}/81 ((−1/3)² on two crossings), the middle arcs raised 25/82944, all raised 14641/207360000; four chords side by side: uniform 1/64 (c = 1, the bound attained), the chords' own arcs raised 1/64 (no payment, equality), the between-arcs raised 1/4096 = 2^{−6}(1/2)⁶, three between-arcs paid twice, equality. AT A PINCH (two cycles of S-faces, the chords between them): the prism — two spokes a cycle, the two chords crossing between — reads 1/9 at every assignment of its four arcs (uniform, one, two or four at 3/2): the path through both cycles pays the two chords at κ = 1, (1/4)(2/3)² = 1/9, exactly, and a raised arc gains nothing there; two 3-cycles with three between-chords, uniform 1/81, one arc raised 1/324, all raised 1/8100; two 4-cycles with four, 49/11664 and 344569/2916000000. LEMMA R3′ (THE PAYMENT LEMMA, by exhaustion). Call an arc of G′ ELIGIBLE when its two spokes belong to different chords. (The arc between a chord's own two spokes: for a plaquette wall the face it spans would be the wall itself, so a wall's own arcs have L ≥ 2 and never hold a one-plaquette region; a path's own arc may have L = 1 — the cup's four corners — and receives no payment, as the readings above show, which is the exact formula's d_j^{χ}: a cup-bounded region costs its full factor. THE SECOND HALF AS WRITTEN IS FOR PLAQUETTE LEAVES AND PLAQUETTE WALLS, the certificate's: on a polygon wall of the twelve-term gas the two spokes can be adjacent with a square of the rest between them, that square's corner is then a chord's own arc and ineligible, and the halving of R4 does not reach it; the polygon case re-reads R3′ with that arc admitted.) (i) ONE CYCLE: for every chord diagram on 2m ≤ 8 spokes and every nonempty set A of eligible arcs there is a cut with Σ_{a ∈ A} π(a) ≥ |A|/2 — checked over the 1 + 3 + 15 + 105 diagrams, every cut and every set (14,775 pairs at m = 4): the minimum is exactly ½, at two adjacent arcs of a crossing (`neck_payments.awk`). (ii) A PINCH: the reduced graph has two cycles of spokes (two or more each: one spoke is a tadpole, its star 0) and chords within and between; with exactly one between-chord G′ has a bridge and S(G′) = 0, with none the cycles are independent and (i) applies to each; with two or more, every such diagram (266 of them) has a Hamiltonian path, and against the dressing's local factor at a pinch, 2^{4−2m} for the two cycles, the path bound's 2^{2−2m} is a gain of two payments' worth before any payment: for every nonempty set A of eligible arcs there is a path P with 2 + Σ_{a ∈ A ∩ P} π(a) ≥ |A|/2 — checked over every diagram, every Hamiltonian path and every set (41,494 pairs): the minimum is exactly ½, at the prism with all four arcs raised, whose star the engine reads at 1/9 whatever the arcs' spins (`neck_payments_pinch.c`). ∎ [A THIRD ROAD AT THE PINCH, 04:52: the desktop seat 26d8e019 / iii-bb's NOTE X (910b54293; cp_wallpairs.c, its record and gate 6e88b322b, 7 arms / 7 teeth) reads by the word road, exact, every pair of lone walls on the closed fours of the twelve-term gas — 1,284 orientable pairs MET (a sphere pair q = 1/9 at two crossings, a torus pair q = 1), the orientable value on all 3,672 non-orientable pairs — and at the 612 pairs sharing the pinch of a pinched sphere, both walls through the pinch with a spoke on each cycle: EACH WALL ALONE ZERO and the pair +1/3, on every one. The star road's reading of the same: a lone between-chord is R3′(ii)'s tadpole (one spoke a cycle, the star 0, Schur's bridge), and the pair is the prism at 1/9 = |c|², the magnitude the star gives, the sign the word road's; the factorisation (3a₁)^k Π c(D_v) is S8's on a manifold rest and not claimed at a pinch, where the dressing uses only the bound, which both roads keep.] (The diagrams are a vertex of ℤ⁴'s whole supply, since its eight links admit at most four chords.)

COROLLARY R4 (THE REGION FACTORS, AND THE PRICE OF THE SECOND HALF). Let Δ be a decoration of S by walls and branch-free surface pieces, pairwise link-disjoint, the attachment cycles ARBITRARY (meeting at vertices, a cycle through a vertex twice, at pinches of S), and σ a half-integral assignment to the regions — half-odd, ½, 3/2, …, by parity: the base is ½ and every chord and path is at an integer κ, which κ ∈ j_in ⊗ j_out preserves across every spoke, so d_R ≥ 4 on a raised region and (2/d_e)^{π/2} ≤ 2^{−π/2} on a paid raised arc (the premise the desktop seat db5968f8 asked to be stated, 04:0x) — the pieces at integer spins κ. Then

  |W(Δ, σ)| ≤ [2^{χ(S)} a_½^{F(S)} · Π_P d_κ^{χ_cut(P)} a_κ^{F(P)}] · Π_R (d_{j_R}/2)^{χ_cut(R)} (a_{j_R}/a_½)^{F(R)} · 2^{−N₁(σ)},

χ(S) the leaf's Euler characteristic as its exact weight has it (a pinch resolved), χ_cut that of a region or piece cut open along its boundary (a connected surface with boundary: χ_cut ≤ 1), N₁(σ) the number of raised one-plaquette regions bounded by walls alone. PROOF. §24 (i): |I(D)| ≤ Π_v √S(G_v). At a vertex interior to a region at j, √S = d_j^{(2−d_v)/2} a cycle of S-faces; at a vertex of a piece off S, d_κ^{(2−d_v)/2}; at a vertex on the cycles, R3 with the path of R3′ taken on A_v := the corners at v of the raised one-plaquette wall-bounded regions (every corner of such a region is eligible, its two edges being in different walls): √S(G_v) ≤ 2^{c′_v − m_v} Π_arcs d^{(1−L)/2} Π_paths d_κ^{(1−L′)/2} Π_{off-S cycles} d_κ^{(2−L)/2} · 2^{−Σ_{A_v} π/2}, c′_v the number of components of G′ (one where a between-chord joins the two cycles of a pinch, else c_v), and R3′ gives Σ_v [Σ_{A_v} π + 2(c_v − c′_v)] ≥ Σ_v |A_v|/2 = 2N₁. The powers of two collect to Π_v 2^{c_v − n_v/2} = 2^{χ(S) − F(S)} with S's pinches resolved; the rest by region: Σ_{v interior} (2 − d_v)/2 + Σ_{corners} (1 − L)/2 = V_int − E_int = V_cut − E = χ_cut(R) − F(R), by 4F = 2E_int + E_bdy and the cut boundary's having as many vertices as links; the same for a piece. Multiply by the activities d_j a_j a face. ∎ The bracket is the dressing's own bound for Δ at the pieces' spins (a wall Σ_κ d_κ a_κ, a surface piece of s plaquettes Σ_κ d_κ a_κ^s, χ_cut ≤ 1). Summing over the half-integral σ other than the uniform one and charging each raised region to one piece on its boundary — a surface piece when there is one, else the four walls of a one-plaquette region in equal parts, else the m(R) ≥ 3 walls of a larger one in equal parts — puts on the activities: a wall (1 + ε_h(1)/8)⁸ (at most eight regions border a wall, two faces at each of its four links; a wall-only one-plaquette region costs ε_h(1)/2 by N₁ and has four walls; every other wall-only region costs at most ε_h(2)/3 a wall, below ε_h(1)/8); a surface piece of s plaquettes (1 + ε_h(1))^{4s+4} (at most 2E_bdy(P) regions border it and E_bdy(P) = 4s − 2E_int(P) ≤ 2s + 2). So the second half's factors are μ_wall = (1 + ε_h(1)/8)⁸ − 1 on z₁ and μ_piece(s) = (1 + ε_h(1))^{4s+4} − 1 on z_surf(s): at β = 0.3650, μ_wall = 0.556 per cent; μ_piece(2) = 6.8 per cent, μ_piece(5) = 14 per cent — absorbed at every s, the certificate pricing a piece at max(z_surf(s)(1 + μ_piece), z_br(s)) and reading, at its best α, z_br/z_surf = 3.00 at s = 2, 20.3 at 3, 79.6 at 4, 410 at 5 (its own activities, read 03:2x). Without the payment lemma the one-plaquette regions cost ε_h(1) and a wall (1 + ε_h(1)/4)⁸ − 1 = 1.11 per cent. **(s) THE THIRD INPUT, ITS RAISED RESTS: THE DRESSING OF A LEAF AT ANY SINGLE SPIN IS BOUNDED BY THE DRESSING AT ½ — THE PAYMENT IDENTITY 2^{2−2m}(2/d)^{2m−2} = d^{2−2m} AT EVERY VERTEX, THE ONE-SWITCH PATH AT A PINCH — AND THE MIXED RAISED ASSIGNMENTS AT EVERY BASE COST LESS THAN THE SECOND HALF'S FACTORS ALREADY ON z₁ AND z_surf: "RESTS AT RAISED OR INTEGER SPIN" LEAVES THE THIRD INPUT FOR WALLS AND BRANCH-FREE SURFACE PIECES, THE CERTIFICATE UNCHANGED (2026-11-06 04:31 EST, read from `date` in the command that wrote this line; the trunk seat; a desk corollary of (r)'s R3 and R4, written after the second half landed and before it was read as such; the instrument `STDLIB/build/aperanton/neck_ratios.awk` on the certificate's own Bessel library, its record `neck_ratios.2026-11-06a.txt`, landed 2c3ef4329).**

WHAT THE CERTIFICATE ALLOTS. Its leaf term is 4σ(F)ũ^F e^{2α̃F} with σ(F) = 1 + Σ_{j ≥ 1} d_j²(a_j/u)^F over every spin j ≥ 1, half-odd and integer, and ũ = u e^{κ_U}: a leaf at spin j is given its own weight up to d_j² a_j^F and THE SAME DRESSING e^{κ_U F}, the root of the dressing recursion at the ½ activities z₁ = Σ_κ d_κ a_κ (1 + μ_wall), z_surf(s)(1 + μ_piece(s)), z_br(s). Corollary U (M1's Lemma S) prices the leaf's spins before decoration and says so; THE PROOF's third input — "the dressing's joint decoration bound on contact rests, on rests at raised or integer spin and for branched decorations at higher spin" — is the claim that the ½ root dresses the raised leaves too. Here that claim is proved for the rests the second half reaches.

COROLLARY R5 (the dressing of a rest at one spin). Let S be a branch-free closed set of plaquettes, j₀ ≥ ½ any spin, and Δ a decoration of S by walls and branch-free surface pieces, pairwise link-disjoint, the attachment cycles arbitrary, every region at j₀ and the pieces at integer spins κ (κ ∈ j₀ ⊗ j₀). Then

  |W(Δ, σ_{j₀})| ≤ d_{j₀}^{χ(S)} a_{j₀}^{F(S)} · Π_P d_κ^{χ_cut(P)} a_κ^{F(P)}

— the ½ dressing's own bound for Δ (R4's bracket) with the leaf's weight at j₀ in place of 2^{χ}a_½^F; so the sum over decorations of the leaf at j₀ is at most d_{j₀}^{χ(S)}a_{j₀}^{F(S)} times the dressing's generating function at the ½ activities, which the certificate's root bounds by e^{κ_U F}. PROOF. §24 (i): |I(D)| ≤ Π_v √S(G_v), and R3 (a) at every vertex on the cycles. At a vertex interior to the leaf, √S = d_{j₀}^{(2−d_v)/2}. At a vertex whose figure is one cycle: every arc is at j₀, so every one of the path bound's 2m − 2 payments falls on an arc at j₀, and R3 (c) reads S(G′) ≤ 2^{2−2m}(2/d_{j₀})^{2m−2} = d_{j₀}^{2−2m}; with (a), √S(G_v) ≤ d_{j₀}^{(2m−n_v)/2}·d_{j₀}^{1−m}·Π_paths d_κ^{(1−L′)/2} = d_{j₀}^{(2−n_v)/2}·Π_paths d_κ^{(1−L′)/2}: the bare star at j₀ times the paths' factors, the chords costing nothing — at ½ this is R4's bracket, and the identity is the second half's payments spent on the leaf's own spin. At a pinch (two cycles of S-faces at v) with between-chords: with exactly one the reduced graph has a bridge and S(G′) = 0; with two or more, take the Hamiltonian path with ONE switch — from the spoke next to a between-chord's end u on the first cycle round that cycle to u, across the chord to w, from w round the second cycle — which exists whenever a between-chord does (a cycle's Hamiltonian path from a spoke's neighbour to the spoke is the cycle less one arc); its payments fall on arcs at j₀ except at most two on the chord edge, each 1/d_κ ≤ 1/3, so S(G′) ≤ d_{j₀}^{−(2m−2−p_c)} d_κ^{−p_c} = d_{j₀}^{4−2m}·(d_{j₀}/d_κ)^{p_c}/d_{j₀}² ≤ d_{j₀}^{4−2m} for p_c ≤ 2 — the two cycles' bare value, whatever j₀. Without a between-chord the cycles are independent and the one-cycle case applies to each. Collect over the vertices as in R4: Π_v √S_v ≤ d_{j₀}^{χ(S)−F(S)}Π_P d_κ^{χ_cut(P)−F(P)} (the leaf's pinches resolved in χ(S), as its exact weight has them); the activities d a a face give the claim. ∎

COROLLARY R5′ (the mixed assignments at a base). Let σ assign to the regions of Δ spins of one parity, j₀ the least of them (the BASE), every region at some j ≥ j₀ of that parity. Then |W(Δ, σ)| is at most R5's bound at j₀ times Π_R (d_{j_R}/d_{j₀})^{χ_cut(R)}(a_{j_R}/a_{j₀})^{F(R)} times the gains of R4 read at base j₀ — a payment on a raised arc of spin j′ now worth d_{j₀}/d_{j′} in S — and R3′ gives every raised one-plaquette region bounded by walls two payments. SETTING. Call v a SINGLE contact when the two cycles through ℓ share no other spoke at v, a DOUBLE contact when they share two. After R3 (a)'s contraction of the arcs, the reduced graph G′ at a single contact is two cycles on their spokes glued at ℓ — a BOUQUET — with the chords and paths of the decoration, a chord at ℓ allowed (a wall on the contact link, its other spoke on one of the sheets).

A. THE MISCITATION. R6's proof ends "Lemma M then bounds (2/3)^{N₄}(1/3)^{N₆}Σ_ρ 2^{χ(T_ρ)} by 4·2^{b₁(G)+N₆}". It does not. Lemma M (M1 WORK.md §3) resolves by Creutz only the four-fold chords off a spanning forest T of the contact graph, CUTS T ∪ G₆ by S11 and reads each cut piece exactly, ±2^{χ(P)−|P|}Π_b χ_½(z_b), |χ_½| ≤ 2: the forest links' three pairings are never summed in absolute value, and its 4 at a forest is S6's signed exact value. The sum R6 forms is bounded, for a Z₂-minimal leaf, only by 4·2^{N₄}5^{N₆} — every full resolution is one connected closed surface (S12 (a)), 2^{χ} ≤ 4, and (2/3)^{N₄}3^{N₄}(1/3)^{N₆}15^{N₆} = 2^{N₄}5^{N₆} — and it is not the allotment. At a single four-fold link ℓ (the census' double contacts, N₄ = 1, exact value 4 by S6) the three pairings are the one natural at an endpoint v, whose sheet Σ_σ is a sphere (forced by the value, below), and two crossed ones, each merging the two corner cycles through ℓ at every endpoint where it is crossed. If the four faces pair the same way at both ends — ℓ UNTWISTED — each crossed resolution loses two vertices, χ = 0, and the absolute sum is (2/3)(4 + 1 + 1) = 4, the allotment, with the signs +, +, + forced by the value (χ(Σ_σ) = 1 would give at most 2). If they pair differently — ℓ TWISTED: at v′ the corner cycles run A₁…B₁ and B₂…A₂ — the three resolutions read 2^{χ} = 4, 4, 2, the signs +, +, − forced, and the absolute sum is 20/3 = (5/3)·4: the first term of the series 4(5/3)^k the header records, read here on one link. In general the merge count gives the absolute sum (2/3)^{N₄}·4·(1 + 2·¼)^{N₄} = 4 exactly on a forest with no twisted link and no vertex at which two corner cycles share two spokes (there the doubly crossed pairing may keep both cycles and the count fails). So R6 proves the dressing of a contact rest at the allotment for that class, decorations off the contact links, and at 2^{N₄}5^{N₆} times the allotment in general — not what (t) said. The desktop seat's remark on (t), that the discarded signs are "already paid" by the certificate's 4·N_c(F)σ(F)ũ^F, is corrected with it: at a forest the allotment is the signed value and pays for nothing beyond itself; that seat wrote in the same message that it had not re-derived Lemma M's constant, and its signature does not cover the step that was wrong.

B. THE CHANNEL BASIS CARRIES d_J. The orthonormal basis of Inv(½^{⊗4}) at a four-fold spoke in the scheme (A₁A₂ → J, B₁B₂ → J) is ι_J = √d_J times the composite of two Frobenius-normalised trivalent vertices joined by the J edge — the composite has norm d_J^{−1/2}, ι†ι = id/d_J at each vertex — so R3 (b) reads S(G_v) = Σ_J d_J|⟨Γ′_J⟩|² with Γ′_J the trivalent network, and with a wall at the spoke Σ d_{J₁}d_{J₂}|⟨Γ′_{J₁J₂}⟩|². R7's proof dropped the d_J: the joined channel is at most 3·2^{−2m}Π(2/d)^{π}, THREE SIXTEENTHS of 2^{4−2m} and not a sixteenth, and with a wall at ℓ the (1,1) channel 9·2^{−2−2m}, NINE SIXTY-FOURTHS and not one; R7's conclusion stands, ¼ + 3/16 + 9/64 = 37/64 < 1. The engine reads it exactly: the bouquet of two squares with one between-sheet chord is 1/48 = (1/16)·3·(¼)(2/3)² — the arcs' 1/16, d₁ = 3, the path bound of the joined network with both payments on the J edge — where without the 3 the sheets-apart channel, a bridge at one chord, would leave 1/144. (u)'s "3/2 of its activity" for a wall on a double-contact link is the ratio of two absolute resolution sums, six terms at ½ against three at 2/3, and is 3/2 of the allotment only in A's merge-count class.

C. THE CHANNEL EXPANSION (LEMMA R9). Every link of X ∪ Δ but the contact links carries a rank-one projector — Inv(j ⊗ j) and Inv(½ ⊗ ½ ⊗ κ) are lines — so expanding each contact link's projector in its orthonormal channel basis, §24 (i)'s contraction before its Cauchy–Schwarz step is an identity:

  I(X ∪ Δ, σ) = Σ_J Π_ℓ d_{J_ℓ} · Π_v ⟨Γ′_v(J)⟩,

the sum over J ∈ {0, …, 2j}^{contact links} (the four faces at a contact link are at one spin j: the regions are X cut along the attachment cycles, which avoid the contact links), Γ′_v(J) the trivalent network at v with each contact spoke split by its channel, each vertex's network the one R3 (a) reduces. At a vertex v where the two corner cycles through ℓ share no other spoke and no decoration joins them — no wall with a spoke on each, no piece with a spoke on each — a channel J_ℓ ≥ 1 makes its J edge a bridge of Γ′_v(J), and a closed network with a bridge of spin ≥ 1 vanishes (cut it: either side is an invariant of V_J, which is zero): ⟨Γ′_v(J)⟩ = 0. THEOREM. Let X be a Z₂-minimal contact leaf whose contact graph is a forest (H1), at no vertex of which two corner cycles share two spokes (H2), and Δ, σ a decoration by walls and branch-free surface pieces and a half-odd assignment with no decoration joining two distinct corner cycles at a contact vertex (H3). Then every term with J ≠ 0 vanishes — the links with J_ℓ ≥ 1 form a subforest, nonempty with a leaf vertex, at which its one channel edge is a bridge, and a twisted link is killed at the end whose natural pairing the basis is — and

  |W(X ∪ Δ, σ)| = |Π_v ⟨Γ_v(0)⟩| ≤ 2^{χ(Σ_σ)} a_½^{F(X)} · Π_P d_κ^{χ_cut(P)} a_κ^{F(P)} · Π_R (d_{j_R}/2)^{χ_cut(R)}(a_{j_R}/a_½)^{F(R)} · 2^{−N₁(σ)},

Σ_σ the closed sheet of the chosen pairings (connected by S12 (a), χ ≤ 2; at a twisted link the far end's corner cycles merge in it), by §24 (i), R3, R3′, R4 and R5′ at Σ_σ's vertices — THE ALLOTMENT TIMES THE BRACKET, the decorations anywhere: a wall or a piece with a spoke on a contact link attaches, in the one surviving sheets-apart channel, to the cycle its other spokes lie on (R7's (1,0) and (0,1) readings), and a raised region at a contact link has its four faces at one spin and the channel J = 0 at it. ∎ So the sum over the decorations and assignments of that kind is at most the allotment times the dressing's generating function at the ½ activities with the second half's factors: the certificate's one root dresses them.

D. WHAT IT COVERS. The census' 3,840 pinches at F = 20 (no contact link: R3′(ii) and R4 alone), its 576 at F = 18 and 960 at F = 20 with one four-fold link (H1 and H2 automatic at N₄ = 1), [CORRECTED in (w) A on the lists, 05:54: the pinches have ONE four-fold link and are twisted every one, the 960 have TWO; all in the class by the refined criterion of (w) B, measured]  every tail leaf with N₄ = 1, and every forest leaf satisfying H2 — their dressing at the allotment for every decoration and assignment satisfying H3; at N₄ = 1 it suffices that ONE endpoint of ℓ carry no between-sheet decoration, the one channel needing both. WHAT REMAINS. (i) H3's failures: a subforest G₁ of channels at J ≥ 1 survives only when every leaf vertex of G₁ is rescued by a between-sheet decoration, its term Π_ℓ d_{J_ℓ}Π_v⟨Γ′_v⟩ with, at a rescued single contact, |⟨Γ′_v(1)⟩| ≤ 2^{−m}Π(2/d)^{π/2} against the sheet's 2^{1−m₁−m₂} — the engine's 1/48 the first number — and the count of surviving subforests tree by tree (interval partitions on a path, not Bell numbers); (ii) H2's failures, the vertices where two corner cycles share two spokes, whose (1,1) channel reads 1/8 beside the (0,0) channel's 1/8 (the double contact of (t)), and the leaves with b₁(G) ≥ 1, where K_r lives; (iii) the branched decorations at higher spin and the junction's pinched rows, as before. The next act is (i), the between-sheet term's bound with the subforest count, and an instrument reading, on the census' 46,816 contact leaves at F = 22, each four-fold link's pairing at its two ends and each vertex's shared spokes: N₄, the twisted links and the H2 vertices, by member.

B. R9 REFINED — THE BRIDGE END. The kill is local. Fix one pairing σ_ℓ per four-fold link, the same partition of its four faces at both ends (at an end where it is not the natural pairing the two corner cycles through ℓ merge, and the merged cycle counts against every other contact spoke at that vertex); at a vertex v THE CYCLE GRAPH has the corner cycles of σ as nodes and each contact spoke as an edge joining the cycles of its two passages, a loop when they coincide. THEOREM (R9, refined). If some σ gives every four-fold link an end at which its edge is a bridge of the cycle graph (H2*), and no decoration joins two distinct corner cycles at a contact vertex (H3), then every term with J ≠ 0 vanishes and |W(X ∪ Δ, σ)| = |Π_v⟨Γ_v(0)⟩| ≤ 2^{χ(Σ_σ)} a_½^F × R4's bracket, Σ_σ the sheet of σ, connected by S12 (a). PROOF. Take J ≠ 0, a link ℓ with J_ℓ ≥ 1, and its bridge end v. In Γ′_v(J) the corner cycles are connected pieces, joined only by the channel edges of the spokes with J ≥ 1 (a J = 0 spoke is two singlet nodes, joining nothing) and, by H3, by no decoration; ℓ's channel edge is a bridge of the cycle graph, hence of its subgraph on the J ≥ 1 spokes, hence of Γ′_v(J); a bridge of spin ≥ 1 kills (cut it: either side is an invariant of V_J, zero). No forest is needed — the kill is at one end of one link. ∎ (v) C's H1 and H2 are the case in which every link's natural end is such a bridge; the desktop seat's uniform choice on a forest — root each tree, give every link the pairing natural at its child end, the deepest vertex of a nonempty J-subforest a leaf whose one J ≠ 0 edge is its parent edge, natural there — is one σ of H2*; H2* is decided by exhaustion over the 3^{N₄} choices. [THE CRITERION IS SUFFICIENT, NOT THE KILL ITSELF, 07:18: a channel word dies at an end of one of its links where that link's edge is a bridge of the cycle graph restricted to the WORD'S OWN SPOKES at that vertex (the J = 0 spokes deleted), so the exact criterion is over the words' supports; H2* asks for a bridge of the full cycle graph, which is stronger. An open crease chain — four-fold links in a path, every interior vertex a double contact — has no bridge end on its middle links in the full graph, yet every nonzero word dies at the chain's two lone ends or at the end of its own support, so it is in the class while contact_census.c would call it OUTSIDE; a closed crease cycle (Object E's square) is outside under both. The census' two-link leaves are corners: 640 with the two four-fold links perpendicular at a shared vertex, 320 disjoint, none collinear — a crease in ℤ⁴ turns at every vertex, as the desktop seat's corner-plaquette fact has it.] [READ 2026-11-06 06:01 by the desktop seat db5968f8 (its message of 06:0x), SIGNED with the statement completed: (1) the merge at a non-natural end is EXACT, not a charge — each of the spoke's two trivalent nodes is a vertex of the local network where an A-face and a B-face meet, whatever channel the spoke carries, so A and B are one piece there in every term, merges compose, and the cycle graph under σ is the quotient by all of v's non-natural spokes at once; (2) the graph's EDGES must include the decoration's chords at v beside the contact spokes (a chord joining two cycle-nodes that ℓ's edge would separate is a second path and the bridge is gone) — folded in, H3 is a corollary of H2*: SOME σ GIVES EVERY FOUR-FOLD LINK AN END AT WHICH ITS EDGE IS A BRIDGE OF THE CYCLE GRAPH WITH THE CHORDS, and any term with J_ℓ ≥ 1 dies at ℓ's bridge end; the J = 0 spokes' edges are deleted for Schur, not ignored (cutting the J edge with any spin-0 edges leaves an invariant of V_J ⊗ V_0^{⊗k} = V_J, zero); a loop is never a bridge, so the end is a natural one; the 1 + 3 links have no cycle graph at all (the lone face's two contact links make a path at their corner, the faces there not a union of cycles), which is the same fact as "outside the class". The instrument reads the bare cycle graph; a decoration enters the criterion at the vertex it decorates, which is (w) E (i)'s remaining term stated exactly: a term survives only if at every link with J ≥ 1 every bridge end is spoiled by a chord joining the two sides that link's edge parts.]

**(q) NOTE VIII, ADDENDUM III — THE REFUSAL CARRIES THE THEOREM: A WALL THROUGH A PINCH HAS EVERY SPIN-j TERM ZERO FOR j ≥ 1 (iii-bb's gauge argument, NOTE X ADDENDUM I), AND THE WORDS' DERIVED COMPLEX RESOLVES A PINCH INTO TWO VERTICES, SO THE MODE'S REFUSAL NAMES THE THREE CASES IT CANNOT PART (2026-11-06 05:58, read from `date`; the desktop seat db5968f8; record `STDLIB/build/surface_gas_rank/wordsys4_wall.2026-11-06d.txt`; the gate's run of record `aperanton_wordsys_gate.2026-11-06i`).**

THE THEOREM (iii-bb, NOTE X ADDENDUM I): split the pinch into p_A and p_B; a gauge transformation g at p_A leaves every face trace alone and sends the wall's holonomy, a path from p_A to p_B, to g·h; ∫χ_j(gh) dg = 0 for every j ≥ 1. So a lone wall through a pinch has spin-1 term zero and every higher term zero, exactly — ADDENDUM II's "neither +I(R) nor −I(R)" is the theorem's zero, not a reading's.

WHAT THE WORDS DERIVE. The mode's vertices are the corner classes, and two corners are one class only through an edge's ends; at a pinch the two cycles of faces share no edge end, so the derived complex is the surface WITH THE PINCH RESOLVED — two vertices where the geometry has one (R = "A B baCD dc" derives χ = 2, a sphere, where the pinched sphere has χ = 1). A wall through the pinch is therefore an open path there, no face holding two of its consecutive edges, and it lands in the refusal "the letter lies outside a whole copy of T"; so does a mis-stated T, and so does a T whose tree letters a gauge fixing dropped (the 288 cases of the twelve-term gas and this one are gauge-fixed systems).
