**(y) THE LEAFLESS CLASS READ BY A SECOND READER — THE FOURTH INPUT'S FINNER PRICING: LEMMA Ω2 READ, ITS CONSTANT MET ON A ROAD SHARING NO LINE, LEMMA Ω3 AND THE CENSUS READ, THE TERM CARRIED IN THE CERTIFICATE OF RECORD (2026-11-06 22:11 EST, read from date; the trunk seat; the proof agent M1c's WORK.md §2.3–2.7 of 2026-11-02 in `records/agents/2026-11-02/math/M1c/` the text read; the instrument `feven6_fusion.awk`; record `kp_cert_contact.2026-11-06i.txt`).**

THE OBJECT. Theorem 8.9's fourth input: the polymers with NO Z₂-even subset — the leafless class Ω, closed supports every link of which lies in at least two plaquettes and which contain no sheet (THE PROOF's Step 17: "priced by Finner's inequality at (4.74a₁)^F for a polymer of F plaquettes, none having fewer than twenty-two plaquettes"); THE GROUND's revision 59 says of it "that pricing is not yet read by a second reader". It is read here, line by line, by a reader other than its author.

LEMMA Ω2 (Finner 1992, W = 0), READ AND SIGNED. The statement — probability spaces (Ω_i, μ_i), functions h_q ≥ 0 of the coordinates in S_q, exponents with Σ_{q ∋ i} 1/p_q ≤ 1 at every coordinate, then ∫Π_q h_q ≤ Π_q ‖h_q‖_{p_q} — and its proof by induction on the coordinates (integrate one coordinate, Hölder on it with the exponents of the factors containing it padded by the constant 1, the resulting norms a family on the remaining coordinates satisfying the same hypothesis) are correct as written. COROLLARY Ω2, READ: on a polymer X ∈ Ω every plaquette carries an integer spin (a half-odd assignment's support is Z₂-even, and X has no nonempty even subset), so W(X) = ∫Π_{q ∈ X} f_even(U_q) with f_even = Σ_{j ≥ 1 integer} d_j a_j χ_j; the link variables are the coordinates, h_q the plaquette's |f_even| of its four links, and p_q = 6 is a fractional cover because every link of ℤ⁴ lies in at most six plaquettes (two per transverse direction, three transverse directions); ‖h_q‖₆ = ‖f_even‖₆ by the Haar measure's left invariance in one link of the plaquette. Hence |W(X)| ≤ ‖f_even‖₆^{|X|}. SIGNED.

THE CONSTANT ON A SECOND ROAD. M1c read ‖f_even‖₆ by the cosh expansion of the plaquette weight (E[e^{tx}] = 2I₁(t)/t at t = 0..6β, the even part by sign, the sixth power by the binomial theorem; `feven_norm.awk` at 2⁻⁵¹²): 0.0260085 at β = 0.3650, 0.0260936 at 0.3656. The trunk's road shares no line: E[f_even⁶] = Σ over ordered six-tuples of integer spins of Π d_j a_j times dim Inv(V_{j₁} ⊗ … ⊗ V_{j₆}), the invariant count by FUSION (the multiset of spins carried through the six factors, weighted on the certificate's grid by the Bessel library's upper ends; `feven6_fusion.awk`; its known answer E[f_even²] = Σ d_j²a_j² met): E[f_even⁶] = 3.0952·10⁻¹⁰ at 0.3650, ‖f_even‖₆ = 0.02600849 — the cosh road's 0.0260085 TO THE DIGIT; at the couplings of record 0.0259802 (0.3648) and 0.0260652 (0.3654); the ratio to a₁ 4.7243 at all three (the spin-1 part 3·15^{1/6} = 4.7113, the six-tuples with one spin 2 adding 1.68 per cent — 6·5a₂·243a₁⁵·15 against 10935a₁⁶ by hand — the rest below 10⁻⁴). The instrument's first draft read 0.026308, seven per cent high in the moment, because an awk array key is a string and "k > j2" against a number compares as strings ("10" < "2"), so the fused range past twice-spin 8 was wrong; caught by the hand term above, corrected (kn = k + 0), printed.

LEMMA Ω3 AND COROLLARY Ω4, READ. The first-closed-set construction inside X (add the smallest plaquette of X at the smallest link of multiplicity one; stays in X, stops at a closed link-connected S₁ ⊆ X with no even subset, so S₁ ∈ Ω and |S₁| ≥ n_Ω; at most five choices a step, so at most 5^{n−1} such S₁ of size n; the rest Δ a decoration of S₁ in 16.65's sense, every link of S₁ ∪ Δ outside S₁ of multiplicity ≠ 1; X ↦ (S₁, Δ) injective); then the repaired dressing lemma 16.65-b at plaquette activity w for a CLOSED S₁ — its steps using closedness only ((W2): Σ_{ℓ ∈ U}(6 − m_U(ℓ)) = 6|L(U)| − 4|U| ≤ 8|U| since |L(U)| ≤ 2|U| for a closed U; (W3): the walls of a closed set counted once) — gives Σ_Δ w^{|Δ|} ≤ e^{κ_Ω|S₁|}, κ_Ω = 2w + κ′ by the tree recursion with z₁ = z_surf = z_br = w; so Σ_{X ∋ p₀} w^{|X|} ≤ Σ_{n ≥ n_Ω} 5^{n−1}(we^{κ_Ω})^n = ρ_Ω^{n_Ω}/(5(1 − ρ_Ω)) at ρ_Ω = 5we^{κ_Ω} < 1, and the term the certificate owes is T_Ω = 3ρ_Ω^{n_Ω}/(5(1 − ρ_Ω)) at w = ‖f_even‖₆e^{2α̃}, the 3 the link-to-plaquette factor of §14. SIGNED, with the one dependence named: 16.65-b's validity for a closed set that is not even rests on iii-73's signature of its canonical decomposition and first-ownership steps, which the trunk re-read in §20 above.

THE CENSUS, READ. `omega_census.c` (mode 0: closed sets with independent boundary vectors, the matroid's independence oracle as the prune; a count line per F at which a closed node is found): the records `census_mode0_F20.txt` (2.12·10⁹ nodes, 2.69·10⁶ pruned, 274 s; the rank recomputed from scratch at every closed node, 0 disagreements) and `census_mode0_F21.txt` (6.27·10⁹ nodes, 8.17·10⁶ pruned, 948 s, 0 disagreements) carry NO count line: no member of Ω through F = 21 on this road, and through F = 20 on g48's second road (odd irreducible sets with a rest of complement ≤ 6). So n_Ω ≥ 22, as THE PROOF states; F = 22 (g48's (8, 24) family, 352 sets) was not run by M1c and is not run here.

THE TERM IN THE CERTIFICATE OF RECORD. M1c's omega block (‖f_even‖₆ by the cosh expansion inside the engine at 2⁻¹²⁸, κ_Ω by the tree recursion's upper bracket, T_Ω added to the upper end) is carried into `kp_cert_contact.2026-11-06.awk` line for line as OMEGA = 1 at NOM = 22, and record h re-run as record i: page 0.3648 PASS (0.0000261) / 0.3650 FAIL (-0.0000577), tree 0.3654 PASS (0.0000432) / 0.3656 FAIL (-0.0000403), T_Ω at the passing couplings 0.0000000000000000 (page; ‖f_even‖₆ upper 0.0259801562 inside the engine) and 0.0000000000000000 (tree; 0.0260652176), the verdicts of record h unmoved; at F₀ = 20 page 0.3586 PASS (0.0000750) / 0.3588 FAIL (-0.0000097), tree 0.3592 PASS (0.0000666) / 0.3594 FAIL (-0.0000177) (dc91ee4ea). The fourth input's pricing is read by a second reader, its constant met on a second road, and its term inside the number: THE PROOF's Step 17 and THE GROUND's revision 60 say so.

D. WHAT STANDS, WHAT IS OWED, THE WAY BACK. The third input's contact rests are PROVED for one-link leaves and for every leaf without a double-contact vertex, any decoration, any half-odd assignment, and for the census whole; what remains of them is one thing, exactly named: THE BETWEEN-SHEET DECORATIONS AT THE DOUBLE-CONTACT VERTICES OF THE TAIL'S LEAVES. The numbers 0.3650 and 0.3656 stand on that condition; under the charge the condition's own bound allows, the certificate reads 0.3548 and 0.3560, and THE PROOF states both. THE WAY BACK, three roads, none taken yet: (i) the count — a double-contact vertex is a crease, two layers of the sheet sharing two links at a vertex, a crease of n links carrying n − 1 of them on two layers of at least n plaquettes each, so N_dc ≤ F/2 and nothing better is proved; what is needed is the weight of creased leaves under the tail's own count 5^{F−2}, or a lemma lowering a leaf's all-spin bound by a factor beating (1 + W/3)^{N_dc} — the tail's K₁^F is already a factor of 2^{b₁(G)+N₆}'s kind and a crease may be paying twice; (ii) the weight — the plaquettes framed by X at a double-contact vertex joining its two cycles, at most nine, read on the census' 407 by contact_census.c as the first number, and the pieces through them; (iii) the signs — in the bare leaf the (1,1) channel beside the (0,0) sums to the sheet's value (S6), and the 4/3 is paid at the triangle inequality: a signed treatment of a double-contact chain would retire the charge. And one fact for the whole program, read tonight for the first time: A SHARPER TAIL IS WORTH MORE THAN EVERYTHING ENUMERATED — Corollary U's K₁^F, K₁ = √(2(1 + d₁)), carries 99 per cent of the left side at the certified coupling.

**(q) NOTE IX, ADDENDUM I — THE ONE-SIDED SIDE ON PLAQUETTES: THE LEAST CUBICAL MÖBIUS BAND IS 7 PLAQUETTES IN Z³ AND 6 IN Z⁴ (EXHAUSTIVE), A 28-PLAQUETTE KLEIN BOTTLE AND A 30-PLAQUETTE PROJECTIVE PLANE OF Z⁴ BUILT FROM IT, THEIR TWELVE WALLS READ — FIVE ONE-SIDED, EVERY ONE AT −I(R), THE SPIN-2 TERM 0, S7 MET AND VI-v's RULE AGREEING ON ALL TWELVE, AND THE ANCHOR'S SIGN EXERCISED AT S(RP²) = −2 (2026-11-06 07:11, read from `date`; the desktop seat db5968f8; `mobius.c`, `disc.c`, records `mobius.2026-11-06a.txt`, `s7klein.2026-11-06a.txt`, `s7rp2.2026-11-06a.txt` in `STDLIB/build/surface_gas_rank/`; the gate's arm 15, run of record `aperanton_wordsys_gate.2026-11-06j`).**

THE DESK FACT THAT MADE IT A SEARCH. A band of unit squares whose consecutive squares share OPPOSITE edges has all its shared edges parallel — opposite edges of a square are parallel — so it is a cycle times a unit interval in that direction: an annulus, never a Möbius band. A cubical Möbius band must turn, some square sharing adjacent edges with its neighbours, and NOTE IX's census through twenty plaquettes had found no non-orientable closed surface; so the band was searched for directly. `mobius.c` runs Redelmeier's untried-set recursion over the link-connected plaquette sets through a planted plaquette with every link in at most two plaquettes (each set once; the window holding every set of the sizes read), and reads each for being a surface (every vertex star one component), for orientability (a sign walk) and for a boundary that is one cycle. EXHAUSTIVE: in Z³ no Möbius band below seven plaquettes and 504 of seven through the planted plaquette (3,385,942 sets of seven, 3,254,818 surfaces); in Z⁴ none below six and 384 of six (4,388,092 sets, 4,315,996 surfaces). Two drafts corrected by their own counts: the first applied the free-polyomino rule that the root be the lowest cell and read 8 neighbours of a plaquette where Z³ has 12; the second reported the first band met instead of the least.

THE TWO SURFACES. **(q) NOTE X, ADDENDUM III — THE VERTEX LAW AS A THEOREM ON EVERY CLOSED SURFACE WITH AT MOST FOUR SHARED SITES: THE LOCALITY LEMMA, THE EXHAUSTION OF THE LOCAL CONFIGURATIONS BY TWO ROADS, THE SIGN AS THE SIDEDNESS, AND THE GENERAL-s ROAD (2026-11-06 20:47 EST, the desktop seat 26d8e019 / iii-bb; cp_twoconf.c, cp_twoconf_read.sh, cp_haar.c, cp_twoconf_haar.sh, record cp_twoconf.2026-11-06a.txt; aperanton_wallpairs_gate.sh arms 12–13, 15 teeth, run of record aperanton_wallpairs_gate.2026-11-06g.run; landed cf0f0becb).** ADDENDUM I left the sidedness factorisation UNPROVED on non-orientable rests and ADDENDUM II read it on 455,808 pairs of the gas. This addendum proves it — for every closed surface, orientable or not, whose two wall cycles share at most four manifold sites (two integrators sharing no line, meeting on every configuration to three sites and on a 1-in-1000 sample at four, the moment integrator reading the fourth site whole) — and names what stands open above four. THE LOCALITY LEMMA. Let S be a closed surface (any genus, any orientability) and C1, C2 two simple closed cycles on it, link-disjoint, meeting at s sites each a manifold point of S at which the four spokes (C1 in, C1 out, C2 in, C2 out) have a cyclic order. The sites cut C1 and C2 into 2s arcs. Cut S along C1 ∪ C2: each side of an arc is ONE boundary piece of the cut surface — it leaves the corner counterclockwise (left side) or clockwise (right side) of the arc's outgoing spoke at its start site and arrives at the arc's end site at the corner clockwise of the arriving spoke if the local orientations at the two ends agree along the arc, counterclockwise if not: one SIDEDNESS BIT per arc. Each corner is the end of exactly two pieces, so the pieces form a 2-regular graph on the 4s corners whose cycles are the boundary curves, and each curve's word in the arc holonomies is read off the pieces it traverses. Hence the cut word system of the pair — the multiset of boundary words with the walls' words — is a function of the cyclic orders at the s sites, the order in which C2 visits them, and the 2s bits; the components' own topology (handles, cross-caps) enters only the per-component constants (2^χ, the Frobenius–Schur signs), which cancel in r₁, r₂, r₁₂ (ADDENDUM II, the cut road, exact on all 5,568 four-face pairs). Conversely every configuration is realised by a closed surface: the arcs as ribbons with the prescribed twists glued to discs at the sites in the prescribed cyclic orders, every boundary curve capped by a disc. So for fixed s the law is a finite statement: 6^s cyclic orders × 2^(2s) bits × (s−1)! visiting orders. At s = 0 no enumeration is needed: every boundary word is a power of one chord (z for a two-sided cycle, twice; z² for a one-sided one), the integrand is f(z₁)g(z₂) and r₁₂ = r₁r₂ on every closed surface by Fubini. THE INSTRUMENT. cp_twoconf.c enumerates every configuration at s sites, gauge-fixes the tree a₁…a_{s−1} of C1 to the identity (the chords a_s = a, b₁…b_s = b, c, …; any spanning tree leaves one arc trivial and one chord on each other arc), traces the curves and writes each configuration's system in the reader's form; cp_twoconf_read.sh hands them to cp_wallpairs_read.sh (wordsys4 word of, exact rationals) and demands q = r₁₂/(r₁r₂) = (−1/3)^crossings EXACTLY of every configuration, orientable or not, printing any that parts. Six known answers in the engine: the torus square (one crossing, two-sided loops: the one curve abAB), the projective plane's two lines (one crossing, one-sided loops: two two-letter curves), the sphere's tangent circles (one touching: a, b and ab), two of six cyclic orders cross, the counts 24 and 576. THE READING. s = 0: 4 configurations, HELD 4. s = 1: 24 (6 orientable, 18 not), HELD 24 — q = 1 on the 16 touchings, −1/3 on the 8 crossings. s = 2: 576 (72 orientable), HELD 576 — q = 1 / −1/3 / 1/9 at 0 / 1 / 2 crossings (256 / 256 / 64). s = 3: 27,648 (1,728 orientable), HELD 27,648 — q = 1 / −1/3 / 1/9 / −1/27 at 0 / 1 / 2 / 3 crossings (8,192 / 12,288 / 6,144 / 1,024); 25,856 distinct systems, ten minutes. PARTED 0 everywhere, no wall term zero, and in EVERY configuration r₁, r₂ ∈ {+1, −1} — each wall's own term is the sidedness sign of its cycle (S7), so the pair term is r₁₂ = s₁s₂(−1/3)^crossings: THE SIGN IS THE SIDEDNESS AND THE MAGNITUDE IS THE CROSSING COUNT, on every surface. THE SECOND ROAD. cp_haar.c shares no line with wordsys4: it writes each letter g = [[α, β], [−β̄, ᾱ]] and g⁻¹ entry by entry, expands every trace into its 2^L index terms, and integrates each monomial by the moments of the Haar measure on S³, ∫ α^p ᾱ^q β^r β̄^s = [p = q][r = s] p! r!/(p + r + 1)!, every letter's moment tensor exact in sixths, the sum over the index assignments taken depth-first in an order that completes one letter at a time and dies the moment a completed letter's moment is zero (its own plain sum the second road inside, both demanded of eight known answers: wordsys4's three, tr(g)² ↦ 1, tr(g²) ↦ −1, tr(g)⁴ ↦ 2, the torus square ↦ 1/2, an odd power ↦ 0). On every configuration to s = 3 — 28,252 — its r₁, r₂, r₁₂ and q equal the word road's exactly (cp_twoconf_haar.sh, configuration by configuration against the record), twelve seconds for all four s. THE FOURTH SITE. s = 4 is 1,990,656 configurations, and the moment road reads them whole in two minutes on thirty chunks: HELD on all 1,990,656 — q = 1 / −1/3 / 1/9 / −1/27 / 1/81 at 0 / 1 / 2 / 3 / 4 crossings (393,216 / 786,432 / 589,824 / 196,608 / 24,576), PARTED 0, no wall term zero — by the moment road whole; the word road, whose five-letter systems price a 1-in-1000 sample of s = 4 (1,991 configurations, 7,940 systems) at ninety minutes, read that sample and agrees with the moment road on every one of the 1,991 (r₁, r₂, r₁₂, q; 0 differ), so the two roads meet on every system to three sites and on the sample at four. THEOREM (the vertex law at ≤ 4 shared sites): on every closed surface and every pair of link-disjoint wall cycles sharing at most four manifold sites, W(S ∪ w₁ ∪ w₂)/W(S) at spin 1 factorises as (the lone walls' terms) × (−1/3)^crossings, each lone term ±1 by sidedness — the statement ADDENDUM I could not prove, proved by the locality lemma and the exhaustion of 2,018,908 configurations (two roads to three sites; the moment road at four, the word road agreeing on a 1-in-1000 sample there). The trunk's star-road number is pinned across the roads: at s = 2 with two crossings all 64 configurations read 1/9, the 1/9 of its ALMb0b1|LMb0b1 element T₀₀ = 1/144 = (1/16)(1/9). WHAT STANDS OPEN: every s above four. THE GENERAL-s ROAD, at the desk: for each arc the three strands through it are its two sides at spin 1/2 and its wall at spin 1, and the Gaunt integral ∫D^{1/2}(g)_{m₁n₁}D^{1/2}(g)_{m₂n₂}D^{1}(g)_{m₃n₃}dg = (3j)_{m₁m₂m₃}(3j)_{n₁n₂n₃} (1/2 ⊗ 1/2 ⊗ 1 holds the trivial representation once) splits every arc's tensor into one 3j at each end, so the whole integral is a PRODUCT OVER SITES of a local spin network — four 3j's at a site, the tetrahedral net at a crossing (the two wall edges the diagonals of the corner 4-cycle), two bubbles at a touching — and q = Π_sites f(site) with f a function of the site's cyclic order and the arrow pattern of its strands. The theorem at every s is then a finite table: f = −1/3 at a crossing and 1 at a touching for every arrow pattern, with the ε-signs of the reversed spin-1/2 strands cancelling in the product (they do on all 28,252 configurations here; whether each f carries no sign of its own, or signs that cancel only globally, is what the table decides). The instrument is cp_sitefactor: the local evaluation of the four-3j network per site type and arrow pattern, exact in rationals (each 3j of (1/2, 1/2, 1) is ±1/√3 or ±1/√6 and a site holds four of them, so a site's value is rational), its table checked against this addendum's readings at s = 1, 2, 3, 4 as its second road; the moment integrator built here is its engine.
